# Changelog

Tracks what's been built and what's next, phase by phase per `docs/spine-prd.md` §12. Update this file as work lands — newest entry on top within each section. This is the progress log; `CLAUDE.md` is the architecture/rules reference.

## Status

**Phase 4 · Money Out & Books is now fully complete** (Tax → Purchases & Expenses → Reports) — this closes the entire v1 core-product scope. See the Phase 4 Stage 3 Completed entry below for the Reports/FEAT-09 breakdown. Only Phase 5 (Mobile, `apps/mobile`) remains per the PRD's own phase order.

**Current phase:** Phase 0 · Foundation is functionally complete (monorepo scaffold, Docker Compose shape, the money/tax core in `@spine/shared`, the full Postgres/Drizzle schema with a working DB-enforced ledger, a documented local dev workflow, a project README, auth (Better Auth: email/password + organizations + JWT), the design system (`docs/DESIGN.md` + `apps/design-system`), the full PRD §F0 `@spine/ui` set plus the complete Radix Primitives + shadcn/ui component union, a hand-rolled Outlook-style `Calendar` component, full `apps/design-system` documentation coverage, the first-run onboarding wizard, a real Settings UI, a shared `AppShell`, a real S3/MinIO-backed Files module, a GitHub Actions CI pipeline, and a formal WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit-and-fix pass across all 69 `@spine/ui` components).

**Phase 1 · Sales Spine is fully complete**, per the PRD's own story list (`docs/spine-prd.md` §12: S-01.1→S-01.8, S-02.1→S-02.4, S-03.1→S-03.9 + S-03.11, S-10.4, S-10.11, S-10.12, S-12.9): Contacts & CRM (F1, all 8 stories including CSV import, the leads pipeline kanban board, and the per-contact Timeline tab), Catalogue (F2), and Quotes (F3, including the public accept/reject link and owner notifications) are all complete; the in-app notification centre and browser push notifications are both built; and the dashboard setup checklist is wired to real completion state. Only **S-03.12 (mobile)** remains unbuilt among F3's stories — deferred until `apps/mobile` exists (Phase 5). **Post-Phase-1 addition:** S-01.9 (meeting scheduling on a calendar) was added to FEAT-01 and built after Phase 1 closed.

**Phase 2 · Money In is now fully complete.** Invoicing (draft CRUD, issue/void with real §7.2 ledger postings including S-02.3's per-catalog-item revenue split, PDF, email, overdue flagging, one-click reminders, advance/deposit invoices) and Payments (record, multi-invoice allocation with DB-enforced limits, oldest-first suggestion, unallocated customer credit, apply sitting credit to a later invoice, receipt PDF/email) are both fully built with real web UIs (`/invoices`, `/payments`, `/receivables`). Credit notes (S-05.6) and quote→invoice conversion (S-03.10) are both built too. Demo data (S-12.8/F12) covers Invoices + Payments with real seeded totals and postings. Every FEAT-05/FEAT-06/FEAT-09 story in Phase 2's own scope is now built.

**Phase 3 · Jobs & Construction Billing is now fully complete**, built in 5 staged commits per explicit user instruction (no scope deferred). Stage 1: `jobs`/`milestones`/`job_photos`/`job_notes`/`job_variation_orders` schema, real FKs wired from `invoices.jobId`/`invoices.milestoneId`/`journal_lines.jobId` (both existed as placeholder columns since Phase 2/earlier, now pointing at real tables), `JobsService`/`MilestonesService`/`JobsController` covering S-04.1 (create a job from an accepted quote, contract value carried over as a snapshot) and S-04.2/S-04.3 (define milestones as fixed/percent amounts with server-side "percentages must total 100%" validation before any write lands). Stage 2: S-04.4 (mark a milestone complete with photos, field-reachable via `jobs.update_milestones`), S-04.5/S-05.1's milestone path (raise a progress invoice directly from a completed milestone via `InvoicesService.createFromMilestone`), S-04.6 (retention held automatically — `issue()` now splits the AR debit into net-AR + `Retention Receivable` per §7.2 whenever `retentionAmount > 0`), S-04.7 (release retention as a new `retention_release`-type invoice via `InvoicesService.releaseRetention`). Every balance computation across Invoices/Payments/Reports (`balanceDue`, `suggestAllocation`, allocation-limit checks, AR aging) now subtracts `retentionAmount` alongside `amountPaid`/`creditedAmount`, so held-back retention never shows as a collectible or overdue receivable. Stage 3: S-04.9 (variation orders — a new `VariationOrdersService` wraps ordinary `QuotesService` CRUD/send/accept, linking each supplementary quote to a job via `job_variation_orders`; an explicit `apply()` action folds an accepted variation's total into the job's `contractValueAmount` and recomputes every percent-type milestone's `resolvedAmount` against the new base), S-04.10 (general job photos/notes, web upload, field-reachable), S-04.8 revenue side (new `JobCostingService` reads `journal_lines` filtered by `jobId` — never a shadow costing table — summing `income`-account credits as revenue and `expense`-account debits as cost; cost is always 0 today since Purchases/Expenses don't exist until Phase 4, but the query needs no rework once they do). Stage 4 (web UI): `/jobs` list page and `/jobs/[id]` detail page (header, tabbed Milestones/Variation Orders/Photos/Notes/Costing sections, edit dialog, release-retention action), a "Convert to job" action on an accepted quote's detail page (completes S-03.10 alongside Phase 2's invoice-conversion half), and its own top-level "Jobs" sidebar section between Sales and Billing. S-12.3's construction-template effect is presentational only: retention % lives behind a collapsible "Advanced" disclosure in the job edit dialog, defaulting open for orgs on the Construction industry template or any job that already has a nonzero `retentionPct` — Jobs itself stays fully usable for every org regardless of template, per CLAUDE.md's onboarding-is-never-a-gate rule. **Stage 5 (final)**: demo data now seeds 3 jobs at different S-04.1→S-04.7 lifecycle stages (one just started, one with 2 of 3 milestones invoiced and retention accumulating, one fully complete with retention released) via the real `JobsService`/`MilestonesService`/`InvoicesService` methods, exactly like every other demo document type in this codebase; `DemoDataService.deleteAll` now also removes demo jobs (skipping any blocked by `journal_lines.jobId`'s own immutability, matching the existing demo-contact precedent) and its own `jobsDeleted` count reports the real number removed, not the total found; `docs/qa-script.md` gained a full §5 Jobs section plus retention regression checks on the Invoices/Payments/Receivables sections it touches. Verified end-to-end against a live local instance (seed → milestone completion → progress invoicing → retention hold → retention release → deleteAll), not just by reading the code.

**The permission model (§2.1) has been substantially rewritten and built** (S-10.13→S-10.18, S-10.17), cutting across every phase rather than belonging to one: multiple Owners with one DB-protected Primary Owner, Teams subdividing the Staff tier with a configurable capability-based privilege matrix enforced by a `CapabilityGuard` layered on `@OrgRoles`, an Owner-only `/team/*` web area, a real invitation-email/accept flow, and a Profile & Preferences page for every role. See the 2026-08-09/2026-08-10 Completed entries for the full breakdown.

**New: a repeat-quote-conversion safeguard (user-reported, post-Phase-3).** Neither `InvoicesService.convertFromQuote` nor `JobsService.createFromQuote` has ever had a one-shot restriction — a deliberate second conversion is a real use case (staged billing, splitting one quote across two jobs) — but with no total anywhere cross-checking a job's/invoice's amount against the source quote, an ACCIDENTAL repeat click silently doubles the billed/tracked amount. New org preference `quote_conversion_guard` (Settings → "Repeat quote conversion", default **warn**) — `off` (no check), `warn` (a 409 with the existing job(s)/invoice(s) listed, requiring the caller to retry with `confirm: true`), or `block` (rejected outright, no override). One shared `QuoteConversionGuardService` (`apps/api/src/modules/org`) enforces this identically for both conversion paths, checked BEFORE either service does its own work. The web UI's `apiClient` gained an `ApiError` class (extends `Error`, backward-compatible with every existing `catch` site) so the quote detail page can read the 409's structured body and show a real confirmation dialog naming what already exists, rather than a generic error toast. Verified end-to-end against a live instance: off/warn/block all behave correctly, and the guard is quote-scoped across BOTH job and invoice conversions together (converting to a job when 3 invoices already exist correctly warns about all of them).

**Phase 4 · Money Out & Books — Tax (FEAT-08) stage is complete**, first of three planned stages (Tax → Purchases & Expenses → Reports, per the user's own explicit sequencing). Real `tax_rates`/`tax_groups`/`tax_group_rates` tables (S-08.1, S-08.3) replace the earlier Phase 0/1 single-flat-percentage placeholder that only backed onboarding's quick-start step (`GET/PUT /org/tax/default-rate`, kept unchanged for that one purpose). `TaxResolverService` centralizes "resolve a line's `taxRateId` XOR `taxGroupId` into `computeLineTax`'s input shape" and is now injected into Quotes/Invoices/Catalog — every quote/invoice line and catalog item's default tax gained `taxRateId`/`taxGroupId` FK columns (snapshot-on-write, never re-read after) alongside the legacy `taxRatePercentage` (now a computed blended fallback, not user-editable when a real rate/group is set). `isTaxInclusive` (S-08.2) is a new per-line snapshot column, backed by an org-level `prices_include_tax` default setting; `computeLineTax`'s existing `inclusive` parameter (previously always hardcoded `false`) is finally wired through end-to-end. A new `ReportsService.taxSummary` (S-08.4, Owner-only, `GET /org/tax/summary?from&to`) sums `journal_lines` against the two system tax accounts the chart of accounts already seeds (`2100 Output Tax Payable`, `1200 Input Tax Receivable`) — no shadow ledger, matching CLAUDE.md's "derive from journal lines" rule exactly; `taxPaid` is always 0 until Purchases (this phase's next stage) posts to `1200`, needing no rework once it does, same forward-compatible shape `JobCostingService` already established for its own cost side. Web: a fully rebuilt Settings → Tax pane (rate/group CRUD with archive/restore/delete-if-unreferenced, the inclusive-pricing toggle) replaces the old single-rate quick-start form; Quotes' and Invoices' line editors both gained a named-rate/group `Combobox` picker (prefilling from a catalog item's own default) plus a "Price includes tax" checkbox, replacing the old raw percentage `Input`; Catalogue's item form gained the same picker. Demo data now seeds one real "Sales Tax" `tax_rates` row via the real `TaxRatesService` (not hand-inserted) and wires every demo catalog item/quote/invoice line to reference it; `DemoDataService.deleteAll` cleans it up by name once nothing references it. Verified end-to-end against a live local instance: created two rates (one compound) and a group combining them via curl, confirmed `computeLineTax`'s compound math on a real quote/invoice line matched hand-calculated numbers exactly ($100 line, GST 15% + compound State Tax 6.5% → $22.48 tax, not the wrong non-compound $21.50), issued the invoice and confirmed `taxSummary` reported the exact same $22.48 as tax collected with $0 tax paid, confirmed archive/restore and the delete-blocked-when-referenced-by-a-group guard, and confirmed seed → deleteAll leaves manually-created tax rates untouched while removing only the demo-seeded one. `docs/qa-script.md` gained a full §10 Tax section (renumbering old §10-12 to §11-13) with regression checks on Catalogue/Quotes/Invoices totals. The job **variation order** line editor (a third, independently-owned quote-line editor per its own doc comment) was deliberately left on the legacy flat-percentage path — still fully correct, just not retrofitted with the new picker, since S-08.5 doesn't name it and Phase 4's own story list doesn't require it.

**Tax summary gained a per-rate breakdown + a real web surface (user-requested follow-up to the Tax stage above).** `ReportsService.taxSummary` now also returns `collectedByRate` — tax collected grouped by named rate/group (or "X% tax" for a pre-FEAT-08 flat-percentage line), largest first, exactly what a real VAT/GST return needs ("how much at 20% vs. at 5%") that a single blended `taxCollected` figure can't show. Deliberately reads `invoice_lines` (which carries the per-line `taxRateId`/`taxGroupId`/`taxRatePercentage`) rather than `journal_lines` (which only has one blended ledger posting per document) — the two sources can legitimately diverge when an invoice's ledger history outlives its own document row (e.g. `DemoDataService.deleteAll` removes the invoice but never touches its already-posted, permanent journal entry, per CLAUDE.md's immutability rule), discovered live when a stale local dev org's ledger total ($2,406.73) didn't match its current invoices' combined tax ($22.48) after an earlier demo-data cycle. Rather than silently under-totaling, the breakdown now always sums exactly to `taxCollected` by appending a trailing "Other / from removed invoices" row for the unattributed remainder — both figures stay independently correct and the mismatch is explained, not hidden. Web: a new "Tax summary" section at the bottom of Settings → Tax (From/To `DatePicker`s, Refresh button, Collected/Paid/Net figures, the per-rate list) — renders unconditionally like every other pane (no client-side capability check, matching this codebase's documented gap) and surfaces the API's real Owner-only 403 as a legible message rather than a silent failure. Verified end-to-end against the live local instance described above: the breakdown's one real rate row ($22.48) plus its "Other" row ($2,384.25) sum to exactly the ledger's $2,406.73.

**Phase 4 Stage 2 (Purchases & Expenses, FEAT-07) is complete** — vendor bills, expenses with an approval-gated posting trigger, vendor payments allocated across bills, and a payables aging view, closing out the "Money Out" half of Phase 4 · Money Out & Books. New tables `vendor_bills`/`vendor_bill_lines` (structurally mirroring `invoices`/`invoice_lines` exactly — snapshot-on-write lines, draft-is-mutable/recorded-is-immutable, the same FEAT-08 `taxRateId`/`taxGroupId`/`isTaxInclusive` shape, one deliberate difference: `billNumber` is plain vendor-supplied text, never allocated via `NumberingService`, per that module's own doc comment), `expense_categories`/`expenses` (S-07.2/S-07.3's web-submission half, S-07.4's approval gate — the PRD's own acceptance highlight, "an unapproved expense does not post to the ledger, approval is the posting trigger," is enforced by `ExpensesService.approve` flipping `status -> approved` AND posting the §7.2 "Expense paid directly" journal entry in the SAME transaction, so there is no reachable approved-but-unposted state), and `vendor_payments`/`vendor_payment_allocations` (an exact mirror of `payments`/`payment_allocations` including a new hand-written migration, `0029_vendor_payment_allocation_limits.sql`, replicating `0020`'s deferred-constraint-trigger allocation-limit enforcement for the vendor side — flagged as the intended path in Phase 2's own CHANGELOG note, now built; unlike the customer side, vendor payment allocations must sum to EXACTLY the payment amount, since FEAT-07 has no "vendor credit" concept). `VendorBillsService.record()` and `ExpensesService.approve()` both post through the shared `LedgerService` exactly like `InvoicesService.issue()` (row-lock, status re-check, numbering, `postEntry`, audit log, all one transaction) — §7.2's "Vendor bill recorded" (debit Expense/COGS + Input Tax Receivable, credit Accounts Payable) and "Expense paid directly" (debit Expense, credit Bank/Cash) postings are both new. S-04.8 (job costing's cost side) is now genuinely complete — `JobCostingService`'s query already read `journal_lines.jobId` for `expense`-type debits since Phase 3, and needed zero changes once this stage started posting them for real. A new `purchases.manage` capability (Owner/Staff, Team-configurable) gates vendor bill/vendor payment mutation; `expenses.submit`/`expenses.approve` (already existing since an earlier phase) cover the expense side. **Real bug found and fixed while wiring Field access to `expenses.submit`**: `CapabilityGuard` unconditionally rejected any non-owner/non-staff role, including `field`, even on routes that explicitly listed `"field"` in their own `@OrgRoles` — meaning `jobs.controller.ts`'s `completeMilestone` route (S-04.4, `jobs.update_milestones`) has been silently unreachable by Field users since Phase 3 despite its own doc comment claiming otherwise. Fixed: `CapabilityGuard` now passes unconditionally for `field` (trusting that Better Auth's `AuthGuard` already evaluated `@OrgRoles` and would have 403'd first if `field` weren't allowed on that specific route) — verified via the existing `capability.guard.integration.test.ts` suite (updated its "Field is always rejected" test to "Field always passes", all 38 integration tests green) and confirms both the new expense-submission path AND the pre-existing milestone-completion path now work correctly for Field. Web: full `/vendor-bills` (list/detail/builder with a vendor-only contact picker and a job/tax/account line-level picker), `/expenses` (list/detail-drawer with Approve/Reject — reject requires a comment, mirroring void-invoice's required-reason pattern), `/vendor-payments` (list/detail-drawer, a "New vendor payment" dialog that disables submit until allocations sum to exactly the amount), and `/payables` (aging buckets, mirrors `/receivables` exactly) — plus a new "Purchases" top-level nav section (money-out mirror of "Billing", ordered directly after it) and a Settings → Purchases pane for expense-category CRUD (mirrors the Tax pane's own rate/group CRUD-list pattern). Demo data seeds 4 vendor bills across every lifecycle outcome (draft/recorded/paid/voided) and 5 expenses across the full approval-gate lifecycle (submitted/approved/rejected), via the real services exactly like every other demo document type; `DemoDataService.deleteAll` cleans up vendor payments before vendor bills (allocation FK ordering, same precedent `payment_allocations` already established) and cleans up the three demo expense categories by name (same "no isDemo column, clean up by name" convention already used for the demo "Sales Tax" rate). Verified end-to-end against a live local instance via curl: submitted and approved an expense (confirmed the exact debit/credit ledger posting), rejected a second one (confirmed zero ledger impact), recorded a vendor bill with compound tax (confirmed the three-line Expense+Input-Tax/Accounts-Payable posting balanced exactly), paid it off via a vendor payment (confirmed `VPY-0001` allocation, bill balance dropped to zero, excluded from Payables), tested the allocation-limit rejection and the DB trigger backing it, voided a bill with a reversing entry, and ran the full demo seed → deleteAll cycle confirming exact counts both directions. `docs/qa-script.md` gained a full §11 Purchases & Expenses section (renumbering old §11-13 to §12-14) with regression checks on Jobs' Costing tab and the Tax summary's now-real `taxPaid` figure. One story, S-07.6 (bill a customer for a billable expense), has a real working API (`POST /expenses/:id/mark-billed`) but no web UI action yet — flagged as a known gap, not silently left unmentioned.

**Phase 4 Stage 3 (Reports, FEAT-09) is complete — this closes out Phase 4 · Money Out & Books and the entire v1 core-product scope before Phase 5 (Mobile).** All eight stories built in one pass: P&L for any date range (S-09.2, `ReportsService.profitAndLoss`, sums `journal_lines` grouped by income/expense account — never computed from documents directly, per FEAT-09's own acceptance highlight); Balance Sheet as-of a date (S-09.3, `balanceSheet`, assets/liabilities/equity from lifetime account balances, with current-period net income folded in as a computed "Retained Earnings (current period)" equity line rather than posted to the seeded `3200` system account, since no formal period-close posting exists yet — the statement still balances exactly, verified live: $4,306,525 assets = $448,725 liabilities + $3,857,800 equity); click-through drill-down behind any P&L/Balance Sheet figure (S-09.4, `accountDrilldown` — given an account + date range, returns every journal entry with its `sourceType`/`sourceId`, which the web client resolves into a real link via a small `SOURCE_TYPE_ROUTES` map); all-jobs profitability summary (S-09.6, `jobProfitability` — one query grouped by `jobId`, the all-jobs counterpart to Phase 3's existing per-job `JobCostingService.report`, same "derive from journal_lines, never a shadow costing table" rule); CSV export on every new report plus receivables/payables/job-profitability (S-09.7, a tiny shared `toCsv` RFC-4180 writer in `apps/api/src/common/csv.ts`, one `/export.csv` route per report with its own explicit column mapping rather than a generic dispatcher, since each report's row shape genuinely differs); period close (S-09.8, new `PeriodsService`/`PeriodsController` under the `ledger` module managing `fiscal_periods` rows — CRUD + close/reopen — with the actual enforcement living in `LedgerService.postEntry` itself, which now rejects any posting whose date falls inside a closed period, checked inside the same transaction as the posting via `tx` so a concurrent close can't race the write); a custom chart-of-accounts entry (S-09.9, `AccountsService.create` — always `isSystem: false`, rejects a duplicate `code`); and a real dashboard (S-09.10, `ReportsService.dashboardSummary` — cash position from the `1000`/`1010` seeded cash/bank accounts' lifetime net movement, total receivables reusing `receivables()` directly rather than a second balance computation, overdue invoice count, and month-to-date revenue from `income`-account journal_lines credits).

`dashboard.view` (existing capability, whose own description already said "P&L/balance sheet stay Owner-only regardless of this grant") now literally enforces that: `ReportsController`'s new P&L/Balance-Sheet/drilldown routes carry `@OrgRoles(["owner"])` with no capability escape hatch, while dashboard-summary/job-profitability/CSV-export-of-those stay `@RequireCapability("dashboard.view")` like receivables/payables already did — no new capability needed, matching CLAUDE.md's "single source of truth" rule since nothing new needed gating. Web: a new top-level "Reports" nav section (`/reports`, three tabs — Profit & Loss, Balance Sheet, Job Profitability — each independently loaded so a Staff user with `dashboard.view` but not Owner sees Job Profitability load fine while P&L/Balance Sheet show the server's real 403 inline, matching every other pane's "no client-side capability check" convention); period close and "Add account" are folded into the existing Settings → Chart of accounts pane rather than given their own tabs; the dashboard page gained a real 4-card KPI row (`DashboardSummary`) replacing the placeholder note that used to explain why no stat cards existed yet.

**Real bug found and fixed while first live-testing period close**: initial testing tried to close a period covering a *document's own* `issueDate`/`date` field and close-then-issue appeared to silently do nothing — turned out every domain service posts with `date: new Date()` (issue/record/approve time), not the document's own user-editable date field, so backdating a document's `issueDate` was never actually going to produce a backdated ledger posting in this codebase to begin with. The guard is correct as built (confirmed live: closing a period covering *today* correctly rejects a same-day invoice issue with "falls within a closed fiscal period," and reopening immediately un-blocks it) — `LedgerService.postEntry`'s own doc comment now calls this out explicitly so a future reader doesn't rediscover the same confusion.

**Demo data**: the "active" demo job ("Marina office fit-out") now has one vendor bill and one expense tagged to it (`seedJobs` returns `costingJobId`, threaded into `seedPurchases`) — without this, every demo job showed `cost: 0` and a misleading flat 100% margin on the new Job Profitability report, even though the underlying posting already carried `jobId` correctly whenever a real bill/expense had one set. No new tables landed this stage (`fiscal_periods` already existed in schema since Phase 0, `accounts.create` reuses the existing table), so `apps/api/src/db/reset.ts`'s `TABLES` list needed no changes — re-verified exhaustively against the schema (zero missing, zero extra) as a matter of course.

Verified end-to-end against a live local instance: seeded demo data, confirmed P&L totals (net profit $3,857,800), confirmed the Balance Sheet balances exactly, drilled into the AR account and confirmed every listed journal entry linked to its correct real invoice/payment, confirmed Job Profitability showed the job-tagged demo job's real nonzero cost while every other demo job correctly showed cost 0, downloaded and inspected a P&L CSV export, created and closed a fiscal period and confirmed a same-day invoice issue was correctly rejected then correctly un-blocked on reopen, and created a custom account via the new Settings UI. `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`/`test:integration` (38 tests, unchanged) and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`build` all pass. `docs/qa-script.md` gained a full §15 Reports & Dashboard section and closed out its own stale "No P&L/Balance Sheet report exists yet" known-gap note.

**`AlertDialog` vs. `Dialog` audit (user-reported).** Per `AlertDialogContent`'s own doc comment (`packages/ui/src/components/alert-dialog.tsx`), `AlertDialog` is specifically for a pure yes/no confirmation before an action that can't be undone; `Dialog` is for everything else (forms, pickers, viewers). Two new confirmations built this session (release-retention on a job, the repeat-quote-conversion warning above) were built with plain `Dialog` and are now `AlertDialog`. Audited every other `Dialog`/`AlertDialog` usage across `apps/web` — every remaining plain `Dialog` genuinely contains a form control (void-invoice's required reason field, transfer-primary-ownership's owner picker, add/edit forms) so those are correctly left as-is; everything that's a pure confirmation already used `AlertDialog` (contacts, quotes, catalogue, teams, demo-data deletion) with one exception: **deleting a meeting from the calendar had no confirmation at all** — `Calendar`'s (`@spine/ui`) `onEventDelete` fired the API call immediately with zero prompt. `MeetingsCalendar` now intercepts it with its own `AlertDialog` before calling the API, rather than teaching the shared `Calendar` component a confirmation flow every consumer might want shaped differently.

## Next up
- **Phase 4 · Money Out & Books is now fully complete (Tax → Purchases & Expenses → Reports, all three stages) — this closes the entire v1 core-product scope.** Phase 5 (Mobile, `apps/mobile`) is the only phase left per the PRD's own phase breakdown (`docs/spine-prd.md` §12). Before starting Phase 5, worth a full regression pass across Phases 0–4 given how much has landed, and picking up the smaller loose ends below.
- **S-07.6 (bill a customer for a billable expense) has a working API (`POST /expenses/:id/mark-billed`) but no web UI action** — flagged in `docs/qa-script.md`'s Known Gaps, not silently missing. Natural home is either the invoice builder's line editor ("+ Add billable expense") or the expense detail drawer ("Bill to customer" → pick an invoice) — worth deciding which before building it, since the two imply different UX (invoice-first vs. expense-first).
- **No formal period-close posting exists** — closing a fiscal period (S-09.8) blocks new journal postings dated inside it, but doesn't post a closing entry rolling net income into `3200 Retained Earnings`; the Balance Sheet instead shows a computed "Retained Earnings (current period)" equity line for the same effect without a real posting. Worth a proper period-close journal entry if multi-period reporting (comparing one closed year's retained earnings against the next) ever becomes a real requirement — not needed for v1's single-org, ongoing-books use case.
- **The Reports page's actual rendered UI (tabs, drill-down dialog, CSV download) was not visually verified in a browser** — no browser-automation tooling exists in this environment (same limitation noted in every prior session touching apps/web). What *was* verified live: every new endpoint via curl against a real seeded org (P&L/Balance Sheet/drilldown/job-profitability/dashboard-summary/CSV export/period-close/custom-account, all with real computed figures cross-checked by hand), that `/reports` and `/dashboard` both server-render successfully with a real authenticated session (200, not the earlier 500 caught by this same check), and that `pnpm --filter @spine/web build` compiles the new routes without error.
- **No client-side fine-grained capability check exists anywhere in apps/web** — every gated page (Quotes, Teams, Jobs) approximates server-side `@RequireCapability` enforcement by checking coarse `role` (owner/staff/field) client-side instead, since `GET /org/teams` (the only place a Staff member's own effective Team grants would be readable) is hard-coded Owner-only. A Staff member whose Team doesn't actually grant a capability still sees the button and gets a real 403 from the server on click — correct end-to-end, just a less polished UI than gating on the real grant would allow. Worth a `GET /org/members/me/capabilities`-style endpoint (or equivalent) if this UX gap is ever worth closing; not attempted here as it's a cross-cutting change well beyond Jobs' own scope.
- **Variation-order acceptance doesn't auto-apply to the job**: it goes through the ordinary public quote accept/reject flow (`PublicQuotesService`), which has no concept of jobs — a staff member must explicitly click "Apply to job" on the job detail page's Variation Orders tab afterward. Deliberate (see VariationOrdersService.apply's own doc comment), not an oversight — no proactive nudge (e.g. a notification) exists yet for "this variation was just accepted."
- **`MilestonesService.invoiceMilestone`** calls `InvoicesService.createFromMilestone` then updates the milestone's own `status`/`invoicedInvoiceId` as two sequential writes, not one shared transaction (each service owns its own transaction boundary, matching every other cross-service call in this codebase) — retry-safety is `IdempotencyInterceptor` at the controller layer, not a manual re-entrancy guard. Revisit only if a real crash-window data integrity issue shows up in practice.
- Smaller open items within Phase 2's own scope (not blocking Phase 3, but still open):
  - **No dedicated "payment instructions" block/payment link** in the invoice PDF/email body — the balance-due summary is the closest thing built so far, since no payment gateway/link exists yet.
  - **No `GET /:id/history` endpoint for invoices** (or credit notes, or payments) — `AuditLogService.record()` is called on every mutation, matching Contacts/Catalogue/Quotes' own instrumentation, but there's no history *view* endpoint yet, consistent with those modules' own "writes now, view later" precedent (see the audit log rollout item below).
  - **No standalone `/credit-notes` list page** — reachable only from the invoice it corrects, same "invoice-triggered, not yet standalone" scope Payments started with before S-06.4 gave it its own page.
- ~~**Vendor payments (S-07.8, Phase 4 scope) will want the same allocation-limit trigger pattern**~~ — **done**, see the Phase 4 Stage 2 Completed entry (`0029_vendor_payment_allocation_limits.sql`).
- ~~**Demo data still doesn't cover Jobs (Phase 3) or vendor bills/expenses (Phase 4)**~~ — **done**, see the 2026-08-10 "Demo data now covers every previously-unseeded entity" Completed entry, plus the Phase 3/Phase 4 Stage 2 entries' own demo-data notes.
- Quotes Stage 3's own known gaps/rough edges, left as-is because nothing in the task brief called for going further:
  - **Repeat-view notifications aren't de-duplicated.** Every successful public view writes a `quote_events` row AND fires an owner-notification email (S-03.7's literal wording — "the moment a quote is viewed" — doesn't say "only the first time"), so a customer who opens the link five times generates five "viewed" emails. This is very likely to feel noisy in real use; a reasonable follow-up is throttling to "at most one 'viewed' notification per hour per quote" or similar, deliberately not built here since the PRD text doesn't call for it and doing so would be inventing scope.
  - **A rejection does not trigger an owner notification** — only "viewed" and "accepted" do, matching S-03.7's own wording exactly ("viewed or accepted"). The quote's status flip to `rejected` is visible on the authenticated detail page, but there's no proactive email for it. Worth reconsidering if real usage shows owners want to know about rejections just as promptly.
  - **Rate limiting (20 req/min/IP, `@nestjs/throttler`, in-memory) is a first pass, not tuned against real traffic.** It's per-IP only — a customer and their whole office behind one NAT'd IP share the same bucket, which could be too tight for legitimate shared use (e.g. several people at a company opening the same link) or too loose against a determined attacker rotating source IPs. In-memory (no Redis, per CLAUDE.md's single-host stance) also means the limit resets on every API process restart, which is a fine tradeoff for a single-host deployment but wouldn't survive a future multi-instance one without swapping storage.
  - **Token TTL is a flat 30 days**, not configurable per-org or tied to the quote's own `validUntil`. A quote valid for only 7 days still gets a 30-day link — not incorrect (the public page/accept/reject flow independently checks `status === "sent"`, so an expired-by-`validUntil` quote can still technically be viewed/accepted via a live token, matching how the authenticated app treats "expired" as informational, not a hard block — see `QuotesService`'s own `isExpired` comment), but worth a second look if the PRD's "expire" semantics are meant to be a hard cutoff on the public side too.
  - **No public "resend my link" self-service** — if a customer loses the email, only staff re-sending (or revising) the quote mints a fresh token. This wasn't asked for and would need its own rate-limiting/abuse consideration (an anonymous "resend to this email" endpoint is a classic enumeration/spam vector) so it's deliberately not built.
  - **The public web page's actual rendered UI was not visually verified** — no browser-automation tooling exists in this environment (same limitation noted in every prior Quotes session). What *was* verified live: the underlying `GET/POST /public/quotes/:token` calls the page's client component makes (directly, with curl, using a token minted the same way the real send flow mints one), that the Next.js route resolves and server-renders without error, and that `pnpm --filter @spine/web build` successfully compiles `/quote/[token]` as a route. The actual on-screen layout, mobile responsiveness, and dark/light theming were reviewed by reading the component's Tailwind classes against `@spine/ui`'s existing conventions, not by looking at a rendered browser.
- Quotes Stage 2's remaining known gaps (still open, unrelated to Stage 3): no dead-letter queue configured (a permanently-failed job just sits in pg-boss's `failed` state, retained per its default retention window, not routed anywhere for manual triage beyond the `/quotes/email-queue` view, which only shows not-yet-resolved jobs); no Resend (or other) email driver — only Nodemailer/SMTP, per the task's explicit scope; orphaned `files` rows aren't cleaned up when a demo quote (or its PDF) is deleted (`quotes.pdfFileId` is `onDelete: set null`, so the `files` row and its S3/MinIO object simply become unreferenced — the same pre-existing gap as any other upload in this codebase).
- **Quotes mobile (S-03.12)** — fully out of scope until `apps/mobile` exists; PRD explicitly schedules this for Phase 5. The public-link/token design has no web-only assumption baked in that would need revisiting for a mobile "capture acceptance on site" flow, though that story most likely wants a distinct in-app authenticated capture path rather than reusing the public customer-facing link — a decision left for Phase 5.
- Quotes' own smaller loose ends within Stage 1's scope: no drag-and-drop line reordering (up/down buttons only — a deliberate simplification noted in `quote-line-editor.tsx`'s own comment, since a bounded per-quote line list doesn't need a drag library and buttons keep it fully keyboard-operable); no CSV/bulk quote import; no quote-level audit history *view* (writes happen — `entityType: "quote"`/`"quote_line"` — but there's no `GET /quotes/:id/history` endpoint or web History tab, matching Catalogue's own "writes now, view later" precedent, see the audit-log rollout item below).
- Contacts' remaining deferred scope: true per-org configurable lead stages (currently a fixed `new/qualified/proposal/won/lost` enum, not user-editable). **S-01.8 CSV import, S-01.5's pipeline board, and now S-01.3's Timeline tab are all built** — see Completed below. All of FEAT-01 · Contacts & CRM's 8 stories are now complete. S-01.3's timeline currently merges quotes + activities only (invoices/payments don't exist until Phase 2) — see the Timeline entry below for how it's structured to grow without a rework once those land.
- Once Catalogue/Quotes/Invoices land, the dashboard setup checklist's three items need wiring to real completion checks instead of their current static placeholder state, and Contacts' timeline (above) becomes buildable. Quotes/invoices should also grow their own attachments via the new `files` table/endpoints once they exist (Files was deliberately built generic — a plain nullable FK per consumer, not polymorphic — precisely for this).
- Logo/colours on documents (S-10.4) — **partially unblocked**: Quotes Stage 2 (below) now renders a real PDF pulling the org's name, address, and logo (fetched from S3/MinIO and embedded) from `business_profile` settings, so a quote PDF is genuinely branded with what Settings already lets an owner configure. Still not a full "colours" system, though — no accent-colour/theme setting exists anywhere (`business_profile` has no colour field), and Invoices/other future documents will each need their own renderer wired to the same settings key (no shared "document header" component extracted yet, since Quotes is still the only document type). "Full branding customization beyond logo+name+address" (the task brief's own phrase) remains explicitly out of scope.
- Audit log UI (S-10.6) — **the Contacts slice is now real** (see the 2026-08-06 entry below: `AuditLogService`, write instrumentation across `contacts.service.ts`/`leads.service.ts`/`activities.service.ts`, `GET /contacts/:id/history`, a web History tab). Catalogue, Quotes, Invoices, Credit Notes, and Payments all write `audit_log` rows now (`AuditLogService.record()` called on every mutation), but only Contacts has a `GET /:id/history` endpoint or web history view of its own, since nothing in any other module's task brief asked for one. What remains is a **system-wide rollout** — Jobs/Purchases/Expenses still have zero `audit_log` writers (they're still empty `@Module({})` stubs), and there's no cross-module/org-wide audit log *view* (only the per-contact history endpoint exists; nothing lists every audit entry across all entity types in one place). Each future module should wire `AuditLogService.record()` into its own mutating methods the same way the modules above do, and a later pass should decide whether a per-document history view (invoices/credit notes/payments) or an org-wide audit screen is worth building once more modules feed it.
- Full multi-rate tax-group CRUD and inclusive/exclusive pricing (FEAT-08) — the wizard only confirms one default named rate.
- Automated accessibility-testing tooling (axe-core/jest-axe or similar) — recommended by this session's formal WCAG 2.1 AA `@spine/ui` audit (see Completed below) as a follow-up so future component changes get an automated regression net instead of relying on a fresh manual audit each time; deliberately not installed during that pass (out of its stated scope).
- `calendar.tsx`'s pointer-only event-resize drag handles have no keyboard equivalent (flagged, not blocking — the same result is reachable via the keyboard-operable event-editor dialog); worth a keyboard-resize affordance if a future pass has room for it.
- The business-permission model itself is now real (multi-Owner + Teams + capability guard — see the 2026-08-10/2026-08-09 Completed entries below), and `@RequireCapability` is wired onto every module that exists today (contacts/leads/activities/meetings/catalog/quotes/invoices/credit-notes/payments/reports — `reports.receivables` gates on `dashboard.view`, matching that capability's own "receivables ... P&L/balance sheet stay Owner-only" description). Jobs/Ledger (as its own module, distinct from `LedgerService`)/Expenses are still empty `@Module({})` stubs — each needs its own capability wiring (`jobs.update_milestones`, `ledger.view`, `expenses.submit`/`expenses.approve`) the same additive way, alongside `@OrgRoles`, once each module actually lands.
- ~~Catalogue's `DELETE /catalog-items/:id` unguarded~~ — **reviewed 2026-08-09, confirmed correct as-is, not a bug.** Quotes now exists, so `quote_lines.catalogItemId` is a real FK — but it's deliberately `onDelete: set null` (quotes.ts's own schema comment: catalog provenance only, never re-read for pricing since every line snapshots its own name/price/tax at add-time per CLAUDE.md's "documents snapshot their data" rule). A "block delete if referenced" guard would actually be *wrong* here, unlike Contacts (where `journal_lines.contactId` genuinely matters for AR aging/reporting). Verified live: deleted a catalog item referenced by a real quote line — the delete succeeded, the quote line's `catalogItemId` went to `null`, and its snapshotted name/quantity/price were untouched. The web UI's delete confirmation already warns "Prefer Archive if this item may be referenced by a quote or invoice later," which is the correct/sufficient safeguard for what is deliberately a non-blocking relationship.
- ~~`isCompany` partial-update bug~~ — **fixed 2026-08-09**, see Completed below.
- MinIO's `spine` bucket isn't provisioned by `docker-compose.dev.yml`/the prod compose file — `FilesService` now self-heals this on first upload (`HeadBucket` → `CreateBucket` on 404, cached after), so no manual `mc mb` step is required, but a `docker-compose` `minio-init` sidecar (the more conventional pattern) would still be cleaner long-term than a lazy per-process check.

## Completed

### 2026-08-10 — Demo data now covers every previously-unseeded entity across Phases 0–4 Stage 2, and `apps/api/src/db/reset.ts`'s table list is fixed

- **User-requested cleanup pass before starting Phase 4 Stage 3**: demo data (`DemoDataService`) had fallen behind — Jobs' photos/notes/variation orders, Tax's tax groups, Credit notes, Meetings, a real business logo upload, and Teams/Staff/Field users were all either missing or only partially seeded despite their underlying features being complete. Closed every gap in one pass rather than leaving Stage 3 to inherit a demo org that doesn't reflect the real product.
- **Settings**: `business_profile` now optionally carries a real uploaded logo (via `FilesService.upload`, only when object storage is configured and the setting is otherwise unset — graceful degradation per CLAUDE.md) instead of staying unset; `prices_include_tax`, `quote_conversion_guard`, and `discount_approval_threshold` are now seeded explicitly rather than relying on their code-level defaults, so a fresh demo org's Settings pane isn't empty.
- **Tax**: a second rate ("State Surcharge", compound) and a tax group combining it with the existing "Sales Tax" rate, seeded via the real `TaxRatesService`/`TaxGroupsService`.
- **Credit notes**: one partial credit note issued against a seeded invoice via the real `CreditNotesService`, closing the gap explicitly flagged in this file's own "Next up" section.
- **Meetings**: 4 seeded meetings (3 contact-linked, 1 internal) via `MeetingsService`, flagged with a new `meetings.is_demo` column since meetings have no cascading demo-flagged parent for `deleteAll` to key off.
- **Jobs**: a kickoff note and a real uploaded photo per seeded job (via `JobsService.addNote`/`addPhoto`, reusing one uploaded placeholder file), plus one variation order on the "active" demo job — created and sent, deliberately left un-accepted/un-applied, since only a real customer action through the public quote-response flow can legitimately produce an accepted variation (same "don't fabricate status" principle as the existing accepted/rejected demo quotes).
- **Teams/Users**: two real login accounts seeded via Better Auth's own `auth.api.signUpEmail` — Sarah Mendes (Staff, joined the default Team) and Marcus Webb (Field, deliberately no Team, since Field is fixed and non-Team-configurable per CLAUDE.md's permission model). A new `member.is_demo` column marks the *membership* row only, never the underlying `user`/`account` row — per explicit user decision, `deleteAll` revokes a demo user's org access on cleanup but never deletes their actual login account. Idempotent: re-running seed reuses an existing `user` row by email instead of erroring, and a real ordering bug was found and fixed where the throwaway auto-org Better Auth's signup hook creates was being deleted *before* the new membership row was re-pointed at the real org — since `member.organizationId` cascades on `organization.id`, this silently deleted the membership the very next statement was trying to update. Fixed by re-pointing the membership first, then deleting the now-safely-orphaned auto-org only if it differs from the target org.
- **`DemoDataService.deleteAll`** extended to remove every new entity type in FK-safe order (credit notes before invoices, since `credit_notes.invoiceId` is `onDelete: restrict`; meetings and demo members last), with real per-type counts returned alongside the existing ones.
- **`apps/api/src/db/reset.ts`**: its hardcoded `TABLES` truncation list had drifted since before Jobs/Tax/Purchases/Credit Notes/Meetings/Teams existed — missing ~19 tables, meaning `pnpm db:reset` was silently leaving stale rows behind in every table added since. Fixed and verified exhaustively (every `pgTable(...)` name across `apps/api/src/db/schema/*.ts` cross-checked against the array, zero missing/extra). **Deliberately does not seed demo data itself** — user explicitly wants `pnpm db:reset` to stay a bare wipe, with demo data only ever loaded through the existing onboarding "Load demo data" UI flow, matching real first-run behaviour.
- New migration `0030_purple_doctor_octopus.sql` (`member.is_demo`, `meetings.is_demo` + its `(org_id, is_demo)` index) — applied to both the dev DB and the separate `spine_test` integration-test database (`.env.test`'s own `DATABASE_URL`, which needed the same migration run against it manually since it isn't auto-migrated).
- Verified end-to-end against a live local instance: full seed → inspect every new entity type via curl/psql → `deleteAll` → re-seed cleanly a second time (confirming idempotency, including for Sarah/Marcus's reused accounts). `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests) and both apps' typecheck pass.
- `docs/qa-script.md`'s §1 (Setup, Signup/onboarding) gained coverage for every new seeded entity plus a `pnpm db:reset` note in Setup.

### 2026-08-10 — `DemoDataService.deleteAll` count accuracy across all five entity types

- **All five counts (`contactsDeleted`/`catalogItemsDeleted`/`quotesDeleted`/`invoicesDeleted`/`paymentsDeleted`) now report the real number of rows actually removed inside the transaction, not `demoX.length`** — the same fix Phase 3 already applied to `jobsDeleted` after live testing caught the discrepancy there. In practice today only `contactsDeleted` can diverge (a demo contact referenced by permanent `journal_lines.contactId` ledger history can't be hard-deleted, same `onDelete: "restrict"` reasoning as jobs — see the method's own doc comment); the other four have no such blocking FK and were already numerically correct, but are now tracked via a real counter too so a future FK/constraint change can't silently make them wrong again without anyone noticing.

### 2026-08-10 — S-05.4: advance/deposit invoices — the last open story in Phase 2 · Money In

- **Design decision made with the user before building**: an `advance`-type invoice posts NOTHING to the ledger on issue — no debit AR, since nothing is actually owed for goods/services yet (an advance is a deposit *request*, not a claim on work already done). `InvoicesService.issue()` now branches on `existing.type === "advance"`: it still allocates a real gapless number and flips to `issued`, but skips the whole AR/revenue/tax posting block entirely (`journalEntryId` stays `null`). §7.2's own "Advance received" row (debit Bank/Cash, credit Customer Advances) is a *payment*-received posting, not an invoice-issued one, and it already existed — `PaymentsService.create` fires it the moment a deposit is actually recorded as an unallocated payment (S-06.4). "Advance applied to invoice" (debit Customer Advances, credit AR) also already existed (`PaymentsService.applyCredit`, S-05.5). So S-05.4 turned out to be almost entirely about *not* posting on issue and getting the surrounding guards right, not a new posting rule.
- **Real correctness bugs found and fixed while adding the advance-invoice guards**: `PaymentsService.create`'s and `applyCredit`'s own balance checks, and `suggestAllocation`'s own balance computation, were all still computing `totalAmount - amountPaid` — missing the `creditedAmount` subtraction added when credit notes (S-05.6) landed earlier this session. All three now correctly compute `totalAmount - amountPaid - creditedAmount`, so a payment/credit application against a partially-credited invoice no longer over-estimates its remaining balance.
- New guards across every place an invoice's "balance" is read for allocation purposes: `PaymentsService.create`/`applyCredit`/`suggestAllocation` and `CreditNotesService.create` all now reject an `advance`-type invoice with a clear, actionable error (it never posted to AR, so there's nothing to allocate a payment or credit note against) — both server-side (the actual enforcement) and client-side (`record-payment-dialog.tsx`/`apply-credit-dialog.tsx` now filter advance invoices out of their own "pick an invoice" lists, so the dead-end option isn't even offered).
- Web: the invoice builder's existing type selector (Standard/Advance/Progress — it turned out to already exist) is now backed by real behavior; the invoice PDF shows "DEPOSIT INVOICE" instead of "INVOICE" for this type; a "Deposit" badge appears next to the status badge on both the invoices list and detail page; "Record payment"/"Issue credit note" buttons are hidden on an advance invoice's own detail page (same reasoning as the server-side guards).
- Verified live end-to-end: created and issued a $500.00 advance invoice, confirmed via direct SQL that zero journal entries were posted for it, confirmed its PDF renders with the "DEPOSIT INVOICE" title, confirmed attempting to record a payment allocated to it is rejected with a clear error, then recorded that same $500.00 as an unallocated payment instead and confirmed the real "Advance received" posting fired correctly (debit Bank $500.00 / credit Customer Advances $500.00, balanced) — the actual intended lifecycle works end-to-end, not just the issue-time behavior in isolation. `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests) and both apps' `typecheck` still pass unchanged.
- **This closes out Phase 2 · Money In** — every story in FEAT-05/FEAT-06/FEAT-09's Phase 2 scope is now built. Phase 3 · Jobs & Construction Billing is next per the PRD's own phase order.

### 2026-08-10 — S-02.3: revenue posting split by each line's own catalog-item income account

- **Closes a long-flagged Stage 1 simplification.** `InvoicesService.issue()` previously posted an invoice's entire net subtotal to one blended revenue account (the seeded 4100 "Sales — Services" default, or a caller override) — flagged in that method's own doc comment and CHANGELOG's Next-up since Invoicing Stage 1 first landed. Now posts one credit line PER distinct income account: each `item`-type line's `lineSubtotalAmount` (post-discount, pre-tax net) is grouped by its own catalog item's `incomeAccountId` (`catalog_items.incomeAccountId`, an existing S-02.3 field that was populated in the schema but never actually read at posting time until now) via a new `InvoicesService.resolveRevenuePostingLines` helper.
- A line falls back to the same default/override account as before when it has no `catalogItemId` (a free-form line never added from the catalogue) or its catalog item has no `incomeAccountId` set (S-02.3 is a PRD "SHOULD," not "MUST" — an org that never bothered configuring per-item income accounts gets exactly the old single-blended-line behavior, reached via the fallback path rather than a separate code path, so nothing changes for orgs not using the feature). Discount/tax handling is unchanged — still net-of-discount per line, tax still posts as its own single line to Output Tax Payable.
- Verified live end-to-end: created a catalog item ("Widget") with its `incomeAccountId` explicitly set to a second seeded revenue account (code 4000, "Sales — Goods," distinct from the seeded default 4100 "Sales — Services"), built and issued a two-line invoice mixing that item with a plain free-form line, and confirmed via direct SQL that the posted journal entry split correctly: $50.00 credited to Sales — Goods (4000), $100.00 credited to Sales — Services (4100, the free-form line's fallback), $150.00 debited to Accounts Receivable — balanced, and each line landed on the account it should have. `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests, including `ledger-integrity.integration.test.ts`) and `typecheck` still pass unchanged.

### 2026-08-10 — S-03.10's invoice half: convert an accepted quote into a draft invoice

- `InvoicesService.convertFromQuote(orgId, actorUserId, quoteId)` (`POST /invoices/from-quote/:quoteId`) — only callable on an `accepted` quote (400 otherwise). Reads the quote's own already-snapshotted lines (never re-reads catalog items — `quote_lines` snapshotted name/price/tax when they were added, per CLAUDE.md's "documents snapshot their data") and creates a DRAFT invoice by delegating to the existing `InvoicesService.create()` — not a parallel insert path, so a converted invoice gets identical validation/audit-log/totals-computation behavior as one built from scratch. `create()` gained a new optional `sourceQuoteId` param (previously the column existed as provenance-only scaffolding with nothing ever setting it — see invoices.ts's own long-standing doc comment) which now actually gets populated on conversion.
- Deliberately no "already converted" guard — a quote can be converted more than once (e.g. a first invoice covering part of the quoted work, a second later invoice covering the rest); S-03.10's own wording doesn't ask for a one-shot restriction, and building one would be inventing scope Phase 2 has no milestone/progress-billing UI to actually drive yet.
- Web: a "Convert to invoice" button on the quote detail page, shown only when `status === "accepted"`, navigating straight to the newly-created draft invoice's own detail page (same "land on what you just created" pattern as the existing "Revise" button).
- Verified live end-to-end against a real seeded quote (flipped to `accepted` directly in Postgres for this test, since the demo seed itself doesn't include any pre-accepted quotes): converted a 3-line, $16,400.00 quote, confirmed the resulting draft invoice's `sourceQuoteId`, `totalAmount`, and all three lines' name/quantity/price matched exactly, then issued it and confirmed a real gapless number (`INV-0005`) was allocated — the full lifecycle works, not just the conversion step in isolation. `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests) and both apps' `typecheck` still pass unchanged.

### 2026-08-10 — S-05.10: one-click payment reminder

- `POST /invoices/:id/send-reminder` (`InvoicesService.sendReminder`) — only callable on an invoice that's actually overdue (per `isOverdue()`'s own definition), 400s otherwise. Reuses the exact same `INVOICE_SEND_EMAIL_QUEUE`/`InvoiceEmailWorker` as `sendEmail` (S-05.7) rather than a parallel mechanism — a reminder IS an invoice email, just reminder-flavored copy — via a new `isReminder?: boolean` job-data flag. Same PDF attachment, same `emailStatus`/`emailError`/`emailSentAt` bookkeeping either way; only the subject/opening line branch (`InvoiceEmailWorker.attemptSend`): a reminder says "Reminder: INV-0002 is now 5 days overdue" instead of "sent you an invoice," using days-overdue rather than restating the due date (the reminder's own existence already implies the date has passed).
- Two entry points, both gated on `invoice.isOverdue`: a "Send reminder" button on the invoice detail page, and a "Send reminder" item in the list page's per-row "more" menu (`InvoiceRowActions`) — the latter is the actual "one click" surface S-05.10's own wording asks for ("so following up isn't a chore" implies following up on several overdue invoices from the list, not opening each one individually). The row-menu action surfaces success/failure via the explorer's existing toast (`onReminderSent`/`onReminderFailed` callbacks threaded through `InvoicesTable` → `InvoiceRowActions`), not a silent no-op.
- Verified live against a real seeded overdue demo invoice and a real local SMTP server (MailDev): confirmed the reminder request returns `emailStatus: "pending"` immediately, polled and confirmed it flips to `"sent"`, and confirmed a reminder attempt against a non-overdue invoice correctly 400s. `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests) and both apps' `typecheck` still pass unchanged.

### 2026-08-10 — S-05.6: credit notes against an issued invoice

- **First new document type since Quotes/Invoices**: `credit_notes`/`credit_note_lines` (`apps/api/src/db/schema/credit-notes.ts`, migration `0024_fancy_makkari.sql`). Structurally the simplest of the three — `draft -> issued` only, no void (there's no PRD story asking to undo a credit note; a mistaken one is left as a small permanent correction, matching CLAUDE.md's "nothing financial is ever deleted" literally rather than inventing a reversal-of-a-reversal nobody asked for). Always tied to one specific `invoiceId`, which must be `issued` (a credit note against a draft/void invoice makes no sense — nothing's posted to AR yet, or the posting was already reversed).
- New `invoices.creditedAmount` column (denormalized running total, same "fast single-row check" reasoning as `amountPaid`) — kept as its own field rather than folded into `amountPaid`, since a credit note reduces what's owed without any cash changing hands, a meaningfully different fact for future reporting even though both currently feed `balanceDue = totalAmount - amountPaid - creditedAmount`. Every existing `balanceDue`/`isOverdue` computation updated to account for it: `InvoicesService.list/getOne`, `isOverdue()`, `InvoicePdfDocument` (which also gained a "Credited" line item), `InvoiceEmailWorker`'s balance-due email copy, and `ReportsService.receivables` (S-06.7) — nothing double-counts or drifts.
- `CreditNotesService.issue` posts §7.2's "Credit note issued" row — **debit Revenue + Tax Payable, credit Accounts Receivable** (the mirror image of "Invoice issued") — and is blocked if the credit note's total would push the invoice's effective remaining balance below zero (a credit note corrects what's owed, it can't manufacture a negative-balance/refund-owed state). `revenueAccountId` is optional on `issue()`, same default-to-account-4100 fallback as `InvoicesService.issue`'s own.
- Web: `CreateCreditNoteDialog` on the invoice detail page — deliberately a single free-form line (description + one total amount), not a multi-line builder like invoices/quotes, since a credit note is a small one-off correction and no PRD wording asks for more. Create-then-issue happens as one atomic action (closer to how a payment is recorded in one step than how an invoice is built as a draft first). The amount is submitted as the line's tax-inclusive total with `taxRatePercentage: 0` rather than exposing a separate tax-rate field — the server's line-tax computation treats its `unitPriceAmount` as pre-tax and adds tax on top, which would have silently inflated a credit beyond what the user typed had this dialog tried to back-calculate a pre-tax amount from a tax-inclusive total. `CreditNotesSection` shows issued credit notes read-only, same pattern as `PaymentsSection`.
- Verified live end-to-end against seeded demo data: created and issued a $500.00 credit note against a $3,950.00-balance invoice, confirmed the invoice's `balanceDue` dropped to $3,450.00 and `creditedAmount` to $500.00, confirmed the posted journal entry directly in Postgres (debit Sales — Services $500.00 / credit Accounts Receivable $500.00, balanced), confirmed a second over-credit attempt (a credit note total exceeding the invoice's now-reduced remaining balance) correctly 400s, and confirmed the receivables/AR-aging report (S-06.7, built earlier this session) correctly reflects the reduced balance with no double-counting. `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests, run against the newly-migrated schema) and both apps' `typecheck` still pass unchanged.
- Not built (explicitly out of scope for this pass, flagged in Next-up): demo-data seeding doesn't yet include a sample credit note (PRD §11.3's seed-data spec doesn't call for one specifically, unlike its "3 jobs"/"retention"/"expenses" callouts); no standalone `/credit-notes` list page (reachable only from the invoice it corrects, same "invoice-triggered, not yet a standalone page" scope Payments started with before S-06.4 gave it a `/payments` page of its own).

### 2026-08-10 — S-05.5: apply a payment's sitting unallocated credit to a later invoice

- `PaymentsService.applyCredit(orgId, actorUserId, paymentId, input)` (`POST /payments/:id/apply-credit`, `@RequireCapability("invoices.manage")`) — the missing other half of S-05.5 flagged in every prior Next-up entry: a payment recorded with some or all of its amount left unallocated (S-06.4, sitting in `Customer Advances`, account 2200) can now be applied against one or more invoices that weren't available (or weren't chosen) when the payment was first created. New `applyPaymentCreditSchema` in `packages/shared/src/schemas/payments.ts` reuses the existing `paymentAllocationInputSchema` — same shape as `create()`'s own `allocations`, just targeting an existing payment instead of a new one.
- Posts §7.2's "Advance applied to invoice" row — **debit Customer Advances, credit Accounts Receivable** — a genuinely different journal entry from `create()`'s "Payment received"/"Advance received" postings, since no new cash changes hands; this is a pure reclassification of money already sitting as credit. Same validation shape as `create()`'s own allocation loop (invoice must be `issued`, an allocation can't exceed the invoice's remaining balance) plus one more: the total requested can't exceed the payment's own `unallocatedAmount`.
- New `ApplyCreditDialog` (`apps/web/src/app/(app)/payments/apply-credit-dialog.tsx`) — a deliberately smaller form than `RecordPaymentDialog` (no date/method/amount fields, since nothing new is being recorded), reachable via a new "Apply credit" button on `PaymentDetailDrawer`, shown only when `unallocatedAmount !== "0"`. `payments-explorer.tsx`'s list refreshes after applying (new `onChanged` prop on the drawer) since a payment's own `unallocatedAmount` column changes.
- Verified live end-to-end: recorded a fresh $1,000.00 payment as pure unallocated credit against a contact with a real outstanding invoice, applied it via `POST /payments/:id/apply-credit`, confirmed the payment's `unallocatedAmount` dropped to `0` and the target invoice's `balanceDue` dropped by the same amount, confirmed the posted journal entry directly in Postgres (debit Customer Advances $1,000.00 / credit Accounts Receivable $1,000.00, balanced), and confirmed a second over-allocation attempt against the now-zero remaining credit correctly 400s. `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests) and both apps' `typecheck` still pass unchanged.

### 2026-08-10 — S-06.7 / S-09.5: receivables view with AR aging buckets — first real `ReportsModule`

- `apps/api/src/modules/reports/` was an empty `@Module({})` stub until now — `ReportsService.receivables(orgId)` computes 0-30/31-60/61-90/90+ (plus "current", not-yet-due) aging buckets by filtering `issued` invoices with a positive `balanceDue`, per CLAUDE.md's own "AR aging ... comes from filtering the ledger, not from separate calculations — don't build a shadow costing system" rule (read here as: derive on read from the same `totalAmount - amountPaid` invoices already carry, not a parallel maintained receivables table). `GET /reports/receivables` (`ReportsController`) is `@OrgRoles(["owner", "staff"])` + `@RequireCapability("dashboard.view")` — the one financial report Staff can be Team-granted per that capability's own existing label/description ("Receivables ... P&L/balance sheet stay Owner-only regardless of this grant"), so no new capability was needed.
- New `/receivables` web page (`apps/web/src/app/(app)/receivables/`) — five `StatCard`s (one per bucket) + a total, and a table of every outstanding invoice sorted oldest-overdue-first, each row linking to its invoice. Added to the Billing nav section (`nav-sections.tsx`), alongside Invoices/Payments.
- Verified live against seeded demo data: the seeded overdue invoice (`dueInDays: -5`) correctly bucketed into "0-30" with `daysOverdue: 5`, the not-yet-due one into "current", and paid-in-full/void/draft invoices were correctly excluded entirely; bucket totals summed exactly to `totalOutstanding`.
- `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests) and both apps' `typecheck` still pass unchanged.

### 2026-08-10 — S-06.6 receipt PDF, and a real cross-cutting bug fix: `CapabilityGuard` was silently no-op'ing "no active org" 403s on every `@RequireCapability` route over real HTTP

- **S-06.6 — receipt PDF**, exact structural mirror of `InvoicePdfService`/`InvoicePdfDocument` (`apps/api/src/modules/pdf/payment-pdf.service.ts` + `payment-pdf.template.tsx`): renders a payment's own stored `payments`/`payment_allocations` rows (never re-reads invoices for current data, matching CLAUDE.md's "documents snapshot their data" spirit extended to a receipt) into a branded PDF — business logo/name/address, received-from/date/method/reference, an "Applied to" table when allocated to one or more invoices, amount received, and a "held as credit" line when partially/fully unallocated (S-06.4). `GET /payments/:id/pdf` (live-rendered, same convention as `InvoicesController.getPdf`) and `POST /payments/:id/send-receipt` (synchronous — not queued via pg-boss like `InvoiceEmailWorker`, since a receipt resend is a low-volume, user-initiated single action, not something issued in bulk; see `PaymentsService.sendReceiptEmail`'s own doc comment) are both new. `payment-detail-drawer.tsx` gained "Download PDF" and "Email receipt" buttons in its footer, same pattern as `invoice-detail.tsx`'s own PDF/email buttons. The payment-creation confirmation email (`sendConfirmation`, unchanged) deliberately still doesn't attach the PDF — it fires synchronously inside `create()`'s post-commit path, and a slow PDF render has no business blocking or risking that confirmation; the PDF is opt-in via the new render-on-demand actions instead.
- **A real, previously-undetected bug found and fixed while verifying the above against a real running server**: every `@RequireCapability`-gated POST endpoint (invoices `issue`/`void`/`send-email`, payments `create`/`delete`, quotes, catalog, etc. — anything layered with `CapabilityGuard` on top of `@OrgRoles`) was unconditionally 403'ing with "No active organisation on session" over real HTTP, despite a valid session with `activeOrganizationId` set and an `owner` membership row confirmed directly in Postgres. `CapabilityGuard`'s own doc comment claimed Nest would run `@thallesp/nestjs-better-auth`'s internal `AuthGuard` first (since `AuthModule.forRoot(...)` is imported before `CapabilityGuard` is registered in `app.module.ts`'s `providers` array) and populate `request.session` before `CapabilityGuard.canActivate` read it. That assumption was wrong: confirmed live with a temporary debug log that `request.session` is `undefined` inside `CapabilityGuard` even on routes where `@OrgRoles`' own check on the *same request* already passed — Nest does not order cross-module global `APP_GUARD`s by import position, only by each module's own discovery order in the DI graph, and that order put `CapabilityGuard` first. This had been invisible until now because `capability.guard.integration.test.ts` (the guard's only existing test coverage) hand-builds a fake `ExecutionContext` with `request.session` pre-set directly — it exercises the guard's authorization *logic* correctly but never exercises real guard-ordering over real HTTP, so the ordering bug had zero test coverage. **Fix**: `CapabilityGuard` now resolves the session itself via `auth.api.getSession()` (the exact same primitive `AuthGuard` uses internally, via `fromNodeHeaders`), falling back to `request.session` first in case it's already set — no longer trusts guard ordering in either direction. Verified live end-to-end post-fix: a fresh signup's session immediately passed a capability-gated `POST /payments` (previously 403'd instantly), `POST /invoices/:id/issue` likewise, and the new `POST /payments/:id/send-receipt` succeeded and actually delivered a PDF-attached email through a real local SMTP server (MailDev). Full `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (38 tests, including `capability.guard.integration.test.ts` itself) still passes unchanged.
- This is a correctness bug against CLAUDE.md's own "server-side authorization on every endpoint" rule in the sense that the *previous* behavior was fail-closed (over-rejecting, not under-authorizing) — no security hole, every affected route simply refused every caller including legitimate owners/staff — but it meant a meaningful slice of the app's actual mutating surface (issuing/voiding invoices, recording/deleting payments, anything capability-gated) may never have actually been reachable over real HTTP outside of hand-built unit-style guard tests. Worth a project-wide sanity pass (smoke-testing a few more capability-gated POST/PUT/DELETE routes for real, not just via `capability.guard.integration.test.ts`'s synthetic context) next time any of them are touched, in case there's a second, unrelated reason any specific route still fails post-fix.

### 2026-08-10 — Close known gaps from the permissions work: HTTP-level tests, invite-as-owner removal, expired-invite UX, docs

- **First real HTTP-level test harness in this codebase.** Every prior "integration" test called services/guards directly against real Postgres, never through NestJS's own routing — so `@OrgRoles`/`CapabilityGuard` were never actually exercised as a real request hits them. New `apps/api/src/test-utils/test-app.ts` boots the real `AppModule` via `@nestjs/testing` and issues real `fetch` requests with real session cookies against an ephemeral port; `members.http.integration.test.ts` uses it to cover deactivate/reactivate/promote/demote through the real controller+guard chain, including the one thing service-layer tests structurally couldn't prove — a Staff session gets a genuine 403 from an owner-only route, and an unauthenticated request gets a genuine 401. Needed `unplugin-swc` in `vitest.integration.config.ts`: Vitest's default esbuild transform doesn't emit `emitDecoratorMetadata` output, which Nest's DI relies on for parameterless-typed constructor injection (e.g. `Reflector` with no explicit `@Inject`) — without it, `Test.createTestingModule` fails to resolve dependencies that boot fine under `nest build`'s real `tsc` compilation. Full 38-test integration suite passes.
- **Owner can no longer be granted at invite time.** Now that promote-to-Owner exists (below), inviting someone directly as `"owner"` was a redundant, inconsistent second path. `POST /org/invitations` now rejects `role: "owner"` server-side (Better Auth's own `createInvitation` would otherwise allow it — its own check only blocks a caller from inviting the `creatorRole` if *they* aren't already that role, which an Owner always is); the shared invite-role dropdown (onboarding + Users page) drops "Owner" from its options. Verified live: inviting as owner 400s with a clear message, inviting as staff still works.
- **Expired invites now read as expired, not just "pending but a bit late."** The Users page's pending-invites list gives an expired row the same `bg-overdue-bg text-overdue` badge styling already used for overdue invoices, inline copy explaining the link no longer works, and switches its Resend button from ghost to secondary variant so it reads as the obvious next action.
- **CLAUDE.md's permission-model section corrected** — it previously said invitation-acceptance didn't exist at all; that's been true since 2026-08-09 (below). Updated to describe the real flow (`sendInvitationEmail` → `/accept-invite/[id]` → Better Auth's `getInvitation`/`acceptInvitation`, both requiring a session matching the invited email) and to note Owner is only ever granted via promote, never invite.
- Stories: none new (hardening/gap-closing on S-10.2/S-10.13-16's existing scope), but the HTTP test harness is genuinely reusable infrastructure — the next module wiring `@OrgRoles`/`@RequireCapability` for the first time (see the Next-up item above) should write its guard-chain tests the same way, not fall back to service-layer-only coverage.

### 2026-08-09 — Fix invite emails never sending, add pending-invite listing, Teams detail UX fixes (user feedback on the permissions work)

- **Invite emails were silently never sent.** Better Auth's `organization` plugin only emails an invitee if a `sendInvitationEmail` callback is configured — ours had none, so `POST /org/invitations` created a real `invitation` row with zero user-visible effect, no error, no log. Wired `sendInvitationEmail` in `auth.ts` (reusing the existing `EmailService`/Nodemailer-SMTP wrapper, graceful no-op if SMTP isn't configured per CLAUDE.md's degradation rule) to send a real email linking to a new accept-invitation page.
- **Built the full accept-invitation flow this unlocked** — it didn't exist anywhere before. New `apps/web/src/app/accept-invite/[id]`: Better Auth's `getInvitation`/`acceptInvitation`/`rejectInvitation` both require an authenticated session matching the invited email (they never create an account inline), so the page branches on session state — no session → prompts sign up (a new `?inviteId=&email=` query-param variant of the existing signup form, landing back on the accept screen instead of onboarding) or log in (`?next=` redirect support added to `/login`); session with the wrong email → explains and offers sign-out; matching session → shows org/role and Accept/Decline. Since `getInvitation` itself can't reveal the invited email pre-auth, added a small unauthenticated `GET /public/invitations/:id` (same trust model as the existing public quote-acceptance link — reachable by anyone with the id, throttled, exposes only email/role/org name for a still-pending invitation) so the signup link can pre-fill and lock the email field instead of leaving it blank.
- **`GET/DELETE/:id/resend` added to `/org/invitations`** — feedback item "list all users including who's been sent an invite." Lists pending invitations (Better Auth's `listInvitations` returns every status; filtered to pending here), cancels one, or resends via `createInvitation`'s own built-in `resend: true` flag (refreshes expiry, re-sends the email, keeps the same invitation id — no manual cancel-then-recreate needed). Users page now shows a separate "Pending invites" section below the members table.
- **Team detail page**: Members and Privileges are now `Tabs` instead of two stacked sections, and gained an explicit "Back to teams" button matching the existing `contacts/[id]` back-button convention (a breadcrumb's clickable middle segment alone doesn't read as a strong back affordance one level deep).
- Verified end-to-end against real Maildev (not just asserted): an invite email arrives with a working link, accepting it joins the *invited* org (confirmed via the invitee's `role`/`isPrimaryOwner` after accept, not their own auto-created org from signup), the invitation drops out of the pending list once accepted, and resend/cancel both work — including a resent email actually landing in Maildev a second time.
- Stories: S-10.2 (invite flow, now actually functional end-to-end), the pending-invites half of S-10.13/S-10.18.

### 2026-08-09 — New permission model: multiple Owners, Teams with a privilege matrix, capability-gated API, Users/Teams web area, Profile & Preferences

- **This is the PRD/CLAUDE.md permission-model rewrite** (multi-Owner with one protected Primary Owner, Teams subdividing the Staff tier with a configurable privilege matrix, Owner and Field staying fixed/non-configurable) turned into working code across four phases, plus a fifth Profile & Preferences slice. Docs were updated first in an earlier session (`docs/spine-prd.md` §2.1/§4.2/§7/§9/§12, `CLAUDE.md`'s new "Permission model" section) — this entry is the implementation.
- **Phase A — schema**: `teams`/`team_memberships`/`team_privileges` tables, `member.is_primary_owner` with a partial unique index enforcing at most one Primary Owner per org at the DB level (not just application logic), a hand-authored backfill migration seeding a Primary Owner + default "General Staff" team for every pre-existing org, and Primary-Owner/default-team seeding wired into the real signup hook (not just the standalone seed script).
- **Phase B — API**: a new `CapabilityGuard`/`@RequireCapability` decorator layered *on top of* the existing `@OrgRoles` (never replacing it) — Owner always bypasses, Field always rejects, Staff passes iff the union of their Teams' grants includes the capability.
