Full `TeamsModule` (CRUD, privilege-matrix upsert, member assignment with org/role validation, default/last-team deletion guards). `MembersService` gained `promote`/`demote`/`transferPrimaryOwner`, each protecting the Primary Owner invariant the same way the pre-existing sole-owner deactivation guard already did. `@RequireCapability` wired additively onto every existing mutating endpoint (contacts/leads/activities/meetings/catalog/quotes/invoices/payments) — Jobs/Ledger/Expenses/Reports left alone since they don't exist yet (see Next-up).
- **Phase C — web**: a new top-level, Owner-only "Team" sidebar section with real routes (`/team/users`, `/team/teams`, `/team/teams/[id]`) replacing the old Settings-dialog `TeamPane` — gated both by hiding the nav link for non-Owners and by each page independently re-checking Owner status server-side via a new `GET /org/members/me`, since a hidden link was never meant to be the actual access control. The privilege-matrix UI renders straight from `@spine/shared`'s `CAPABILITIES`/`CAPABILITY_LABELS` — the same list `CapabilityGuard` enforces — so the UI can't offer a capability the server doesn't also check.
- **Phase D — Profile & Preferences**: a new self-service page (name/email/password/avatar/theme) reachable by every role including Field, via Better Auth's own `updateUser`/`changeEmail`/`changePassword` APIs rather than raw writes. Needed loosening `POST /files` from owner+staff to owner+staff+field (a Field user's own avatar upload was otherwise 403'd) and a new `themePreference` column on `user` so the theme setting persists server-side instead of only in `localStorage`. This app has no email-verification flow configured, so an email change here applies immediately, not with re-verification — documented as a known gap, not silently assumed away.
- Verified at every phase against real Postgres/a real boot, not just typechecked: 34 integration tests passing by the end of Phase D, a real `nest start` confirming every new route registers and DI resolves, and curl-driven end-to-end walkthroughs (signup → Primary Owner, team CRUD, capability enforcement, cross-role rejection, profile updates) against live dev servers.
- Stories: S-10.13 → S-10.18, S-10.17 (Profile & Preferences), the permission-model half of §2.1's rewrite.

### 2026-08-09 — Fix the ACTUAL root cause of the recurring org-membership loss: integration tests were wiping the dev DB

- The prior entry's fix (org-creation failure handling in the signup hook) was real and correct, but did NOT explain why the user's account kept losing its organisation/membership repeatedly, even after being manually repaired, with no code changes in between and no DB/Docker resets on their part (confirmed directly with the user: they only restart the Node process via `nest start --watch`).
- **Found the actual cause**: `apps/api/src/db/ledger-integrity.integration.test.ts`'s own cleanup runs `TRUNCATE journal_lines, journal_entries, accounts, organization RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE`. There was only ONE `DATABASE_URL` in `.env`, shared between the real local dev server and `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration`. `TRUNCATE organization ... CASCADE` deletes every row that references `organization.id` — every `member` row in the whole database, cascading further to `session`/etc. — regardless of which org they belong to. Every time that test suite ran against the shared dev DB (including by this session itself, run repeatedly as part of verifying earlier fixes — directly causing the exact symptom being investigated, on the user's own real account, live, mid-session), it silently wiped every real org/membership in the database as a side effect of test cleanup, with zero relationship to demo data, restarts, or the signup hook.
- **Fixed with proper test-DB isolation**, per the user's own choice over a narrower "don't TRUNCATE so broadly" patch: new `.env.test` (repo root, checked into git — no secrets, just a DB name) overrides `DATABASE_URL` to a separate `spine_test` database on the same local Postgres server. `apps/api/package.json`'s `test:integration` script now loads `.env.test` before `.env` (dotenv-cli is first-file-wins per key, not last — got this backwards on the first attempt, caught immediately by re-running and confirming the dev DB's org count still dropped, then fixed the `-e` flag order and reverified). CI is unaffected — it already spins up its own single-use, ephemeral Postgres container per run (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), so this override is a no-op there regardless.
- **README updated** with the one-time `spine_test` database creation + migration steps, and a note on why `test:integration` must never share a `DATABASE_URL` with local dev again.
- Also removed the temporary diagnostic logging (`requireOrgId`'s own logging, `session.create.after`'s logging, and a global `AuthErrorLoggingFilter`) added earlier in this same investigation to help catch a live occurrence — no longer needed now that the real cause is confirmed and fixed; reverted those three spots back to their exact pre-diagnostic form.
- **Re-repaired the user's account** (org + owner membership + chart of accounts) one final time now that it's actually safe from being wiped again — their session row itself didn't survive the last TRUNCATE either, so this required a fresh login rather than being invisible, which is expected and harmless.
- Verified conclusively, not just asserted: recorded the dev DB's exact `organization`/`user` row counts, ran `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` (all 6 tests passing) TWICE in a row, and confirmed the counts were bit-for-bit identical before and after both runs — proof of isolation, not just "it seems fine." Also reconfirmed `hp@gmail.com`'s membership survived both runs.
- Stories: none (dev-infra bug fix, no new scope) — but this was actively corrupting local dev state throughout this entire debugging session and probably others before it.

### 2026-08-09 — Fix: a signup whose org-creation step failed left a permanently broken, unrecoverable account

- User reported "insufficient permission" on every page after restarting the dev server, with `Organization plugin error: You are not a member of this organization` in the API logs, and suspected it was related to demo data. Investigated live against the real dev DB rather than guessing: ruled out demo-data code (grepped `demo-data.service.ts` — it never touches `member`/`organization`), ruled out a plain process restart (reproduced sign up → seed demo data → kill and restart the API process → same session cookie still worked correctly), ruled out DB/Docker resets (user confirmed they only restart the Node process, never Postgres), and ruled out Better Auth's session-refresh mechanism (read its own source: a refresh only `UPDATE`s `expiresAt` on the existing session row, never touches `activeOrganizationId` or creates a new row). Found the actual cause by querying the dev DB directly: the user's real account (`hp@gmail.com`) had a `user` row and a valid `session`, but the session's `activeOrganizationId` pointed at an organisation id that didn't exist anywhere in the `organization` table, and the account had zero rows in `member` at all — the org-creation step of signup (`apps/api/src/auth/auth.ts`'s `user.create.after` hook, which calls `auth.api.createOrganization` then `seedChartOfAccounts`) had failed for that one signup at some point, silently, while the user row itself was already committed and the login flow reported success.
- **Immediate fix**: recreated a real organisation + owner membership + chart of accounts for the affected account via a one-off script that called the exact same `auth.api.createOrganization`/`seedChartOfAccounts` calls a real signup uses, then pointed their existing session at it — confirmed live via SQL that the session now resolves to a real org with a real owner membership. No re-login needed. Script was deleted after running; it was a one-off recovery tool, not general-purpose.
- **Root-cause fix**: `databaseHooks.user.create.after` in `auth.ts` now wraps `createOrganization`/`seedChartOfAccounts` in a try/catch. On failure, it deletes the organisation (if one got created before the failure — it has no FK back to the user/member that created it, so it would otherwise leak as a permanent, empty, unowned org) and the just-created user row (its `member`/`session`/`account` rows cascade automatically via existing `onDelete: "cascade"` FKs), then re-throws — so a signup whose org setup fails now fails LOUDLY and completely, leaving no trace, rather than silently succeeding into a permanently broken, unrecoverable account. The user is free to retry signup with the same email afterward.
- **New regression test** (`apps/api/src/auth/signup.integration.test.ts`) — force-fails `seedChartOfAccounts` via `vi.spyOn` inside a real `auth.api.signUpEmail` call and asserts both the user row and the org row are gone afterward, reproducing the exact failure mode found live rather than only testing the happy path the existing test already covered.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/api` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `test:integration` passes (3 tests now, was 2 — the new failure-path test plus the two pre-existing signup assertions). Live: confirmed the happy-path signup flow is completely unaffected (fresh signup → session correctly gets a real `activeOrganizationId` → a guarded endpoint returns 200), and confirmed the originally-reported account (`hp@gmail.com`) now resolves correctly end-to-end.
- Stories: none (bug fix, no new scope) — though this directly protects PRD §6.8's implicit guarantee that a completed signup produces a working account.

### 2026-08-09 — Fix: clicking a later occurrence of a recurring meeting silently moved the whole series' start date

- User reported that on `/meetings`, clicking a repeating event's second-to-last occurrence moved the whole series' start day to the clicked occurrence. Real bug in `@spine/ui`'s `Calendar` component (`packages/ui/src/components/calendar.tsx`), not `/meetings`-specific — every consumer of `Calendar` with recurring events was affected.
- **Root cause**: the event editor dialog (`CalendarEventEditor`) pre-fills its `start`/`end` form state from `state.instance.occurrenceStart`/`occurrenceEnd` — the CLICKED occurrence's own computed date — rather than from the series' own `event.start`/`event.end`. Editing a recurring event always rewrites the WHOLE series (per the dialog's own "Editing a recurring event updates the entire series" copy, and `handleSave`, which has no per-occurrence branch — it calls `onEventUpdate` with the same event `id` regardless of which occurrence was clicked). So opening the editor on any occurrence past the first and clicking Save — even with zero changes — silently wrote that occurrence's date back as the new series start, since the form's `start` value was never the series' real anchor to begin with. `event.start === occurrenceStart` only for the FIRST occurrence (and always true for a non-recurring event), which is exactly why this only surfaced on later occurrences, matching the user's own report.
- **Fixed**: `CalendarEventEditor`'s edit-mode effect now seeds `start`/`end` from `event.start`/`event.end` (the series' own anchor), never from the clicked instance's occurrence dates. No behavior change for non-recurring events (`occurrenceStart`/`occurrenceEnd` always equal `event.start`/`event.end` for those, per `CalendarEventInstance`'s own doc comment) — this only changes what happens when a recurring event's 2nd+ occurrence is clicked.
- **Design-system docs updated** (`apps/design-system/src/app/components/calendar/page.tsx`'s Recurrence section) to explain this explicitly — a non-obvious "any occurrence edits the whole series, keyed off the series' own start" rule worth documenting so it isn't reintroduced by a future change to the editor.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui`/`@spine/web`/`@spine/design-system` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` passes (unaffected — pure UI-layer fix). Traced the actual data path end-to-end to confirm the fix reaches `/meetings` specifically: `meetings-calendar.tsx`'s `handleUpdate` sends whatever `event.start` the editor returns straight through as `PUT /meetings/:id`'s `startAt` — before the fix this was the clicked occurrence's date on a 2nd+ occurrence edit; after, it's always the series' own original start, matching the intended "editing updates the whole series, without moving it" behavior. No browser-automation tooling available in this environment to click through the actual UI, so this was verified by tracing the full pre-fill → submit → API-payload path rather than a live click-through.
- Stories: none (component bug fix, no new scope).

### 2026-08-09 — "Load demo data" now seeds business profile, industry template, and currency

- User asked whether onboarding's demo data included settings like business profile — it didn't. `POST /demo-data/seed` (the only caller is the onboarding welcome step's "Use demo data" button, which skips every other wizard step) previously left `business_profile`/`industry_template`/`currency` completely unset, so a demo org's quote/invoice PDFs rendered with a blank header and Settings' Business/Template panes showed nothing, despite the rest of the org looking fully populated.
- **New `DEMO_BUSINESS_PROFILE`/`DEMO_INDUSTRY_TEMPLATE`/`DEMO_CURRENCY` constants** (`apps/api/src/modules/demo-data/demo-data.service.ts`) — a fictional "Riverstone Builders" general contractor (name/address/industry text, no logo — seeding a fake one would need a real S3/MinIO upload through `FilesService` from inside a seed script, out of scope; the PDF header degrades to text-only exactly as it already does for any real org with no logo uploaded, so this is cosmetic, not a functional gap). Industry template is `"construction"` and currency is explicit `USD`, chosen to match `DEMO_CONTACTS`/`DEMO_CATALOG_ITEMS`, which are already construction-themed — a demo org whose data looked construction-flavored but was tagged "generic" in Settings would be a confusing mismatch for anyone evaluating the industry-template feature.
- **New `DemoDataService.seedSettingIfUnset()`** — writes a setting only if the org doesn't already have one at that key, via the newly-injected `SettingsService` (`DemoDataModule` now imports `OrgModule`). Confirmed with the user's own "is this safe to call twice" bar this codebase applies everywhere: seeding twice leaves the first seed's values in place, and — more importantly — seeding after a REAL business profile was already set (a realistic path if this were ever called from somewhere other than the pre-wizard welcome step) doesn't clobber it. In practice `seed()`'s only caller runs before any settings step could have set these, so the guard is currently a formality, but it's an explicit one rather than a silently relied-on assumption.
- **`deleteAll()` deliberately does NOT remove these settings** — same reasoning already applied to a demo contact with posted ledger history (see the earlier "Demo data now covers Invoices + Payments" entry): a `settings` row is a singleton per org+key, not a taggable/deletable "demo" row, and removing it would leave the org with literally no business profile rather than restoring some prior state (there is no prior state to restore to — the guard above already means these are only ever set once, at seed time). Leaving them in place after "delete all demo data" matches how a real org's own settings are never touched by that action either way.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/api` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `test:integration` passes. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres: seeded a fresh org and confirmed `business_profile`/`industry_template`/`currency` all populated correctly via `GET /org/settings/:key`; re-ran seed on the same org and confirmed the values were unchanged (not re-randomized or duplicated); on a separate fresh org, set a real business profile FIRST (`PUT /org/settings/business_profile`, "Real Company Inc"), then seeded, and confirmed the real profile survived untouched — the guard works as designed, not just in the happy path.
- Stories: S-12.8/F12 (demo data), extending its existing partial coverage — no new story number.

### 2026-08-09 — Dialog follow-up #2: fix Calendar's event editor losing its height cap entirely (scrollbar gone, footer pushed off-screen)

- The previous same-day fix (migrating Calendar's event editor to `DialogBody`) introduced a worse regression, caught by the user from a screenshot: the dialog lost its height cap entirely — no scrollbar, and the Cancel/Create event buttons rendered below the dialog's own rounded border, cut off by the viewport.
- **Root cause**: Calendar's event editor is the only `DialogContent` usage in the whole codebase (checked every other call site to confirm) where `DialogHeader`/`DialogBody`/`DialogFooter` are wrapped in an extra element — a `<form onSubmit={...}>`, needed to make the Enter key and the submit button actually save the event. A plain `<form>` is not a flex container by default, so even though `DialogContent` is `flex flex-col` with a `max-h-[85vh]` cap, `DialogBody`'s `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` had nothing to flex against — the `<form>` itself just grew to fit all its content, unbounded, pushing `DialogContent` (and the footer inside it) taller than the cap could actually constrain, since `DialogContent`'s `max-h` was fighting a child that refused to shrink.
- **Fixed**: the `<form>` itself is now `className="flex min-h-0 flex-1 flex-col"` — `flex-1` lets it claim `DialogContent`'s bounded height, `min-h-0` overrides the flex-item default (`min-height: auto`, which lets a flex child grow past its container to fit its own content) so it actually respects that height, and `flex-col` makes it a proper flex parent for `DialogHeader`/`DialogBody`/`DialogFooter` to lay out inside. This is the same "flex child needs `min-h-0` to make `overflow-y-auto` on ITS OWN child actually engage" issue as the `settings-dialog.tsx` fix earlier the same day — same root cause, different location.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui` `typecheck`/`lint` clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/web` `typecheck`/`build` clean. Live: confirmed no other `DialogContent` usage anywhere in the app wraps its header/body/footer in an intermediate non-flex element the same way (grepped every call site) — this was a one-off, not a pattern needing a wider fix. `/meetings` briefly 500'd on the first request after the change (same transient Fast-Refresh recompile pattern noted in both earlier Dialog entries today), settled to consistent 200s with real page content on follow-up requests.
- Stories: none (component fix, no new scope).

### 2026-08-09 — Dialog follow-up: `DialogHeader` gets its bottom border; Calendar's event editor gets padding back

- User caught two gaps in the same-day Dialog rework: `DialogHeader` had no bottom border (should match `DrawerHeader`'s exact border, per that entry's own claim of matching it — it didn't), and the Calendar component's "New event"/"Edit event" dialog (used by `/meetings`) had zero padding around its form fields, since it was never migrated to `DialogBody`.
- **`DialogHeader` now has `border-b border-border`**, correcting the earlier entry's inaccurate doc comment claiming it already matched `DrawerHeader` — it hadn't.
- **The real gap**: the earlier sweep for `Dialog` call sites only searched `apps/web/src` and `apps/design-system/src`, missing `packages/ui/src` itself — `calendar.tsx`'s own event-editor `DialogContent` (used internally by every `Calendar` instance, including `/meetings`) still had its form fields sitting directly against the now-unpadded `DialogContent`, plus its own hand-rolled `max-h-[60vh] overflow-y-auto` on the middle `<div>` — the exact same category of bug `DialogBody` was built to fix, just missed because it lives in the component library rather than an app page. Fixed: that div is now `DialogBody`, and the manual `max-h-[60vh]` was removed (redundant — `DialogContent`'s own `max-h-[85vh]` cap plus `DialogBody`'s `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` already bounds it correctly, matching every other migrated dialog). Checked `command.tsx` (the other internal `packages/ui/src` `Dialog` user, the ⌘K search-palette pattern) too — deliberately left alone, since it already intentionally opts out of `DialogHeader`/`DialogFooter`/padding (`overflow-hidden p-0`) with its own internal `CommandList` scroll region; not a missed case.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui`/`@spine/web`/`@spine/design-system` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` still passes. Live: `/meetings` briefly 500'd on first request after the source change (confirmed transient — three consecutive follow-up requests all returned a clean 200 with real page content, same Fast-Refresh-recompile pattern as the original Dialog rework's own verification note).
- Stories: none (component fix, no new scope).

### 2026-08-09 — `@spine/ui` Dialog: pinned header/footer, scrollable body (new `DialogBody`), rolled out everywhere

- User reported Record Payment's footer scrolled away with the content instead of staying pinned at the bottom, and asked for Dialog to follow the same header/body/footer split Drawer already has (only the body scrolls). `Dialog`/`DialogContent` never had this — header/footer were plain siblings with margin, and callers with tall content (Record Payment, the CSV import wizard, the email queue) each hand-rolled their own `max-h-[…vh] overflow-y-auto` directly on `DialogContent`, which scrolls the WHOLE dialog including the footer, not just the middle.
- **`packages/ui/src/components/dialog.tsx` restructured to mirror `drawer.tsx` exactly**: `DialogContent` is now a height-bounded (`max-h-[85vh]` at `size="md"`, unchanged fixed `h-[min(720px,85vh)]` at `size="lg"`) `flex flex-col`, with no default padding of its own. New `DialogHeader`/new `DialogBody`/`DialogFooter` each own their own padding and border, matching `DrawerHeader`/`DrawerBody`/`DrawerFooter`'s exact classes: header and footer are `shrink-0` (never scroll), `DialogBody` (new export) is the only `flex-1 overflow-y-auto` region between them. Confirmed with the user first that BOTH sizes should get the height cap (not just `size="lg"`), so a tall `size="md"` dialog like Record Payment also gets a genuinely pinned footer, not just large ones.
- **Every existing `Dialog` usage in the app updated to the new shape** — 16 files: `record-payment-dialog.tsx` and `new-payment-dialog.tsx` (the two directly reported, both previously hand-rolling `max-h-[85vh] overflow-y-auto p-6` on `DialogContent` itself — removed, replaced with a plain `DialogBody`), `activities-section.tsx`, `addresses-section.tsx`, `contact-persons-section.tsx`, `leads-section.tsx`, `lead-dialog.tsx`, `meetings-calendar.tsx`, `email-queue-dialog.tsx` (also had its own hand-rolled `flex-1 overflow-hidden` middle region, now a plain `DialogBody`), `import-contacts-dialog.tsx` (a 3-step wizard that had ALREADY manually built the exact same header/scroll-body/footer split by hand, compensating for `p-0` with per-section padding — now uses the real primitives instead, same visual result with less bespoke CSS), `invoice-detail.tsx`'s void-reason dialog, and `logo-crop-dialog.tsx`. `settings-dialog.tsx` (the one dialog that builds its own internal two-pane nav+content layout, no header/body/footer at all) needed one small fix: its inner `<div className="flex h-full min-h-0">` was relying on `DialogContent` being a plain block container — now that `DialogContent` is itself `flex flex-col`, that inner div is a flex ITEM, so `h-full` alone no longer reliably fills the remaining space; changed to `flex min-h-0 flex-1` to correctly claim the remaining height as a flex child.
- **Design-system docs updated** (`apps/design-system/src/app/components/dialog/page.tsx`): the main preview, Anatomy code sample, and the "Confirming a destructive action" example now show `DialogBody`; a new `DialogBody` API subsection explains when to reach for it; a new "Scrollable body, pinned header and footer" example demonstrates the actual fix (a tall `Textarea` inside `DialogBody` with the footer staying visible). `components/select/page.tsx`'s "Inside a Dialog" example updated the same way, since it's the other doc page with a live `Dialog` composition.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui`/`@spine/web`/`@spine/design-system` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` still passes (unaffected — UI-only change). **A real regression was caught and fixed during live verification, not by static checks**: `/invoices`/`/payments`/`/quotes` briefly 500'd in the running dev server — traced by bisecting with `git stash`/`git stash pop` against the live server to confirm it was transient Next.js Fast-Refresh churn from rapidly stashing/restoring 16 files while the dev server was mid-recompile, NOT a real bug in the dialog changes — confirmed conclusively once the server settled: three consecutive `curl` checks on every affected route (`/invoices`, `/payments`, `/quotes`, `/catalog`, `/leads`) returned a clean 200 with real page content and no error boundary in the response body.
- Stories: none (component/infra fix, no new scope).

### 2026-08-09 — Invoice detail page now shows every payment allocated against it

- User asked to see all payments related to an invoice on the invoice detail page — nothing surfaced this before; the earlier "Web UI for Invoices and Payments Stage 1" session explicitly scoped that out.
- **New `PaymentsService.listForInvoice(orgId, invoiceId)`** — a real join through `payment_allocations` (not a `contactId` filter over the existing `list()`, which would also surface a payment allocated to a DIFFERENT invoice for the same contact, or a fully unallocated one, neither of which belongs on this specific invoice's page). Each row carries `allocatedToThisInvoice` — that payment's specific allocation against THIS invoice, distinct from its own `amount` — since a single payment can legitimately span several invoices (S-06.2) and the page needs "how much of this payment applies here," not just "this payment touched this invoice somewhere."
- **`GET /payments` gained an `invoiceId` query param**, dispatching to `listForInvoice` when present (mutually exclusive with `contactId` in practice, `invoiceId` simply takes precedence if both were somehow passed) — reused the existing list route rather than adding a new path, since the response shape only differs by one extra field.
- **New `apps/web/src/app/(app)/invoices/payments-section.tsx`** — a read-only "Payments" card on the invoice detail page (each row: payment number linking to `/payments`, date, method, reference, and the amount allocated specifically to this invoice), rendered between the Lines and Totals cards. Shows a plain "No payments recorded against this invoice yet" line rather than a full empty-state illustration, since it's a subsection of an existing page, not its own page. `invoice-detail.tsx` now tracks a `paymentsRefreshKey`, bumped by `RecordPaymentDialog`'s `onRecorded` callback, so recording a payment immediately refreshes this section without a full page reload.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/api`/`@spine/web` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` passes. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres: created two issued invoices ($400.00 and $300.00) for one contact, recorded one $500.00 payment split across both ($400.00 + $100.00) plus a second standalone $200.00 payment against only the second invoice, then confirmed `GET /payments?invoiceId=` for each invoice returns exactly the right rows with exactly the right `allocatedToThisInvoice` amounts (invoice 1: one payment, $400.00 — not its $500.00 total; invoice 2: both payments, $100.00 and $200.00 respectively) — and confirmed the existing `GET /payments?contactId=` path is completely unaffected (still returns the plain shape with no `allocatedToThisInvoice` field). **A mistake made and fixed during this verification**: ran `rm -rf apps/web/.next` while the user's own long-running `next dev` process (from `turbo run dev`) was still serving requests, which broke it (`__webpack_modules__[moduleId] is not a function`, then a bare 500) — a genuinely risky operation against someone else's live process that should not have been done without stopping the server first. Recovered by restarting `apps/web`'s dev server directly (`pnpm dev`, backgrounded) and re-confirming `/invoices/:id` returns 200 with the Payments section present; the user was told explicitly that their dev server is now running detached from the original `turbo run dev` supervision and may want to restart it themselves to restore the normal process tree.
- Stories: none new — a UI gap closed within already-shipped S-06.1/S-06.2 scope.

### 2026-08-09 — Send invoice emails (S-05.7) and payment confirmation + org notification

- Second half of "let's build payments standalone UI and then let's do sending invoice and payment confirmation emails" — this session closes the email-sending gap.
- **New `InvoiceEmailWorker`** (`apps/api/src/modules/invoices/invoice-email.worker.ts`) — a near-exact structural copy of `QuoteEmailWorker` (same render-then-persist-then-attach-and-email shape, same `emailStatus`/`emailError`/`emailSentAt` bookkeeping, same graceful degradation when SMTP is unconfigured or the contact has no email on file). The one real difference: invoices have no public accept/reject link (that's quotes-only), so the email body includes a plain-language balance-due summary instead — the closest thing S-05.7's "payment instructions" wording can back up today, since no payment link/gateway exists.
- **`InvoicesService.sendEmail()` is a deliberately SEPARATE action from `issue()`** — per the user's own explicit choice this session (offered as an option: auto-send-on-issue vs. a separate action; separate action was chosen) — issuing an invoice only posts to the ledger; a new `POST /invoices/:id/send-email` (issued invoices only) is what actually queues the email, so a business can issue without immediately notifying the customer. New `INVOICE_SEND_EMAIL_QUEUE`/`InvoiceEmailWorker` provider registered in `InvoicesModule` (now also importing `EmailModule`/`FilesModule`/`OrgModule` — the last one needed directly by the worker for `business_profile` branding, not just transitively via `PdfModule`, since Nest resolves each module's own declared imports independently — a real `UnknownDependenciesException` was hit and fixed live, not just inferred).
- **Payment confirmation is TWO independent channels**, per the user's own "both" choice: (1) a plain HTML/text confirmation email direct to the paying contact — no PDF attachment, since `PaymentPdfService`/S-06.6 doesn't exist yet, tracked separately — summarizing the amount, what was applied to which invoice(s), and any remainder held as credit; (2) an org-facing notification via the ALREADY-GENERIC `NotificationsService.notify()` (the same primitive S-03.7's quote-viewed/accepted notifications use), extended with a new `payment_received` type — email to owner/staff plus an in-app row linking to `/payments/:id`. Both fire from a new private `PaymentsService.sendConfirmation()`, called only after `create()`'s transaction has committed (same "never let a notification failure undo or block an already-committed action" posture as every other `notify()` call in this codebase) — never throws, every failure is logged and swallowed.
- **`PaymentsModule`/`PaymentsService` gained three new dependencies**: `EmailModule` (the confirmation send), `NotificationsModule` (the org notification), `OrgModule` (business name in the confirmation email).
- **Web**: `invoice-detail.tsx` gained an "Email invoice" button (issued invoices only) calling the new endpoint, plus the exact same pending/sent/failed status block quote-detail.tsx already has (including the same 4-second polling while `emailStatus === "pending"`, since the actual send happens out-of-band via the queue worker). `invoices/types.ts` gained `emailStatus`/`emailError`/`emailSentAt` fields it was previously missing entirely (never needed until this session, since nothing wrote them before).
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/api`/`@spine/web` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` passes. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres AND real SMTP (the dev environment's own MailDev container, `SMTP_HOST=localhost:1025`, already configured in `.env`) — not just a "does it enqueue" check: issued an invoice with a real contact email, called `send-email`, confirmed `emailStatus` transitioned pending → sent with `pdfFileId` populated, and confirmed via MailDev's own container logs that a real SMTP message was received (`"Spine sent you an invoice (INV-0001)"` → the contact's address). Confirmed `send-email` on a draft invoice correctly 400s. Recorded a $600.00 payment against that invoice and confirmed via the same MailDev logs that BOTH emails landed — `"Spine received your payment (RCP-0001)"` to the customer, `"Payment received from Email Test Customer"` to the signed-up owner account — and confirmed via `GET /notifications` that the in-app row was written with the correct `type: "payment_received"`, message, and `link`. Confirmed the invoice's `amountPaid`/`balanceDue` updated correctly alongside all of this. One real bug caught by NestJS's own DI resolution at boot, not by inspection: `InvoiceEmailWorker` initially failed to start with `UnknownDependenciesException` (missing `SettingsService`) — `InvoicesModule` needed its own explicit `OrgModule` import, fixed and reverified live before continuing.
- Stories: S-05.7, plus the confirmation/notification half of S-06.6 (a text/HTML receipt exists; the PDF attachment itself is still open, see "Next up").

### 2026-08-09 — Standalone `/payments` page — S-06.1–S-06.5's own home, not just invoice-triggered

- User asked to build the standalone Payments UI next (deferred as a deliberate scope cut in the earlier "Web UI for Invoices and Payments Stage 1" session, whose own note flagged S-06.4's "payment with no invoice yet" case had no real entry point without one). This closes that gap.
- **New `apps/web/src/app/(app)/payments/` route** — `types.ts` (mirrors `payments`/`payment_allocations`' row shape; list responses have no `allocations` field, only `GET /payments/:id` does — confirmed against `PaymentsService.list`/`getOne`'s own different shapes before assuming, not guessed), `payments-table.tsx` (flat, no grouping, mirrors `invoices-table.tsx`'s shape minus a status column — a payment has no status enum, just recorded-or-not), `payments-explorer.tsx` (stat cards for total payments + unallocated credit total — the latter only shown when every listed payment shares one currency, since a raw cross-currency sum would be nonsense — contact filter, no status filter), `payments-skeleton.tsx`, `use-payment-stats.ts`, `contact-picker.tsx`/`entity-toast.tsx` (duplicated per this codebase's established per-feature-folder convention), and `page.tsx`.
- **New `new-payment-dialog.tsx`** — a contact-first sibling to the existing invoice-triggered `invoices/record-payment-dialog.tsx`, not a shared component (duplicated for the same reason contact-picker/entity-toast are — no cross-feature-folder imports exist anywhere else in this codebase). Starts from a contact picker instead of a preset invoice, then follows the same S-06.3 oldest-first-suggestion-then-editable-allocation flow. This is the first REAL entry point for S-06.4 ("record a payment with no invoice yet, held as customer credit") — the invoice-triggered dialog could only reach that outcome by opening from some invoice and then not allocating to it, never a natural path when there's genuinely no invoice yet.
- **New `payment-detail-drawer.tsx`** — read-only (a payment has no draft/edit lifecycle per `payments.ts`'s own schema comment: it's a single atomic "record a payment" action, not a document), following Catalogue's drawer-not-page precedent for a flat, single-entity record with no line items worth a whole page. Shows date/method/reference/amount/allocated/unallocated, each allocation line-item linking to its invoice, and notes. Delete is only offered when `allocatedAmount === "0"` (`PaymentsService.delete`'s own guard) — hidden entirely rather than shown-then-rejected, matching invoices/quotes' own row-actions precedent.
- **`Payments` registered in the sidebar** (`apps/web/src/lib/nav-sections.tsx`) under "Billing", after Invoices, using a `Banknote` icon.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (`/payments` compiles as a dynamic route). One real mistake caught by typecheck before it ever ran: `PaymentsTable` was first written passing a nonexistent `onRowClick` prop straight through to `DataTable` (copied the wrong pattern from memory) — `DataTable` has no such prop; the correct, actually-used-elsewhere pattern (confirmed by reading `catalog-table.tsx`) is a clickable button inside a column's own `render`. Fixed before any live testing. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres (fresh isolated throwaway org/contact, left in place afterward per this session's own established practice): confirmed `GET /payments` returns `[]` for a contact with none, recorded a fully-unallocated $250.00 cash payment via the exact payload `new-payment-dialog.tsx`'s submit handler sends (S-06.4), confirmed `GET /payments/:id` returns the `allocations: []` shape the drawer's "held as unallocated customer credit" message depends on, confirmed `GET /payments` (the list endpoint the table itself calls) omits `allocations` entirely as expected, deleted the unallocated payment successfully (200), and recorded a second payment to confirm gapless numbering (`RCP-0002`) continues correctly across the delete.
- Stories: S-06.1, S-06.2, S-06.3, S-06.4, S-06.5 (web UI half — API-side was already complete).

Moved it to a new "Billing" section (`apps/web/src/lib/nav-sections.tsx`) rather than leaving it grouped with Contacts/Pipeline/Meetings/Catalogue/Quotes — matches the PRD's own phase naming (`docs/spine-prd.md` §12: Phase 1 "Sales Spine" vs. Phase 2 "Money In", not part of Sales) and its enquiry → quote → job → invoice → payment → books workflow, where a quote is a sales document but an invoice is a financial one. ### 2026-08-09 — Fix: `pnpm db:reset` was silently skipping `invoices`/`invoice_lines`/`payments`/`payment_allocations`

- User asked whether the tables added this session were covered by the dev DB reset script. They weren't — `apps/api/src/db/reset.ts`'s hardcoded `TABLES` list (used for its single `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` statement, not a migration replay) was last updated before Invoices/Payments existed, so a reset would leave those four tables' rows behind entirely undetected — no error, since `TRUNCATE` simply doesn't touch a table that isn't named in its own statement.
- **Fixed**: added `invoice_lines`, `invoices`, `payment_allocations`, `payments` to `TABLES`, placed near the existing quote-related entries. Cross-checked programmatically against every `pgTable(...)` name across `apps/api/src/db/schema/*.ts` to confirm no other table has silently drifted out of sync the same way — none had; this was the only gap.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck` clean. Live against the real local dev Postgres: seeded a fresh org with demo data (5 invoices, 2 payments, confirmed present via direct SQL row counts), ran `pnpm db:reset`, and confirmed via a follow-up SQL query that `invoices`/`payments`/`invoice_lines`/`payment_allocations` — along with every other table checked (`quotes`, `contacts`, `organization`, `journal_lines`, `accounts`) — were all correctly emptied (`Truncated 33 tables + 12 pg-boss tables`, no FK errors).
- Stories: none (infra bug fix, no new scope).

### 2026-08-09 — Demo data now covers Invoices + Payments — 5 seeded invoices (draft/issued/void/partial/paid) and 2 real payments

- User asked whether demo data included the two modules that landed earlier this same day (Invoices, Payments Stage 1) — it didn't; `DemoDataService` was last touched before either module existed, and PRD §11.3's own seed-data spec explicitly calls for "invoices with partial payments" as part of the demo dataset. This session closes that gap for everything currently buildable — retention/jobs (Phase 3) and vendor bills/expenses (Phase 4) genuinely aren't yet, see "Next up".
- **New `DEMO_INVOICES` seed set** (`apps/api/src/modules/demo-data/demo-data.service.ts`) — 5 invoices reusing the same seeded contacts/catalogue items by index as `DEMO_QUOTES` already does, spanning every outcome Phase 2 can currently produce: one paid in full, one issued and partially paid (also overdue, `dueInDays: -5`), one issued with no payment yet, one left as an unissued draft, and one issued then voided with a reason (S-05.8's reversing entry, shown in the demo set for the first time). Seeded via the real `InvoicesService.create`/`.issue`/`.void` and `PaymentsService.create` — same principle `seedQuotes` already established for quotes (real totals/numbering/posting logic reused, not duplicated) — so a seeded "issued" demo invoice has a genuine `INV-0001`-style number and a real §7.2 journal entry, not a hand-set row. Deliberately does NOT wire a `sourceQuoteId` to any specific seeded quote — S-03.10 (quote → invoice conversion) isn't built yet, so there's no real conversion action to seed through.
- **Found and fixed a real bug while testing this**: `PaymentsService.create` had no `isDemo` parameter at all — it hardcoded `isDemo: false` on every payment, unlike `InvoicesService.create`/`QuotesService.create`, which both already accepted one. This wasn't reachable through any existing code path (no caller needed it before this session), but it meant a seeded demo payment's `payment_allocations` row survived `deleteAll`'s later invoice-deletion step even after the payment row itself was correctly removed as demo data — caught live via a real `DrizzleQueryError` (`payment_allocations_invoice_id_invoices_id_fk` violation) when testing "delete all demo data" end-to-end, not by inspection. Fixed by adding the same `isDemo = false` parameter `InvoicesService.create` already has, and passing `true` from `seedInvoices`.
- **`DemoDataService.deleteAll` reordered and extended**: payments are now deleted first (their allocations cascade with them — `payment_allocations.payment_id` is `onDelete: cascade`), then invoices, then quotes, then contacts, then catalogue items — required because `payment_allocations.invoice_id` and `invoices.contact_id` are both `onDelete: restrict`, so anything referencing a row must go before it. One deliberate, documented behavior change: a demo contact that had an issued or paid demo invoice posted real, immutable `journal_lines` history against it (unlike quotes, which never post to the ledger) — that contact genuinely cannot be hard-deleted afterward, matching exactly how a real contact with real ledger history already behaves (`ContactsService.delete` offers deactivation instead). The invoice/payment rows themselves are still fully removed regardless; only the contact record can be left behind. `DemoDataService.status()` was adjusted to not check for a leftover demo contact (it would otherwise report `hasDemoData: true` forever after this happens even once) — it now checks catalogue items/quotes/invoices, which are always fully removable.
- **Settings' "Delete all demo data" pane and the onboarding welcome step's copy updated** (`apps/web/src/app/settings/panes/data-pane.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/onboarding/steps/welcome-step.tsx`) to mention quotes/invoices/payments, not just contacts/catalogue items, and to note the contact-retention edge case in a doc comment for the next person who touches this pane.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/api`/`@spine/web` `typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` passes. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres, twice — the first run reproduced the `isDemo` bug exactly as described above (seed succeeded, `deleteAll` 500'd with the FK violation, confirmed via the API's own error log and a direct SQL query showing the orphaned payment had `is_demo: false`); after the fix, a second fresh isolated org's full cycle succeeded end to end: seed created 5 invoices (4 issued, 1 voided) and 2 payments, a direct SQL trial-balance query across every journal line the seed posted confirmed total debits exactly equal total credits ($29,440.00 each side) and that Accounts Receivable's net balance ($5,850.00) exactly matches the sum of the two still-outstanding invoices' own balances, and `deleteAll` then correctly removed 14 contacts/15 catalogue items/6 quotes/5 invoices/2 payments — except exactly the 4 contacts that had an issue()'d demo invoice, confirmed by name to be precisely the expected ones, left behind as documented, with `GET /demo-data/status` correctly reporting `hasDemoData: false` afterward regardless.
- Stories: S-12.8/F12 (demo data), extending its existing partial coverage — no new story number.

### 2026-08-09 — Web UI for Invoices and Payments Stage 1 — `/invoices` list/detail/builder, invoice-triggered "Record payment" dialog

- Both Invoices Stage 1 and Payments Stage 1 (see the two entries directly below) landed API-only in prior sessions this same day, verified end-to-end via curl/direct SQL but with zero web UI. This session builds that UI, mirroring the existing Quotes web UI (`apps/web/src/app/(app)/quotes/`) as closely as possible — it's the proven reference pattern in this codebase for a multi-line document with a builder, detail page, and list.
- **New `apps/web/src/app/(app)/invoices/` route** — `types.ts` (hand-written types mirroring `invoices`/`invoice_lines`' row shape, bigint money fields as strings per the established JSON convention), `invoice-builder.tsx` (contact picker, currency, type, issue/due dates, notes/terms, line editor — no "valid until", unlike quotes, since that's a quotes-only concept), `invoice-line-editor.tsx` (a close copy of `quote-line-editor.tsx`, same item/heading/text line kinds and `computeLinePreview` approach via the shared tax engine), `invoice-detail.tsx` (line breakdown, totals with a "Paid"/"Balance due" pair once anything's been paid — mirroring `InvoicePdfDocument`'s own presentation — status badge, live PDF preview/download, and action buttons for Edit/Issue/Void/Delete-draft/Record-payment), `invoices-table.tsx` (mirrors `quotes-table.tsx` **minus** the version-grouping logic — invoices have no `parentQuoteId`/versions, so every row stands alone), `invoices-explorer.tsx` (stat cards using the API's own computed `isOverdue`/`balanceDue` rather than reinventing that logic client-side, status filter including a synthetic "Overdue" option matching quotes' own synthetic "Expired" filter pattern, contact filter), `invoice-row-actions.tsx`, `invoices-skeleton.tsx`, `use-invoice-stats.ts`, `contact-picker.tsx`/`entity-toast.tsx` (verbatim duplicates of quotes' own, matching that file's own stated precedent), and `new`/`[id]`/`[id]/edit` page wrappers plus `invoice-edit-loader.tsx`.
- **Void requires a reason, enforced in the UI, not just the API.** `invoice-detail.tsx`'s Void button opens a `Dialog` with a required `Input` for the reason — the "Void invoice" submit button calls `POST /invoices/:id/void` with `{reason}` and shows a client-side error if the field is empty, since the task required this not be skippable. (Separately noted below: the API's own empty-reason rejection currently surfaces as a raw 500 rather than a clean 400 — a pre-existing zod-error-handling gap unrelated to this session's work, harmless here since the UI's own required-field check prevents an empty submission from ever reaching the API.)
- **New `record-payment-dialog.tsx`** (lives in `invoices/`, not a standalone payments page — see the scope note below) — S-06.1/S-06.2/S-06.3/S-06.4/S-06.5's web surface in one dialog. Takes a `contactId` and, when opened from an invoice's own "Record payment" button, a `presetInvoiceId`/`presetBalanceDue`. On open it fetches the contact's other outstanding issued invoices (`GET /invoices?contactId=&status=issued`) and, once an amount is entered, calls `GET /payments/suggest-allocation` for an oldest-due-first starting split (S-06.3) that the user can freely edit — add another invoice to the split, change any row's amount (clamped to that invoice's own balance), remove a row, or leave the whole amount unallocated as customer credit (S-06.4). If the suggested split (oldest-due-first) doesn't happen to include the specific invoice the dialog was opened from, that invoice is still given a spot in the editable table ahead of the raw suggestion — the whole point of clicking "Record payment" from an invoice's own detail page is to pay THAT invoice, so it shouldn't silently disappear behind an older invoice the user wasn't looking at. Submits via `POST /payments`; on success closes the dialog, shows a toast, and re-fetches the invoice detail page's own data so its `balanceDue`/`amountPaid` reflect the new payment immediately.
- **Deliberately did NOT build a standalone `/payments` list page.** The task brief's own instruction was to default to not building one unless clearly warranted; the invoice-triggered dialog plus the invoice detail page's own balance refresh covers the actually-requested flow (recording a payment against a specific invoice, or leaving part of it unallocated). The gap this leaves: S-06.4's "record a payment with no invoice yet" case has no dedicated entry point today (a payment can still end up fully unallocated by opening the dialog from an invoice and simply not allocating anything to it, but there's no natural jumping-off point when there's no invoice at all yet) — flagged in "Next up" as the reason a `/payments` page will eventually be worth adding.
- **`Invoices` registered in the sidebar** (`apps/web/src/lib/nav-sections.tsx`) under "Sales", after Quotes, using a `Receipt` icon (no collision with existing `FileText`/`Package`/`Target`/`Users`/`CalendarDays` icons already in use there).
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, and `build` all clean (`/invoices`, `/invoices/[id]`, `/invoices/[id]/edit`, `/invoices/new` all compile as dynamic routes). Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres (fresh isolated throwaway org/user via `/api/auth/sign-up/email`, left in place afterward for the usual `journal_lines.contact_id` `onDelete: restrict` reason — confirmed live that a delete attempt on the test contact is still correctly blocked): created a draft invoice via the exact payload shape `invoice-builder.tsx`'s `buildPayload()` sends (10 × $100.00 @ 8% tax → $1,000.00 subtotal / $80.00 tax / $1,080.00 total), issued it (`INV-0001`), called `GET /payments/suggest-allocation` and confirmed it returns the shape `record-payment-dialog.tsx` expects, recorded a $500.00 payment against it via the exact payload `RecordPaymentDialog`'s submit handler sends (`POST /payments` with one allocation) and confirmed via a fresh `GET /invoices/:id` that `amountPaid`/`balanceDue` updated correctly ($500.00 paid, $580.00 balance due), confirmed voiding that same now-partially-paid invoice correctly 400s ("Cannot void an invoice with payments allocated against it" — matching the detail page's own guard on when the Void button even appears), then created and issued a second, unpaid invoice (`INV-0002`) and voided it with a real reason end to end, confirming `status: "void"` and `voidReason` both landed correctly. Also fetched `GET /invoices/:id/pdf` and confirmed a valid single-page PDF comes back, and confirmed `GET /invoices` (the explorer's own list call) returns the shape `invoices-table.tsx` expects. Browser-automation tooling wasn't available in this environment (same limitation noted in prior Quotes sessions) — the actual on-screen rendering was confirmed via `curl` against the running Next.js dev server (`/invoices` returns a clean 200 with no error boundary in the RSC payload) and by reading the component tree against `@spine/ui`'s established conventions, not by looking at a rendered browser.
- Stories: S-05.9 (now surfaced in a UI), plus the web-UI halves of S-05.1/S-05.2/S-05.3/S-05.8 (invoices) and S-06.1/S-06.2/S-06.3/S-06.4/S-06.5 (payments) — no new story numbers, this session is UI-only against already-shipped API scope.

### 2026-08-09 — Payments Stage 1 (API only) — record + multi-invoice allocation, oldest-first suggestion, unallocated credit, DB-enforced allocation limits

- Second stage of Phase 2 · Money In, continuing straight on from Invoicing Stage 1 per the user's own sequencing choice. Scoped to the ledger-correctness core (S-06.1, S-06.2, S-06.3, S-06.4, S-06.5) — receipt PDF (S-06.6) and the receivables/AR-aging view (S-06.7) are their own follow-up stage, same split pattern Invoices used for its own PDF-vs-email staging.
- **New `payments`/`payment_allocations` tables** (`apps/api/src/db/schema/payments.ts`, migration `0019_outstanding_nocturne.sql`, applied to the dev DB). A payment is recorded against a *contact*, not directly against an invoice, per §7's own data model — required for S-06.4 (a payment with no invoice yet, held entirely as unallocated customer credit). `payments.allocatedAmount` is a denormalized running total; `unallocatedAmount` (`amount - allocatedAmount`) is what a partial or fully-unapplied payment's remaining credit is. A payment has no draft state, unlike quotes/invoices — S-06.1's own wording ("record a payment") is a single atomic action.
- **New DB-level allocation-limit trigger** (`apps/api/drizzle/0020_payment_allocation_limits.sql`, hand-authored in the same style as the pre-existing `0001_ledger_integrity_triggers.sql`, registered in `drizzle/meta/_journal.json`/`0020_snapshot.json` the same way `0001` was) — directly implements §6's own F6 acceptance highlight: *"allocations can never exceed either the payment amount or the invoice balance — enforced at the database level, not just the UI."* A per-row CHECK can't express this (it's a cross-row sum), so — exactly like `journal_lines`' own balance check — it's a `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint trigger that re-sums on every insert/update/delete and also keeps `payments.allocated_amount` in sync as a side effect, so the invariant holds even if a future caller ever writes `payment_allocations` directly rather than through `PaymentsService`.
- **New `PaymentsService`/`PaymentsController`** (`apps/api/src/modules/payments/`): `create()` validates each allocation against its invoice's live remaining balance and the payment's own total before writing (app-level check ahead of the DB trigger, for a clearer error message), writes the allocation rows, bumps each allocated invoice's `amountPaid`, and posts ONE balanced journal entry per payment covering both applicable §7.2 rules at once — "Payment received" (debit Bank/Cash, credit AR) for the allocated portion, "Advance received" (debit Bank/Cash, credit Customer Advances) for any unallocated remainder — reasoning that the cash received is one real-world event, not two. `suggestAllocation()` (S-06.3) is a pure read: sorts a contact's outstanding issued invoices oldest-`dueDate`-first (nulls last) and greedily fills each until the payment amount runs out, returned as a starting point for the caller to edit before actually submitting `create()`. `delete()` only allows removing a payment with zero allocations (posts a reversing entry via the existing `LedgerService.reverseEntry`); an allocated payment is permanent, matching CLAUDE.md's posture on posted financial events. `bankAccountId` is an optional per-request override (mirrors `InvoicesService.issue`'s own `revenueAccountId` pattern), defaulting to the seeded "Bank Account" (code 1010).
- `AuditEntityType` gained `"payment"`.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/shared build`, `typecheck`+`lint`+`build` all clean across `@spine/api`; `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck` unaffected (no web changes this stage either). `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` passes, including the ledger-integrity test's `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE`, which reaches the new tables transitively via `organization`'s own cascade. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres (fresh isolated throwaway org/contact/two invoices — $500 due 2026-08-15 and $300 due 2026-09-15 — cleaned up after except the org/contact/journal rows themselves, left in place for the same `onDelete: restrict` reason noted in the Invoicing Stage 1 entry below): `GET /payments/suggest-allocation` for a $900 payment correctly proposed the earlier-due invoice first, then the later one, with $100 left unallocated; submitting that exact payment (`POST /payments`) returned `RCP-0001` with `allocatedAmount: 80000`/`unallocatedAmount: 10000`, both invoices' `amountPaid`/`balanceDue` updated correctly (confirmed via a fresh `GET /invoices/:id`, not just the payment response), and the posted journal entry balanced across all three legs (Bank $900 debit = AR $800 + Customer Advances $100 credit, confirmed via direct SQL). Confirmed the app-level over-allocation guard 400s on a follow-up over-limit request, then confirmed the **DB-level trigger independently** by bypassing the API entirely — a raw SQL transaction inserting a `payment_allocations` row that would push an already-fully-paid invoice over its own total was rejected at `COMMIT` with the trigger's own error message, and the transaction left zero rows behind (confirmed via a follow-up `SELECT`). Recorded a separate pure-unallocated $50 advance (S-06.4), confirmed it deletes cleanly with a correctly-linked reversing journal entry, and confirmed attempting to delete the earlier *allocated* `RCP-0001` correctly 400s.
- Stories: S-06.1, S-06.2, S-06.3, S-06.4, S-06.5.

### 2026-08-09 — Phase 2 kickoff: Invoicing Stage 1 (API only) — draft CRUD, issue with real ledger posting, void with reversal, PDF

- First work on Phase 2 · Money In (`docs/spine-prd.md` §12: S-05.1's scratch path, S-05.2, S-05.3, S-05.8). Scoped, with the user's agreement, to "Invoices core first" — data model, draft/issue/void, numbering, PDF, and journal postings — before Payments, advances, credit notes, or a web UI, mirroring how Quotes Stage 1 also shipped API-only before its web builder followed.
- **New `invoices`/`invoice_lines` tables** (`apps/api/src/db/schema/invoices.ts`, migration `0018_mature_phil_sheldon.sql`, applied to the dev DB) — structurally a close mirror of `quotes`/`quote_lines` (same snapshot-on-write convention, same cascade-delete + full-replace lines, same draft-is-mutable/issued-is-immutable shape) with invoice-specific additions: `status` includes a terminal `void` (not just draft/issued), `type` (`standard`/`advance`/`progress`) and `sourceQuoteId`/`jobId`/`milestoneId` are present now as nullable scaffolding for S-03.10/S-05.4-5/Phase 3 even though nothing sets them yet, `retentionAmount` and `amountPaid` are present for the same forward-compatibility reason, and `journalEntryId`/`voidJournalEntryId` point at the two `journal_entries` rows an invoice's lifecycle can produce.
- **New `LedgerService`** (`apps/api/src/modules/ledger/ledger.service.ts`) — the first domain-facing journal-posting code in the app; previously `journal_entries`/`journal_lines` existed only as schema plus a DB-trigger integrity test with hand-inserted rows. `resolveSystemAccount(s)` looks up an org's seeded system accounts (AR, Retention Receivable, Input/Output Tax, AP, Customer Advances) by their well-known chart-of-accounts codes; `postEntry` writes a balanced entry (with its own app-level balance check ahead of the DB's deferred constraint trigger, for a clearer error message) inside the caller's own transaction, never its own; `reverseEntry` posts a debit/credit-swapped mirror of an existing entry and links it via `reversedById`, implementing CLAUDE.md's "corrections are reversing entries, never edits" for the first time in this codebase. Every future domain module (Payments, Purchases, Expenses) is meant to post through this same service, per CLAUDE.md's "posting rules live in one module."
- **New `InvoicesService`/`InvoicesController`** (`apps/api/src/modules/invoices/`) — CRUD on drafts (full-replace lines, same server-computed-totals-only convention as `QuotesService`, duplicated rather than shared per each module's own doc comment on why), `POST /invoices/:id/issue` (allocates the gapless number via the existing `NumberingService`, flips draft→issued, and posts the §7.2 "Invoice issued" entry — debit AR, credit Revenue + Output Tax Payable — inside the same transaction as the status flip, so a posting failure rolls back the whole issue), and `POST /invoices/:id/void` (S-05.8: requires a `reason`, blocked if `amountPaid > 0` since Stage 1 has no payment-unwind flow yet, posts a reversing entry via `LedgerService.reverseEntry`). Revenue posts to a single blended account per invoice (defaults to seeded code 4100 "Sales — Services", overridable via an optional `revenueAccountId` in the issue request body) rather than splitting by each line's own catalog-item income account — a documented Stage 1 simplification, see "Next up".
- **New `InvoicePdfService`/`InvoicePdfDocument`** (`apps/api/src/modules/pdf/invoice-pdf.template.tsx`) — same `@react-pdf/renderer` approach and logo-as-data-URI fetch (with graceful degradation) as `QuotePdfService`, adapted for invoice fields (due date instead of "valid until", a highlighted balance-due row when `amountPaid < totalAmount`).
- `AuditEntityType` gained `"invoice"`/`"invoice_line"` (API-side only so far — the web copy in `contacts/types.ts` has no invoice history view to wire up yet, since there's no invoice web UI in this stage).
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/shared build`, `typecheck`+`lint`+`build` all clean across `@spine/api`; `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck` unaffected (no web changes this stage). `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` passes, including the pre-existing ledger-integrity test, whose `TRUNCATE ... CASCADE` now also covers the new tables cleanly. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres (isolated throwaway org/contact, cleaned up after except the org/contact/journal rows themselves, which `journal_lines.contact_id`'s `onDelete: restrict` correctly refused to let be deleted — left in place, harmless, in a never-reused org, matching this project's own established practice for this exact situation): created a draft invoice (10 × $100.00 @ 8% tax → $1,000.00 subtotal / $80.00 tax / $1,080.00 total), issued it (number `INV-0001`, journal entry posted and confirmed balanced via direct SQL: debit AR $1,080.00 = credit Sales $1,000.00 + Output Tax $80.00), confirmed editing an issued invoice and voiding an already-void invoice both correctly return 400, then voided it with a reason (reversing entry posted, linked via `reversedById`, confirmed the AR account's net balance across both entries is exactly $0.00), and confirmed the PDF endpoint returns a valid single-page PDF.
- Stories: S-05.1 (scratch-create path only — from-quote/from-milestone paths are S-03.10/S-04.5, not yet built), S-05.2, S-05.3, S-05.8.

### 2026-08-09 — Fix long-flagged issues: `isCompany` partial-update bug; confirmed Catalogue delete is correct as-is

- Worked through the two outstanding items flagged in "Next up" from prior sessions.
- **Fixed**: `updateContactSchema` was `createContactSchema.partial()`, so `isCompany`'s `.default(false)` still fired on parse even when the field was entirely absent from a `PUT /contacts/:id` body — silently resetting a company contact back to "individual" on any partial update that didn't happen to include `isCompany` (e.g. a logo-only or notes-only save). `contacts.service.ts`'s own `input.isCompany !== undefined` gate was already correct; the schema defeated it upstream by never letting `isCompany` actually be `undefined`. Fixed by giving `updateContactSchema` (`packages/shared/src/schemas/contacts.ts`) its own explicit shape — every field `.optional()`, none `.default()`d — instead of deriving it from the create schema. `createContactSchema` itself is untouched, so create-time defaulting still works.
- **Reviewed, not a bug**: Catalogue's unguarded hard-delete was flagged speculatively before Quotes existed. Quotes now exists, and `quote_lines.catalogItemId` is a real FK — but `onDelete: set null` was the deliberate original design (provenance-only; every line snapshots its own name/price/tax and never re-reads the catalog item). Confirmed live that deleting a referenced catalog item correctly leaves quote lines intact with `catalogItemId` nulled, matching the intended behavior; no guard needed or added.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/shared build`/`typecheck`/`lint`, `@spine/api typecheck` all clean. Live end-to-end against a real local dev Postgres using isolated throwaway test orgs (cleaned up after, except one leftover unarchived contact in a never-reused throwaway org — harmless, doesn't touch real dev data): reproduced the exact pre-fix bug first (`PUT /contacts/:id` with only `notes` set would have flipped `isCompany` to `false`), confirmed the fix holds (`isCompany: true` survives the same partial update), and confirmed create-time defaulting is unaffected (`POST /contacts` with no `isCompany` still yields `false`). Separately verified the catalog-item delete-while-referenced scenario end-to-end via curl.
- Stories: none (bug fix / verification, no new scope).

### 2026-08-09 — MiniCalendar: put the collapse icon on the same row as the month nav

- Follow-up to the toggle-placement fix below — the icon was inside the mini-calendar panel, but on its own row above the month header rather than sharing it with the `< >`/"Aug 2026" row, per a reference screenshot of the target layout (arrows + month grouped left, action icon pushed far right, single row).
- `MiniCalendar` (`@spine/ui`) gained an optional `headerAction?: ReactNode` prop, rendered at the end of its existing month-nav header row — and that row's layout changed from three evenly `justify-between`-spaced items (prev, label, next) to two groups (`prev + next + label` on the left, `headerAction` pushed right via the row's own `justify-between`), so the arrows and label now sit tightly together the way the reference shows. Fully backward compatible — every other `MiniCalendar` usage (`DatePicker`, `Calendar`'s own toolbar popover) omits the prop and renders unchanged. Documented in `apps/design-system/src/app/components/calendar/page.tsx` with a new `MiniCalendar` API subsection (it had no dedicated one before, despite being an exported `@spine/ui` component) per CLAUDE.md's docs-drift rule.
- `/meetings`'s sidebar now passes the collapse button as `MiniCalendar`'s `headerAction` instead of wrapping `MiniCalendar` in an extra header row of its own.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`/`lint`, `@spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, `@spine/design-system typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean. Live via Playwright: the collapse icon now renders on the same row as the prev/next arrows and "August 2026" label, at the far right, matching the reference layout.
- Stories: S-01.9 (UI polish, no new story).

### 2026-08-09 — Meetings sidebar: move the collapse toggle into the mini-calendar header

- User didn't like the collapse button's placement (a standalone button floating to the left of the whole calendar, outside both the toolbar and the sidebar). Moved it to match the reference screenshot exactly — inside the mini-calendar panel's own header, top-right, next to where the month label sits.
- Collapsing the sidebar removes that button from view along with the rest of the panel, so a re-expand affordance still has to exist somewhere: when collapsed, the `aside` shrinks to a thin `w-10` vertical strip (not fully removed from the layout) containing just the same toggle icon, docked to the right edge in roughly the same spot — not moved to the main Calendar's own toolbar, which would've been more consistently placed but wouldn't match the reference layout.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean. Live via Playwright: the icon renders inside the mini-calendar's header when expanded, clicking it collapses to the thin strip (calendar grid reflows to fill the freed width), and clicking the strip's icon re-expands back to the full panel.
