### 2026-08-09 — S-01.9 follow-up: Odoo-style multi-calendar sidebar, activity overlay, collapsible rail

- User asked for a right-side sidebar closer to Odoo's calendar: multiple toggleable "calendars," a checkbox to overlay activities, and a collapsible panel. This app has no external calendar sync, so "multiple calendars" was grounded in real data rather than fabricated: each org member becomes a toggleable calendar (real `GET /org/members` + each meeting's existing `organizerUserId`), not a placeholder concept.
- **`Calendar` (`@spine/ui`) gained `readOnlyEventIds`/`onReadOnlyEventClick`** — events whose id is in the set skip drag/resize entirely and route clicks to the caller's own handler instead of the built-in editor dialog. Required real internal-architecture changes, not just a page-level wrapper: `handleEventClick` now checks the set before opening the editor; `TimeGridEvent` (the Day/Week draggable block) takes a new `isReadOnly` flag that swaps its move/resize pointer handlers for a `stopPropagation`-only handler (so a click doesn't fall through and start a slot-create drag underneath) and hides the resize handles; `onEventUpdate` is also guarded centrally as defense in depth. Month/Agenda views needed no changes — they have no drag, only the already-centralized click path. `CalendarEventInstance` is now exported from `@spine/ui` (previously internal-only) since callers need its type for `onReadOnlyEventClick`. Design-system docs updated: API tables, a feature bullet, and a new live "Read-only overlay" example — per CLAUDE.md's docs-drift rule.
- **`/meetings` sidebar rewrite** (`meetings-calendar.tsx`): a `Calendars` `Collapsible` section lists every org member with an avatar + checkbox (real names/ids from `GET /org/members`, matched against each meeting's `organizerUserId` — unchecking one filters it out of `events` client-side); a top-level checkbox toggles all members at once. A `My Activities` checkbox fetches `GET /activities` and overlays every activity with a `dueAt` as a 30-minute placeholder block in a neutral `slate` color (activities have no real end time, so this is a deliberately short, visually distinct marker, not a fabricated duration) using the new `readOnlyEventIds` mechanism — clicking one opens a small read-only `Dialog` (type, notes, due time) instead of the meeting editor, and it can't be dragged. All three toggle states (sidebar collapsed, hidden organizers, activities visible) persist to `localStorage`, matching the sidebar-collapse pattern already used by `app-layout.tsx`. The whole right rail collapses via a `PanelRightClose`/`PanelRightOpen` button (same icon pair the main app sidebar already uses for its own collapse, for visual consistency), independent of the `Calendars` sub-section's own collapse.
- **Fixed a pre-existing hydration bug surfaced while testing this**: `useState(new Date())` seeded the calendar's `date` at module-init time, which could render server-side with a `new Date()` several days stale relative to the client's real clock in a long-running dev session (confirmed via the HTTP `Date` response header being correct while the rendered week label wasn't — a Next.js dev-mode staleness issue, not app logic, since `toolbarLabel`'s week-range math is a pure function of `date`). Fixed structurally regardless of root cause: the page now renders a neutral loading placeholder until a `hydrated` mount effect re-seeds `date` with a client-computed `new Date()`, so SSR never emits date-derived text that could mismatch the client. Also replaced the activity-detail popup's hand-rolled backdrop `<div>` (found not to close on Escape, blocking further interaction) with the real `Dialog` component, inheriting Radix's focus trap and Escape-to-close for free.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`/`lint`, `@spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, `@spine/design-system typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean. Live against a fresh isolated test org (a contact, a meeting, and an activity with a `dueAt`, cleaned up after): confirmed via Playwright the toolbar's week label now matches the mini-calendar's highlighted day (no hydration mismatch), the Calendars section lists the test org's member with a working per-member checkbox, toggling "My Activities" shows/hides the activity marker in a visually distinct style from the real meeting, clicking the activity marker opens the read-only Dialog (title/due time) and Escape closes it, and the whole sidebar (plus the Calendars sub-section independently) collapses/expands correctly.
- Stories: S-01.9 (follow-up polish, no new story).

### 2026-08-08 — S-01.9: meeting scheduling — new `/meetings` calendar page + contact linkage

- Not in the original PRD — user asked for a dedicated meeting-scheduling page similar to a reference Outlook/Google-Calendar-style screenshot, with contacts linkable to meetings and those meetings surfacing on the contact's own Timeline tab. Confirmed nothing like this existed in `docs/spine-prd.md` (only `activities`' own flat `meeting` type — a to-do-style log entry with a single `dueAt`, not a scheduled time range), so this was added as **S-01.9** to FEAT-01 · Contacts & CRM (full acceptance-highlights text in the PRD) before being built, per this repo's established practice for new scope (same as S-10.12's addition earlier in the project). Google/Outlook calendar sync (shown in the reference screenshot) was explicitly scoped **out of v1** — self-hosted only, no OAuth/external API dependency — and left as a candidate follow-up story.
- **New `meetings` table** (`apps/api/src/db/schema/meetings.ts`, migration `0017_cultured_jackal.sql`, applied to the dev DB): `id, orgId, contactId (nullable, onDelete: set null), title, startAt, endAt, allDay, color, description, location, recurrence (jsonb), organizerUserId, createdAt, updatedAt`. Deliberately a separate table from `activities`, not folded in — a real calendar event needs a time range and optional recurrence that `activities`' flat `dueAt` was never designed to hold; `activities`' own "meeting" type is untouched. `contactId` uses `set null` (not `activities`' `cascade`) since a meeting has its own independent existence — deleting the contact shouldn't destroy the meeting record, unlike an activity whose whole purpose is being about that contact.
- **New API module pieces** in the existing `ContactsModule` (`meetings.service.ts`/`meetings.controller.ts`): standard owner+staff-mutate CRUD at `/meetings`, `GET /meetings` supports both `?contactId=` and `?from=&to=` (range-overlap) filters, full `AuditLogService` write-through (new `"meeting"` `AuditEntityType`, mirrored into `apps/web`'s own copy of that union + `HistorySection`'s label map). New `packages/shared/src/schemas/meetings.ts` (`createMeetingSchema`/`updateMeetingSchema`, `endAt >= startAt` refined) — required a `pnpm --filter @spine/shared build` since it's a built package, not source-consumed like `@spine/ui`.
- **The existing (previously unused anywhere in `apps/web`) `Calendar` component gained a `contactOptions` prop** (`packages/ui/src/components/calendar.tsx`) — when provided, its built-in event editor dialog shows an optional Contact field (a `Combobox`) between Title and the All-day toggle, round-tripping the pick through a new optional `CalendarEvent.contactId` (`packages/ui/src/lib/calendar-utils.ts`). Fully backward compatible — omit `contactOptions` and the field simply doesn't render, so every other hypothetical `Calendar` caller is unaffected. Design-system docs updated in the same change (`apps/design-system/src/app/components/calendar/page.tsx`): API tables, a feature-list bullet, and a new live "Linked to a contact" example — per CLAUDE.md's docs-drift rule.
- **New `/meetings` page** (`apps/web/src/app/(app)/meetings/`): composes `Calendar` (now with `contactOptions` wired from the org's contact list) with a persistent right-side rail — `MiniCalendar` (already existed, previously only used inside `Calendar`'s own toolbar popover) plus a "Meetings by contact" summary list — matching the reference screenshot's always-visible mini-calendar rather than Calendar's own click-to-open popover version. Full width, same `FULL_WIDTH_ROUTES` opt-in mechanism the Pipeline board uses (`app-layout.tsx`), since a scheduling calendar is wide-by-nature content. Meetings are fetched for a rolling ±2-month window around the visible date (re-fetched only when the date navigates outside that window) rather than computing each view's exact visible range, which would have meant duplicating logic private to `calendar.tsx`.
- **Contact detail Timeline tab** (`timeline-section.tsx`, from the S-01.3 entry below) now merges a third source: `GET /meetings?contactId=`, each rendered as "Meeting: {title}" with the meeting's `startAt` (when it happens/happened) as its timeline timestamp — not `createdAt` (when it was scheduled) — matching how quote events already use their real event times rather than log-write times.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/shared build`, and `typecheck`+`lint` clean across `@spine/shared`/`@spine/api`/`@spine/ui`/`@spine/web`/`@spine/design-system`; production `build` clean across `@spine/api`/`@spine/web`/`@spine/design-system` (`/meetings` and `/components/calendar` both compile). Migration applied to the real local dev Postgres. Live end-to-end against a fresh isolated test org (a contact + a meeting created via the API, cleaned up after): confirmed via curl that create/update/contactId-filter/range-filter/delete all work and write correct audit-log rows (including a real before/after field diff on update); confirmed via Playwright that the `/meetings` page renders matching the reference layout (toolbar, week grid, persistent mini-calendar, "Meetings by contact" list), that clicking an event opens the editor with the Contact field correctly pre-filled to the linked contact's name, and that the same meeting appears on that contact's Timeline tab with its location and a calendar icon.
- Stories: S-01.9 (new).

### 2026-08-08 — S-01.3: per-contact Timeline tab (quotes + activities merged)

- Last open story in FEAT-01 · Contacts & CRM. The PRD's literal wording is "every quote, invoice, payment and note for a contact on one timeline" — invoices and payments don't exist yet (Phase 2), so this builds the real half: quote lifecycle events (created/sent/accepted/rejected) merged with logged activities (call/meeting/note/task, which already cover "note") into one chronological feed. Structured so invoice/payment events slot in as a third event source once those modules land, not reworked from scratch — the merge point is a flat, pre-sorted array the generic Timeline component just renders.
- **New `@spine/ui` component**: `packages/ui/src/components/timeline.tsx` exports `Timeline` + `TimelineItemData` — a vertical connected-rail feed (icon + title + description + timestamp per item, tone-colored dot for success/critical events using the existing `paid`/`overdue` tokens) that's domain-agnostic, same reusability rationale as the `Kanban` component: it doesn't know what a "quote" is, only how to lay out a caller-supplied, caller-sorted list of items. Documented at `apps/design-system/src/app/components/timeline/page.tsx` (added to `nav.ts` under Data display) with a live demo, feature list, anatomy, API reference, and a real-usage code example, per CLAUDE.md's docs-drift rule.
- **New `apps/web/src/app/(app)/contacts/timeline-section.tsx`**: fetches `GET /quotes?contactId=` and `GET /activities?contactId=` (both pre-existing endpoints, no new API surface) and derives timeline events client-side from each quote's own `createdAt`/`sentAt`/`updatedAt`-on-terminal-status fields — not from `quote_events` (that table is scoped to the public accept/reject link's view tracking and has no per-contact read endpoint, so using the quote's own fields avoids inventing a new backend surface for data the frontend can already derive). Wired into `contact-detail.tsx` as a new "Timeline" tab, made the default (first) tab ahead of "Contact persons", since PRD's own framing ("so I know where we stand before a call") makes it the primary view now that there's real data to show.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`/`lint`, `@spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, `@spine/design-system typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (`/components/timeline` prerenders alongside every other component doc page). Exercised live against a real local dev Postgres using an isolated throwaway org/contact (a draft quote created then sent, two activities — a note and a call — cleaned up after, archived rather than hard-deleted since the delete guard correctly blocked removing a contact with quote history, confirming that pre-existing guard still works): the Timeline tab renders as the default tab, correctly shows "Quote QUO-0001 sent" and "Quote QUO-0001 created" (both real timestamps) interleaved with the note and call, newest-first. The accepted/rejected tone rendering was verified via the design-system docs page's live demo rather than a full public-token accept flow (no Mailhog/mail-catcher running in this environment to retrieve the real signed token a customer would use) — the tone-mapping code itself is a direct, verified read of `quote.status`, so this is a presentation-only gap, not an unverified code path.
- Stories: S-01.3. **All of FEAT-01 · Contacts & CRM (S-01.1 → S-01.8) is now complete.**

### 2026-08-08 — Kanban column minWidth raised 200px → 275px

- User asked for a wider column floor. Changed `DEFAULT_MIN_WIDTH` in `packages/ui/src/components/kanban.tsx` from 200 to 275 (still overridable per-column via the existing `minWidth` prop, or bypassed entirely via `width` for a fixed non-flexing column) and updated the matching default in the design-system Kanban docs page's feature list and API table.
- Practical effect on the leads pipeline specifically: at a standard ~1440px laptop viewport, 5 columns × 275px + gaps now slightly exceeds the available width, so the board falls back to its horizontal scroll (previously fit at 200px) — this is the expected, correct behavior per the flex-then-scroll design from the fix below, not a regression; wider monitors (≳1500px content width) still fit all 5 without scrolling.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`/`lint`, `@spine/web typecheck`, `@spine/design-system typecheck`/`lint` all clean. Live against a fresh isolated test org (5 leads across all 5 stages, cleaned up after): confirmed via Playwright that each column measures exactly 275px at a 1440px viewport, the board correctly scrolls horizontally to reveal the Lost column, and the page itself still doesn't scroll vertically while doing so (`document.documentElement.scrollHeight === window.innerHeight` held throughout).
- Stories: S-01.5 (styling tweak, no new story).

### 2026-08-08 — Fix: Lost column was pushed off-screen; Pipeline page now full width

- User reported the Lost column wasn't visible on the pipeline board. Root cause: `KanbanColumn` (from the redesign below) always used a fixed 272px width, and 5 columns × 272px + gaps reliably exceeded the ~1136px of content width `AppShellContent`'s 1200px reading-width cap left after the sidebar and page padding — Lost was rendering, just scrolled off the right edge of `KanbanBoard`'s `overflow-x-auto` row with only a thin native scrollbar as the (easy to miss) hint that more columns existed.
- **`KanbanColumn` (`@spine/ui`)** now defaults to flexible width (`flex: 1 1 0%`, floored at a new `minWidth` prop, default 200px) instead of a fixed 272px — columns spread out to fill whatever width the board is given. `width` is kept as an explicit opt-out for callers who want a fixed, non-flexing column width instead. Design-system docs (`apps/design-system/src/app/components/kanban/page.tsx`) updated: feature list, API table, and a new "Sizing" paragraph covering the width side of the existing height-sizing guidance, plus the live demo no longer passes a fixed `width` so it matches the new default.
- **`AppShellContent` (`@spine/ui`)** gains a `fullWidth?: boolean` prop (default `false`, preserving every existing page's behavior) that swaps its wrapper's `max-w-[1200px]` for `max-w-none` — for pages whose content is wide by nature (a multi-column board) rather than form/prose, where the cap just wastes space. Documented in `apps/design-system/src/app/components/app-shell/page.tsx`'s feature list and API table per CLAUDE.md's docs-drift rule.
- **`apps/web/src/components/app-layout.tsx`** (the one place across the whole app that renders `AppShellContent`, wrapping every authenticated page) now computes `fullWidth` from a small `FULL_WIDTH_ROUTES` pathname allowlist (currently just `/leads`) using the same `usePathname()` + prefix-match already used for sidebar nav active-state — an explicit per-route opt-in, not a global change, so every other page (Contacts, Settings, Quotes, etc.) keeps its existing 1200px cap unchanged. The page header (`AppShellHeader`) benefits automatically since it renders inside the same now-uncapped wrapper as the board — no separate change needed for "the title should be full width too."
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`/`lint`, `@spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, `@spine/design-system typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean. Live against a fresh isolated test org (5 leads across all 5 stages, cleaned up after), via Playwright at two widths: at 1440px, `board.scrollWidth === board.clientWidth` (no overflow) and all 5 columns including Lost visible with no scrolling; at 1920px, the header/toolbar/board all visibly use the full available width edge-to-edge. Confirmed the scoping is correct by also screenshotting `/contacts` at 1920px in the same session — it still renders capped to 1200px, unaffected.
- Stories: S-01.5 (layout fix, no new story).

### 2026-08-08 — Lead dialog: Source is now a creatable Combobox, not plain text

- `lead-dialog.tsx` now takes an additional `leads` prop (the board's already-loaded full list, passed down from `leads-board.tsx` — no new fetch) and derives `sourceOptions` as the sorted, deduplicated set of every distinct `source` value already used across the org's leads, plus the current lead's own value if it isn't already in that set (covers editing a lead whose source no other lead shares). The Source field is now `<Combobox options={sourceOptions} value={source} onValueChange={setSource} allowCreate />` — pick a previously-used value, or type a new one and select "Create '…'" to use it.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint` clean. Live against a fresh isolated test org (2 leads seeded with sources "Referral"/"Trade show", cleaned up after): opening the edit dialog showed the Source trigger displaying "Referral" (not blank), opening it listed both existing sources with "Referral" checked, typing "Google Ads" (not in the list) showed a "Create 'Google Ads'" row, and selecting it + Save changes persisted — confirmed via a fresh `GET /leads` showing `"source": "Google Ads"` on that lead afterward.

Currency was also a raw 3-letter free-text `Input` defaulting to a hardcoded `"USD"`, ignoring the org's actual configured default (`GET /org/settings/currency`, §S-10.1/S-12.4).

On create (not edit — an existing lead keeps its stored currency), the dialog now fetches `GET /org/settings/currency` and pre-selects the org's default instead of always starting at USD.

### 2026-08-08 — Leads pipeline board redesign: reusable Kanban primitive + search/filter/sort/view settings

- Follow-up to the same day's initial S-01.5 board (below): the user asked for the board to match a reference kanban screenshot's toolbar (search, filter, sort, view-settings) and interaction details (board itself never scrolls, only cards do; a dedicated "Add lead" affordance; clicking a card opens an edit dialog; the Lost column visually distinct), plus for the kanban mechanics themselves to become a proper `@spine/ui` component with design-system docs rather than living only in `apps/web`.
- **New `@spine/ui` component**: `packages/ui/src/components/kanban.tsx` exports `KanbanBoard`/`KanbanColumn`/`KanbanCard` — domain-agnostic (no leads/stage knowledge), built on native HTML5 drag-and-drop (no new dependency), each column independently scrollable via the existing `ScrollArea` while the board's own height is fixed (fills its parent via `h-full`, doesn't compute a viewport height itself — the composing page owns that). Documented at `apps/design-system/src/app/components/kanban/page.tsx` (added to `nav.ts` under Data display) with a live interactive demo, feature list, anatomy, a "Sizing" section explaining the `h-full`-needs-a-sized-ancestor caveat, API reference, and a real-usage code sample lifted from the actual leads page — per CLAUDE.md's rule that a new/changed `@spine/ui` component always ships its docs page in the same change.

The Lost column gets `border-overdue bg-overdue-bg` (reusing the existing financial-status color tokens, not `StatusChip` itself, for a "closed-loss" visual cue) instead of the neutral column styling every other stage gets.
- **New `lead-dialog.tsx`**: clicking any card opens a single create/edit `Dialog` (Issue-4 "one dialog, presence of `lead` means edit" pattern, matching `contacts/leads-section.tsx`'s existing dialog) — unlike that per-contact dialog, this one also has a `Combobox` contact picker since the board spans every contact in the org, and adds a delete action behind an `AlertDialog` confirmation.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`/`lint`, `@spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, and `@spine/design-system typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean (`/components/kanban` prerenders alongside all 71 other component doc pages). Exercised live end-to-end with a second isolated throwaway org/2 contacts/many leads (cleaned up afterward): Playwright screenshots confirm the toolbar renders and functions (search/filter inputs, sort menu opens with the correct radio-checked state, view-settings checkboxes toggle), clicking a card opens the edit dialog pre-filled correctly, the Lost column renders with the distinct red tint, and — the specific "board doesn't scroll" requirement — stacking 13 cards into one column left `document.documentElement.scrollHeight === window.innerHeight` (the page itself never grew), with only that column's own list scrolling; drag-and-drop between columns (via the new `KanbanColumn.onDrop`) still moves a card, updates both columns' counts/totals, and persists server-side via the existing `PUT /leads/:id` (confirmed via a fresh `GET /leads` after the browser interaction, not just browser-side state).

### 2026-08-08 — S-01.5: leads pipeline board (org-wide kanban by stage)

Previously only a flat, per-contact leads list existed (`contacts/leads-section.tsx`); this adds the org-wide kanban view the PRD story actually asks for — every lead across every contact, grouped into one column per stage, each column showing its lead count and a running value total (grouped by currency, in case leads mix currencies).

No new API endpoint — `GET /leads` (unfiltered) already returned every lead in the org; the board fetches that plus `GET /contacts` once and groups/joins client-side, the same "load once, derive in memory" pattern `ContactsExplorer` already uses for its stat cards.
- Drag-and-drop moves a card between stage columns via `PUT /leads/:id`, using native HTML5 drag-and-drop (`draggable`/`onDragStart`/`onDrop`) rather than a new dependency — the existing per-contact edit dialog remains the non-drag way to change a lead's stage. Moves are optimistic (UI updates immediately) and roll back to a fresh fetch on failure.
- Updated the now-stale "board is deferred" comments in `contacts/leads-section.tsx` and `leads.service.ts` to point at the new board instead.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean. Exercised live against the real local dev Postgres using an isolated throwaway org/contact/leads (signed up fresh, created 5 leads spanning all 5 stages, deleted all of it afterward — never touched the pre-existing dev org/user). Confirmed via Playwright screenshots: all 5 columns render with correct per-column counts/totals; dragging a card from one column to another visually moves it, updates both columns' counts/totals, and persists (re-fetched `GET /leads` afterward and confirmed the new `stage` server-side, independent of the browser state). Note: an earlier `pnpm build` in this session had overwritten the running dev server's `.next` directory, breaking its dev-mode asset manifest — caught via 404s on `_next/static` chunks during the first screenshot attempt, fixed by clearing `.next` and restarting `next dev` cleanly; unrelated to the new code itself but worth remembering (`build` and `dev` must not share a live `.next` directory).

### 2026-08-08 — Fix apps/design-system drift: Table's row-grouping wasn't documented

- User noticed the Table component's docs page had no mention of the `groupBy`/`groupPrimary`/`groupMemberLabel` grouping feature added during the Quotes session (used for quote version grouping) — a real violation of CLAUDE.md's rule that `apps/design-system` must never drift behind `packages/ui`'s actual exports.
- Added the three props to Table's `TableColumn<T>`/`TableProps<T>` API reference tables, a Features-list bullet, and a new "Grouped rows" example under Examples using a realistic quote-versions dataset (mirroring `apps/web/src/app/quotes/quotes-table.tsx`'s actual usage — same `groupPrimary` "newest version wins" logic, same `groupMemberLabel` version badge) so the docs page demonstrates the real intended usage, not a synthetic one.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck`/`lint` pass; a full clean-cache `next build` succeeds (an initial build attempt failed on unrelated stale `.next` cache artifacts for `/500`, `/components/empty-state`, `/components/toast` — confirmed unrelated to this change by clearing `.next` and rebuilding clean, which succeeded end-to-end including `/components/table`).

### 2026-08-08 — S-01.8: import contacts from CSV

- Last remaining Phase 1 story per the PRD's own list (`S-01.1 → S-01.8`) — a 3-step wizard (upload → map columns → preview & confirm), not a fixed-header-template approach, since real CSV exports from other CRMs/spreadsheets rarely share one header convention. Column mapping is auto-guessed by loose header-name matching (case/punctuation-insensitive aliases) but always shown and overridable before any row is touched.
- **API**: `POST /contacts/import` (`ContactsController`) accepts an array of already-validated `CreateContactInput` rows (capped at 500 — see below) and `ContactsService.importMany()` loops them through the existing single-contact `create()` path sequentially, one per row — same duplicate-detection (matching phone/email, PRD §9's "warns but does not block"), same audit-log write, same transaction shape as a manual create, with no parallel bulk-insert logic to keep in sync. One row's failure doesn't abort the batch; each is reported independently.
- **Web**: `apps/web/src/app/contacts/import/` — `build-rows.ts` (pure CSV-row → validated-row transform, kept outside React for testability), `import-contacts-dialog.tsx` (the 3-step wizard, `papaparse` for parsing), wired into `contacts-explorer.tsx` via a new "Import CSV" button.
- **A code-review pass before shipping caught six real issues**, all fixed:
  1. **Duplicate detection missed archived contacts.** The dialog was reusing the page's own filtered `contacts` state (default excludes archived) for client-side duplicate matching — an archived contact with a matching email would never be flagged, silently re-creating it as a new active contact. Fixed by fetching a dedicated `?includeArchived=true` list specifically when the import dialog opens, not reusing the page's filtered view.
  2. **Server-side duplicate warnings were discarded.** `create()` already returns `duplicateWarning` (used by the single-contact create drawer), but the import wizard's results screen never read it from the per-row response — a duplicate the client-side check missed (e.g. against a contact created by an earlier row in the same batch) would be silently created with no indication anywhere. Fixed: the results screen now shows a "created, but possible duplicates" section per row.
  3. **A failed import request had no safe retry path.** `apiClient` mints a fresh idempotency key per call, and `importMany` doesn't roll back on a later row's failure — a network drop or timeout after the server had already committed some rows gave the user no way to know what succeeded before clicking "Import" again, risking duplicate creation. Fixed: the error path now re-fetches contacts and recomputes duplicate flags before returning to the preview screen, so anything that already landed shows up flagged rather than silently re-created.
  4. **No client-side email format validation.** A malformed email (e.g. "not-an-email") passed the preview screen as "Ready," then failed the server's `z.string().email()` for the *entire batch* at once (the whole `rows` array is validated atomically before any row runs) — one bad email blocked every other valid row with a generic error. Fixed by validating email format client-side in `build-rows.ts`, so it's caught and flagged per-row before submit.
  5. **Auto-mapping collision: a column named "Company" silently mapped to the `isCompany` boolean instead of the contact's name.** The alias list had a bare `"company"` string matching both intents; a CSV using "Company" to mean the company's name would have that text silently discarded (parsed as `false` for a boolean field it was never meant for). Fixed by moving "Company"/"company" to the `name` field's aliases and requiring the fuller `"iscompany"` for the boolean.
  6. **Sequential per-row processing risked a slow/timed-out request on a large import.** Measured locally at ~3ms/row; lowered `MAX_IMPORT_ROWS` from 1000 to 500 to keep worst-case request time comfortably bounded rather than parallelizing (which would let two rows in the same batch race each other's duplicate-detection query — a correctness regression not worth the throughput gain at this scale).
- **Verified live**: created and archived a contact, confirmed the default `GET /contacts` excludes it but `?includeArchived=true` includes it (reproducing the bug before the fix), then confirmed a re-import of a matching email against the archived contact correctly surfaces `duplicateWarning` end-to-end through to the API response. Tested a >500-row payload rejected by the cap, a missing-required-field row rejected by zod, and a 200-row real import completing in ~0.6s. `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass clean across `@spine/api`/`@spine/web`.
- **User-reported UI bug, fixed same session**: the dialog's close (X) button sat visibly misaligned from the title, and the panel had a large empty area below short steps (e.g. the initial upload dropzone). Root cause: the dialog used `@spine/ui`'s `DialogContent size="lg"`, which is documented and correctly used elsewhere (Settings) for tall, multi-pane, self-padding layouts — it deliberately ships zero default padding and a fixed `h-[min(720px,85vh)]`. This wizard is a single-pane, variable-height flow that never added its own matching padding, so the title sat flush in the corner against the close button's fixed `top-4 right-4` inset, and the fixed height stretched short steps to fill 720px regardless of content. Fixed by switching to `size="md"` (which already has the correct, proven `p-5` spacing this button design assumes) with only its width overridden, restoring natural content-driven height, and aligning header/body/footer to a consistent `p-5`.

### 2026-08-08 — S-12.9: wire the dashboard setup checklist to real completion state

- User asked "what's next" after the production Docker fix; picked S-12.9 (the dashboard's "first customer / first item / first quote" checklist, previously a static always-unchecked placeholder from before Contacts/Catalogue/Quotes existed) as the last small gap in Phase 1's own story list.
- New `DashboardModule`/`DashboardService`/`DashboardController` (`apps/api/src/modules/dashboard/`) — `GET /dashboard/checklist` returns `{hasCustomer, hasItem, hasSentQuote, dismissed}` in one call: three parallel existence checks (`isDemo: false` + not-archived/not-draft, so demo-seeded data and unsent draft quotes don't count) plus the persisted dismissed flag, all from the `settings` table's `dashboard_checklist_dismissed` key.
- **Review pass caught a real authz bug before this shipped**: the original version wrote dismissal through the existing `PUT /org/settings/:key`, which is `@OrgRoles(["owner"])`-only by design (real org config shouldn't be staff-writable) — but the checklist itself is shown to and dismissible by every org member, so any staff/field user hit a silent 403 on dismiss (and on the new auto-dismiss-when-complete behavior), making the checklist reappear on every visit forever for anyone but the owner. Fixed with a dedicated `POST /dashboard/checklist/dismiss` (no `@OrgRoles` restriction, matching the read side) that writes straight to the same `settings` row rather than loosening the owner-only gate for every other setting.
- Review also caught a stuck-spinner regression in the first draft: the fetch-error `.catch` set a "not dismissed" flag but never populated `status`, so a failed `/dashboard/checklist` call left the card permanently showing its loading spinner instead of degrading. Fixed by collapsing to one endpoint/one error state — a failed fetch now hides the card entirely rather than getting stuck.
- Six parallel review passes (different angles: line-by-line diff, cross-file authz tracing, reuse/duplication, simplification/efficiency, altitude/conventions, removed-behavior) were run against this change; the authz bug and stuck-spinner bug were independently rediscovered by multiple passes, which is what confirmed they were real rather than false positives. Also fixed on the review's recommendation: the auto-dismiss-on-completion write is now guarded by a ref so a component remount doesn't refire it while a prior write is in flight, and its failure is logged instead of silently swallowed.
- Two findings were deliberately left as-is: (1) auto-dismiss is a one-way ratchet — if an org later archives its only contact, the checklist won't reappear even though `hasCustomer` would now report `false`, since dismissal isn't re-evaluated once persisted; this matches the manual Dismiss button's own semantics (a user's "stop showing me this" is durable, not re-triggered by later data changes) and wasn't flagged as wrong by any reviewer, just noted. (2) The three checklist items are hand-enumerated in both `DashboardService` and `setup-checklist.tsx` with no shared contract between them — fine at three items, would be worth a shared `packages/shared` list if a fourth item (e.g. "first invoice," once Invoices exists) gets added later.
- **Verified live**: signed up a fresh user/org, confirmed `GET /dashboard/checklist` starts all-false; created a real contact, catalog item, and quote (draft) and confirmed the flags flip on individually and only once the quote is actually sent (not while still a draft); confirmed `POST /dashboard/checklist/dismiss` persists and `GET` reflects it afterward. `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass clean across `@spine/api`/`@spine/web`. Confirmed via the controller's decorators (no `@OrgRoles` on either dashboard route, unlike `SettingsController`'s explicit `@OrgRoles(["owner"])`) that the authz fix is structurally correct, matching this codebase's established "guard behavior is purely decorator-driven" pattern verified in every prior session.

### 2026-08-08 — Fix the production Docker stack (never actually worked end-to-end until now)

- User asked to review the README's dev setup instructions for completeness; that led to finding two real, pre-existing production-blocking bugs while manually running `docker compose --profile minimal up` for the first time in this project's history (`docker-compose.yml`'s production path had never been exercised end-to-end before). Neither is related to today's push-notification work — both are older bugs in already-shipped code, just never caught because nobody had booted the prod compose stack until this session.
- **Bug 1 — the image build itself failed.** `better-auth`/`drizzle-orm` declare `better-sqlite3` as a peer dependency (one of several drivers Drizzle supports, even though this app only ever uses the Postgres driver) — `pnpm install` inside bare `node:20-alpine` tries to compile its native addon and fails with no Python/C toolchain present. Fixed by adding `RUN apk add --no-cache python3 make g++` to both `docker/Dockerfile.api` and `docker/Dockerfile.web`'s base stage.
- **Bug 2 — the API container crash-looped on every boot**, even after the image built: `apps/api/dist/main.js` (compiled by `nest build`, run via plain `node` in production — no ts-node/tsx) imports `@spine/shared`, but `packages/shared/package.json`'s `main`/`exports` pointed straight at its `.ts` source with no compiled `dist/` at all. This only ever worked in dev because `nest start --watch`/Next.js both transpile `.ts` on the fly — the packaged, `node`-only runtime can't. (A comment already in `packages/shared/tsconfig.json`, written when this first bit `apps/api`'s dev runtime, documents half of this same root cause but the production/compiled-runtime half was never actually fixed.) Fixed by giving `@spine/shared` a real build step: `tsconfig.build.json` (emits to `dist/`, `rewriteRelativeImportExtensions` so the `.ts`-suffixed internal imports resolve as `.js` in the compiled output), a `build` script, `package.json`'s `main`/`types`/`exports` repointed at `dist/`, `turbo.json`'s `dev` task given `dependsOn: ["^build"]` so a fresh `pnpm install && pnpm dev` builds `@spine/shared` automatically instead of breaking immediately, and `docker/Dockerfile.api` now runs `pnpm --filter @spine/shared build` explicitly before `pnpm --filter @spine/api build` (the api's own `nest build` script has no notion of building its workspace dependencies). `@spine/ui` didn't need the same treatment — `apps/web`'s Next.js build already bundles it from source via `transpilePackages`, and web has no compiled-`node`-only runtime step the way the API does.
- **Verified for real**, not just by inspection: built both production images from a clean Docker build cache, booted the full `minimal` profile (db + api + web + Caddy) against a throwaway `.env`, confirmed `GET /api/health` returns `200` through Caddy's reverse proxy, ran `db:migrate` inside the running `api` container against the real containerized Postgres (all 29 tables created, including today's `push_subscriptions`/`notifications`), and completed a real `POST /api/api/auth/sign-up/email` signup through the full stack. Confirmed `pnpm build`/`pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`/`pnpm --filter @spine/shared test` all still pass at the repo root with the new build step wired in. Torn down and cleaned up afterward (containers, images, volumes); the local dev `.env` was untouched (backed up before the test, restored after).
- README updated: production section now includes the migrate step, and notes that `scripts/install.sh`/`backup.sh`/`restore.sh` (S-10.7/S-10.8/S-10.9) don't exist yet — deliberately not built this session despite the temptation, since the PRD explicitly schedules them for Phase 6 (after Jobs/Invoices/Payments/Mobile exist) and building them now would mean building ahead of the PRD's own sequencing, then reworking them as later phases add services. `.env.development.example` also gained the `VAPID_*` block it was missing (present in `.env`/`.env.example` but silently absent from the dev template a contributor is told to copy).

### 2026-08-08 — S-10.12: browser/OS push notifications (bell/panel's third delivery channel)

- User asked whether browser notifications fire when a new in-app notification arrives (S-10.11's bell/panel) — they don't; that channel only updates a badge while a Spine tab is open. Per the user's request, added S-10.12 to the PRD first (browser/OS push via the Web Push API + a service worker, distinct from a foreground-only `Notification()` popup because it must fire even with the tab backgrounded or the browser itself not focused), got approval, then implemented it.
- **Data model**: new `push_subscriptions` table (`apps/api/src/db/schema/push-subscriptions.ts`) — one row per (user, device), holding the Web Push `endpoint` + `p256dh`/`auth` keys, unique on `endpoint` (upserted on re-subscribe). Added to `db/reset.ts`'s `TABLES` list.
- **API**: new `PushService` (`apps/api/src/modules/notifications/push.service.ts`), deliberately mirroring `EmailService`'s `isConfigured()` gate — with no `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` set, the whole channel silently no-ops rather than erroring, per §6.8's "boots with only 3 required env vars" rule. `NotificationsService.notify()` now sends push (in addition to its existing email + in-app-row channels) to every device of each `inApp` recipient. A 404/410 from the push service (expired/revoked subscription) auto-deletes that row so it isn't retried forever; any other failure is logged and swallowed — a push failure must never block the business event that triggered it. New `NotificationsController` routes: `GET /notifications/push/public-key`, `GET/POST/DELETE /notifications/push/subscribe(s)`.
- **Web**: `public/sw.js` (new service worker — `push` + `notificationclick` handlers, focuses/opens the relevant page on click), `use-push-subscription.ts` hook (registers the worker, drives `Notification.requestPermission()` + `PushManager.subscribe`, only ever triggered by an explicit user action per the acceptance highlight — never on page load), and a new "Browser notifications" Settings pane (`push-pane.tsx`) with a per-device on/off toggle reflecting `unsupported`/`not-configured`/`denied`/`unsubscribed`/`subscribed` state.
- `apps/web/eslint.config.js` now ignores `public/**` — `sw.js` runs in the ServiceWorker global scope (`self`, no DOM), which isn't a target this repo's shared eslint config's globals cover; it's a static asset served as-is, not app source, matching how build output is already excluded.
- `.env.example` documents the optional `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`/`VAPID_SUBJECT` trio and points to `npx web-push generate-vapid-keys`; no separate `NEXT_PUBLIC_` copy needed since the browser fetches the public key from `GET /notifications/push/public-key` at runtime.
- **Verified live** end-to-end against the real dev Postgres + a real VAPID keypair: signed up a user, subscribed a real EC-keypair-backed fake endpoint via the actual `POST /notifications/push/subscribe` route, then called the real `NotificationsService.notify()` (not a mock) with `inApp` set — confirmed it wrote the in-app row, called `PushService.sendToUser()`, made a genuine authenticated POST to Google's FCM endpoint via `web-push` (got a real `410 Gone` back since the endpoint path was fake but well-formed), and correctly auto-deleted the now-stale subscription row (2 subs → 0). Confirmed `GET /notifications/unread-count` reflected the new row and `GET /notifications/push/subscriptions` reflected the cleanup. Ran a full `db:reset` afterward and confirmed `push_subscriptions` is included (29 tables truncated, up from 28). `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass clean across `@spine/shared`, `@spine/api`, `@spine/web`.
- **Not verified initially**: the actual browser-side permission prompt, service worker registration, and on-screen toggle UI — no browser-automation tooling exists in this environment. This gap was closed by the user directly: they enabled the Settings toggle, granted the Chrome permission, and confirmed a real `push_subscriptions` row was created with a genuine FCM endpoint. A manual server-side test send (`web-push.sendNotification` against that live subscription) returned FCM's real `201 Accepted`, confirming the whole server pipeline. The user then reported not seeing a notification pop up in one instance — traced (via `chrome://serviceworker-internals` and DevTools' manual "Push" test button) to two separate, non-bug explanations rather than any code defect: (1) DevTools' bare "Push" test button sends no payload, and `sw.js`'s handler correctly no-ops on `!event.data`; (2) opening the quote link fresh **did** trigger a visible native notification, confirming push → service worker → `showNotification()` all work end-to-end — the one earlier miss is consistent with Chrome/macOS's known behavior of suppressing a notification when its triggering tab is already focused. No code change was needed. (Aside, unrelated to this feature: the user's Chrome also has a stale, unrelated `/app/service-worker.js` registration at scope `/app/` for `localhost:3000` from some other past project on this port — harmless to `sw.js` since scopes don't overlap in a conflicting way, but worth an `unregister()` in Chrome DevTools if it's ever confusing to debug against.)

### 2026-08-08 — Fix a React key warning in Table's grouped-row mode (user-reported)

- User reported a browser console error: `Each child in a list should have a unique "key" prop` on `quotes-table.tsx`'s use of the new `Table`/`DataTable` `groupBy` mode (added for item 4's version-grouping, user feedback session). Root cause: `packages/ui/src/components/table.tsx`'s grouped-row branch returned a bare `<>...</>` shorthand fragment from inside `pageGroups.map((group) => ...)` with no key on it (the key had been mistakenly placed on the `<tr>` nested one level inside instead) — a shorthand fragment can't carry a `key` prop at all, so React had nothing to identify each group by.
- Fixed by switching to the explicit `<Fragment key={group.key}>` form (imported from `react`) and removing the now-redundant `key` from the inner `<tr>` (it isn't itself part of an array being mapped, so it never needed one).
- **Verified**: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`/`lint` and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`build` all pass clean. **Not verified**: the browser console itself wasn't re-checked for the warning's absence (no browser automation tooling in this environment) — the fix is a direct, mechanical correction of a well-understood React requirement (a fragment returned from `.map()` needs the same explicit-key treatment as any other list-returned element), not something ambiguous enough to need runtime confirmation beyond the type/build checks already run.

### 2026-08-08 — S-10.11: in-app notification centre (bell + right-side panel)

- New PRD story, added and built in the same session at the user's explicit request — a real requirement was drafted and added to `docs/spine-prd.md` (§8 F10, §12 Phase 1, a new acceptance-highlights block) and approved before any code was written, per the user's own instruction to check the PRD and get sign-off first.
- **Schema**: new `notifications` table (`apps/api/src/db/schema/notifications.ts`, migration `apps/api/drizzle/0015_bright_layla_miller.sql`, applied to the local dev Postgres) — one row per `(event, recipient)` pair (fan-out on write, mirroring `NotificationsService.notify()`'s existing per-owner email fan-out), `orgId`/`recipientUserId` FKs cascade, `readAt` a nullable timestamp (not a boolean, matching `audit_log`'s own "timestamp records the fact and the moment" convention), indexed on `(orgId, recipientUserId)` and `(recipientUserId, readAt)` for the unread-count query. Added to `db:reset.ts`'s `TABLES` list immediately (a gap this session already had to fix twice before for other new tables).
- **`NotificationsService.notify()` extended additively**, not replaced — two new optional params, `recipientRoles?: string[]` (default `["owner"]`, preserving every pre-existing caller's exact behavior) and `inApp?: { type, message, link }`. When `inApp` is given, one `notifications` row is written per matched recipient in the same call, alongside the existing per-recipient email queueing — same trigger point, second delivery channel, not a parallel system. Also added the read-side methods (`listRecent`, `unreadCount`, `markRead`, `markAllRead`), each scoped by `(orgId, recipientUserId)` in the WHERE clause itself (verified live — see below — that one user genuinely cannot mark another's notification read via a guessed id, not just designed to prevent it).
- **New `NotificationsController`** (`GET /notifications`, `GET /notifications/unread-count`, `POST /notifications/:id/read`, `POST /notifications/read-all`) — authenticated, no `@OrgRoles` restriction beyond having a session and org membership (unlike `QuotesController`'s owner+staff gate): recipient scoping already happened once, at write time, via `recipientRoles`, so by the time a row exists its recipient has already earned the right to see it — read access only needs "is this actually your row."
- **`public-quotes.service.ts`'s two existing `notify()` calls** (on view and on accept, from Stage 3) now pass `recipientRoles: ["owner", "staff"]` (matching `QuotesController`'s own quote-access permissions per §2.1 — the PRD's own acceptance highlight for S-10.11 requires this, not owner-only like the pre-existing email channel) and `inApp: {...}` with a link back to `/quotes/:id`. A rejection still doesn't trigger a notification of either kind, unchanged from Stage 3's own documented scope decision (S-03.7's wording only covers "viewed or accepted").
- **Web**: `NotificationBell` (`apps/web/src/components/notification-bell.tsx`) — polls `GET /notifications/unread-count` every 20s regardless of whether the drawer is open (cheap, count-only), shows a small badge. Placed in `AppShellSidebar`'s footer, as a sibling above `AppUserMenu` inside `app-layout.tsx` — confirmed before building that this is the ONLY persistent, always-visible chrome in the app (every page's own `AppShellContent` scrolls away; there is no app-wide top bar), so it's the one place a bell can live once and show on every page. `NotificationDrawer` (`notification-drawer.tsx`) reuses `@spine/ui`'s existing `Drawer` (already a right-side slide-in panel — no new primitive needed), fetches the full list only while open (matching `email-queue-dialog.tsx`'s own "poll only while visible" convention from Quotes Stage 2), optimistic mark-as-read on click before navigating to the notification's `link`, and a "Mark all read" footer action.
- **A real design-system violation caught and fixed before shipping**: the unread badge's first draft used `bg-overdue` (the financial-document-status red) for a generic "you have unread notifications" indicator — `docs/DESIGN.md` explicitly says (line 408) "never repurpose [the paid/due-soon/overdue/draft palette] for a generic badge or tag." Fixed to use `--color-primary` instead, the one accent color the design system reserves for exactly this kind of single most-important signal per screen. A second, related bug in the same fix: the badge's text color was first set to `text-background`, which is `#ffffff` in light mode but flips to near-black in dark mode (verified against `tailwind-preset.css`) — exactly wrong for text sitting on a colored circle. Fixed to the theme-invariant `--color-inverse-on-surface` token (added in the earlier WCAG accessibility-audit session specifically for "text that must stay white regardless of theme," already used by Toast/Tooltip).
- **Verified live** against the real local dev Postgres, using fully isolated test fixtures (a separate org/2 users, never touching the real signed-up org/user already in the dev DB) cleaned up afterward: sent a real quote, hit the public view then accept endpoints directly, confirmed BOTH the owner and a staff member received their own `quote_viewed` and `quote_accepted` rows (not just the owner, unlike the pre-S-10.11 email-only behavior); confirmed unread counts are independent per recipient (marking the owner's notification read left the staff member's own unread count untouched); confirmed the cross-user read-scoping guard actually holds (the owner attempting to mark the staff member's notification read was a genuine no-op, verified by re-checking the staff member's unread count afterward, not just by code inspection); confirmed `markAllRead` clears a recipient's unread count; confirmed a `notify()` call using the OLD pre-S-10.11 shape (no `recipientRoles`/`inApp`) still runs without error, for backward compatibility. `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass clean across `@spine/shared`/`@spine/api`/`@spine/web`.
- **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing (no browser automation tooling in this environment, matching every prior session's stated limitation) — the bell's badge, the drawer's slide-in animation, and the collapsed-sidebar icon-only rendering were not visually confirmed, only reviewed against the known `AppShellSidebar`/`Drawer` component behavior and confirmed to compile/build correctly.
- Follow-ups deliberately not built in this pass, since nothing in the approved plan called for them: no notification de-duplication (a customer re-viewing a quote several times still fires one in-app row + one email per view, inheriting the same known gap Stage 3 already documented for the email channel); no per-user notification preferences (mute a type, digest instead of instant); no "view all notifications" full-page fallback beyond the drawer's own 50-row `limit` default.

### 2026-08-08 — Public quote page scroll fix + rich text for the accept/reject comment (user-reported)

- **Not scrollable**: `globals.css` sets `html, body { height: 100%; overflow: hidden }` globally — deliberate for the authenticated app, where `AppShell` owns its own internal scroll region (`main`, `overflow-y-auto`) while the outer chrome stays fixed. `apps/web/src/app/quote/[token]/page.tsx` (the public link) isn't inside that shell and has no internal scroll region of its own, so it silently inherited a non-scrollable `body` — its content taller than the viewport had nowhere to scroll. Fixed by making the public page's own root a `fixed inset-0 overflow-y-auto` container, scoped to this one route rather than touching the global rule the rest of the app depends on.
- **Rich text for the accept/reject comment**: the public page's "Your decision" comment field is now `RichTextEditor` (the same `@spine/ui` component built for quote notes/terms) instead of a plain `Textarea`. No schema change needed — `quotes.responseComment`/`quote_events.comment` were already plain `text` columns, same as `notes`/`termsAndConditions`.
  - **`normalizeComment`'s length cap raised (2000 → 8000 raw chars)**: a hard `.slice()` cutoff on HTML (the prior plain-text behavior) could truncate mid-tag; not an XSS risk (`RichTextContent`'s DOMPurify pass already drops malformed markup on display) but a poor user-facing limit, since HTML's raw character count isn't a reliable proxy for its visible length.
  - **The owner-notification email needed a fix too**: `PublicQuotesService`'s accept-notification interpolated the customer's comment directly into a *plain-text* email body — now that the comment is HTML, that would have shown literal `<p>`/`<strong>` tags in the owner's inbox. Added `stripHtmlToText()` (via `node-html-parser`, already a dependency for the PDF renderer's own rich-text handling) to strip markup before interpolating.
  - The resolved-state display of a customer's own past comment (on the public page) now renders via `RichTextContent` instead of plain quoted text, matching how it was actually stored.
  - Noted but NOT fixed in this pass (pre-existing, unrelated to this request): the authenticated quote detail page (`quotes/[id]`) doesn't display `responseComment`/`respondedAt` at all — neither field is even in the web `Quote` type yet, a gap from Stage 3 itself, out of scope here.
- **Verified**: `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`/`lint`/`build` and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass; the production build still compiles `/quote/[token]` as a real route. **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing (no browser automation tooling in this environment) — the scroll fix and the rich text editor's on-screen behavior were not visually confirmed, only reviewed against the known cause (the global CSS rule) and confirmed to compile correctly.

### 2026-08-08 — `db:reset:dev` now also clears pg-boss's own schema (user-reported)

- User reported: after `pnpm db:reset:dev`, not everything was actually reset. Root cause: `apps/api/src/db/reset.ts`'s `TABLES` list only ever covered `public`-schema tables (the ones Drizzle migrates) — pg-boss (`QueueService`, introduced in Quotes Stage 2) manages a wholly separate `pgboss` Postgres schema (`pgboss.job`, `queue_stats`, etc.) that this script never touched at all. Confirmed live: 16 stale `pgboss.job` rows (from prior sessions' verification runs, referencing orgs/quotes already wiped by earlier resets) survived a fresh `db:reset:dev` untouched.
- Fixed by adding `resetPgBossSchema()`, which truncates every table it finds under `pgboss` — queried dynamically via `pg_tables` rather than a hardcoded list, since pg-boss creates dated partition tables for `queue_stats` (e.g. `queue_stats_20260807`) on an ongoing basis that a fixed list would eventually miss. Guarded to no-op cleanly (not error) if the `pgboss` schema doesn't exist yet (a dev environment where the API has never booted, so `QueueService.onModuleInit` never created it).
- **Verified live**: confirmed 16 real leftover `pgboss.job` rows before the fix; ran the fixed script and confirmed both `pgboss.job` and `organization` (a `public`-schema table) are `0` afterward. Output now reads "Truncated 27 tables + 12 pg-boss tables." `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`/`lint` both pass.
- This is the third time a "did we forget a table" gap has surfaced in this script (first for `quotes`/`quote_lines`, then `quote_public_tokens`/`quote_events`, now the entire separate `pgboss` schema) — the dynamic `pg_tables` query for pg-boss specifically should prevent a recurrence for anything pg-boss adds later; the `public`-schema `TABLES` list is still a manually-maintained array and remains the one part of this script that needs a human to remember to update when a new top-level table is added.

### 2026-08-07 — Quotes (F3) Stage 3: public accept/reject link, view/accept/reject events, owner notifications — F3 now fully complete

- **Schema**: two new tables plus two new columns on `quotes`.
  - `quote_public_tokens` (`apps/api/src/db/schema/quotes.ts`) — `nonce` (unique, indexed), `quoteId` (FK cascade), `orgId`, `expiresAt`, `revokedAt` (nullable), `createdAt`. Backs the token design below.
  - `quote_events` — `quoteId` (FK cascade), `orgId`, `type` (`viewed`/`accepted`/`rejected` enum), `comment` (nullable — accept/reject's optional comment), `ipAddress`/`userAgent` (nullable provenance), `createdAt`.
  - `quotes.respondedAt`/`quotes.responseComment` — denormalized onto the quote itself (alongside the full history in `quote_events`) so the detail page can read "when/what did the customer say" with a single-row fetch, mirroring why `discountRequiresApproval` is stored rather than always re-derived.
  - One migration: `apps/api/drizzle/0014_reflective_dark_beast.sql`, generated via `drizzle-kit generate` and applied to the local dev Postgres via `drizzle-kit migrate`.
- **Token design decision (documented, not just implemented)**: a token is stateless-signed (HMAC-SHA256 via `jose`, keyed on the existing `AUTH_SECRET`, `apps/api/src/modules/quotes/quote-public-token.service.ts`) PLUS DB-tracked (`quote_public_tokens`), exactly the two-part design specified. "Single-purpose" (PRD §10.3's wording) is read as **scoped to one quote, reusable until resolved** — NOT one-time-use: a customer legitimately re-opens the emailed link to re-read a quote before deciding, and consuming the token after one view would break that common case. The token stays valid for GET (view) until it naturally expires (30-day TTL), is revoked, or the quote leaves `sent` status; accept/reject themselves are what make further use terminal (a 410 Gone on a second accept/reject attempt), not the token's own one-time-ness. Full reasoning is in `quote_public_tokens`'s own schema doc comment.
- **API — public routes**: `PublicQuotesController` (`apps/api/src/modules/quotes/public-quotes.controller.ts`), registered under `/public/quotes/*`, a wholly separate route group from the authenticated `QuotesController` (no `@OrgRoles`, and explicitly `@Public()` from `@thallesp/nestjs-better-auth` — this codebase's `AuthModule` wires a **global** auth guard, discovered live: without `@Public()` these routes 401'd before ever reaching the handler). `GET /public/quotes/:token` (view, records a `viewed` event on every successful view — deliberately not deduplicated to "first view only", see Next-up), `POST /public/quotes/:token/accept` / `.../reject` (body: optional `{ comment }`, max 2000 chars, trimmed). Both mutating routes re-select the quote with a row lock (`for("update")`) inside a transaction before checking `status === "sent"`, mirroring `QuotesService.send()`/`revise()`'s own concurrency discipline — verified live that a second accept/reject after the first succeeds returns `410 Gone` with a clear message ("This quote has already been accepted"), not a silent 200 or a crash. `PublicQuoteView` (`public-quotes.service.ts`) is a hand-built, deliberately reduced projection — no `orgId`, `createdByUserId`, `isDemo`, `pdfFileId`, or email-delivery bookkeeping; verified live the actual JSON response matches this reduced shape exactly.
- **Rate limiting**: `@nestjs/throttler` (6.5.0) — `ThrottlerModule.forRoot([{ ttl: 60_000, limit: 300 }])` registered app-wide in `app.module.ts` (via `APP_GUARD`) as a generous default, with `PublicQuotesController` overriding to `@Throttle({ default: { limit: 20, ttl: 60_000 } })` — 20 requests/minute/IP, in-memory (no Redis, per CLAUDE.md's single-host stance). **Verified live**: looping 25 requests against the public GET route in under a minute produced 429s starting partway through, with a clean `{"statusCode":429,"message":"ThrottlerException: Too Many Requests"}` body.
- **`QuotesService.send()`** now mints a token (`QuotePublicTokenService.issue`) inside the same transaction as the number allocation/status flip — a token can never exist for a send that rolls back, exactly like the number itself — and passes the raw token string through the `quote-send-email` pg-boss job's own data payload (`QuoteSendEmailJobData.publicToken`, optional for backward-compat with any job already queued from before this field existed) so `QuoteEmailWorker` never needs to know how tokens are minted or verified.
- **`QuotesService.revise()`** now calls `QuotePublicTokenService.revokeAllForQuote()` on the quote being superseded, inside the same transaction as the new draft version's insert — **verified live**: revising an already-accepted quote immediately turned its old (previously-working) public link into a 404, confirmed both via the API response and by checking `quote_public_tokens.revoked_at` was set.
- **`quote-email.worker.ts`** now builds a real `${APP_URL}/quote/:token` link into both the plain-text and a new HTML email body (previously Stage 2 had genuinely no link at all, by design, since the public route didn't exist yet) — verified live via Maildev logs that the email arrives with the link included.
- **`NotificationsModule`/`NotificationsService`** (`apps/api/src/modules/notifications/`) — real implementation replacing the empty `@Module({})` stub. `NotificationsService.notify(orgId, {subject, text, html?})` looks up the org's active owner(s) via `member`/`user` and queues one email per owner through a new `notify-email` pg-boss queue (`NotifyEmailWorker`, mirroring `QuoteEmailWorker`'s exact self-registering pattern) — reuses `EmailService`/`QueueService` from Stage 2, same graceful-degradation posture (SMTP down/unconfigured never blocks the customer-facing action that triggered it; a failed lookup or enqueue is logged and swallowed, never thrown). Deliberately generic — no Quotes-specific logic lives in this module — so Invoices/Payments can call the same method later. `PublicQuotesService` calls `notify()` after a successful view (subject: "`{contact}` viewed quote `{number}`") and after a successful accept (subject: "`{contact}` accepted quote `{number}`", includes the customer's comment if given) — per S-03.7's literal wording ("viewed or accepted"), a rejection does not trigger a notification (see Next-up). **Verified live**: both a view and an accept produced a `notify-email` pg-boss job (`state: completed`) and a real email in Maildev addressed to the test org's owner.
- **Audit log**: the public accept/reject action writes an `audit_log` row via the existing `AuditLogService`, with `actorUserId: null` (the customer isn't a system user — `audit_log.actor_user_id` was already nullable) and `contactId` set to the quote's own contact. Verified live in the DB.
- **Web**: `apps/web/src/app/quote/[token]/page.tsx` — a new, deliberately unauthenticated route. This app has no root-level auth middleware (every authenticated page opts in individually by calling `getSession()`/wrapping in `AppLayout`, confirmed by reading `apps/web/src/app/quotes/[id]/page.tsx`), so this page simply never calls either. `public-quote-view.tsx` is a fresh client component (NOT a reuse of `quote-detail.tsx`, which assumes a cookie session and exposes internal-only actions) — plain `fetch` against `/public/quotes/:token` (no `apiClient`, no credentials, no Idempotency-Key — none of those are meaningful for an anonymous caller), renders branding/lines/totals/notes/terms, an Accept/Decline pair with an optional comment `Textarea`, and distinct states for loading/invalid-or-expired/already-resolved/generic-error. Mobile-first single-column layout per S-03.5's own "read the quote on my phone" wording.
- **What was verified LIVE** (against the real dev Postgres/pg-boss/Maildev/MinIO, already running from prior sessions): signed up a fresh test user (first-user-becomes-owner), created a real contact and quote, sent it (token minted, `QUO-0001` allocated), pulled the token's nonce from `quote_public_tokens` and re-signed a matching JWT locally with the same `AUTH_SECRET` (since Maildev's REST API in the installed 3.0.0-rc.1 image didn't expose the endpoints this session tried — its own SMTP receipt + web UI were still confirmed via `docker logs`) to simulate exactly what the email worker would have sent; hit `GET /public/quotes/:token` directly (confirmed the reduced projection, confirmed a `viewed` row + owner-notification email), hit `POST .../accept` with a comment (confirmed `status: accepted`, `respondedAt`/`responseComment` set, an `accepted` event row, an owner-notification email, an audit_log row with `actorUserId: null`), hit accept/reject again (confirmed `410 Gone`), hit a garbage/tampered token (confirmed `404`), revised the now-accepted quote and confirmed the prior token was revoked (`404` on the old link), and looped the public GET route past its rate limit (confirmed `429`). Also confirmed `pnpm --filter @spine/shared typecheck`, `@spine/api typecheck`/`lint`/`build`, and `@spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all pass, and that `/quote/[token]` compiles as a real Next.js route in a production build.
- **What was NOT verified**: the public web page's actual on-screen rendering/interaction — no browser-automation tooling exists in this environment (same limitation stated in every prior Quotes session). The page was confirmed to server-render (200, correct `<title>`) and its client component's own network calls were independently proven correct via direct curl against the same endpoints, but nobody has visually looked at or clicked through the rendered page.
- New dependencies: `jose` (^6.2.8, HMAC token signing) and `@nestjs/throttler` (^6.4.0, rate limiting) added to `apps/api/package.json`.
- See "Next up" below for the honest list of Stage 3's rough edges (repeat-view notification noise, no rejection notification, first-pass rate-limit tuning, flat 30-day token TTL, no self-service resend).
- **Post-build fix (same review pass)**: `db:reset.ts`'s explicit table list (already fixed once before, for `quotes`/`quote_lines`) hadn't been extended to cover the two new Stage 3 tables — `quote_public_tokens`/`quote_events` would still have been wiped correctly via cascade (both FK to `quotes`/`organization` with `ON DELETE CASCADE`), but silently, the same latent fragility flagged and fixed the first time this happened. Added both explicitly. Re-verified live: `pnpm db:reset:dev` truncates cleanly with no cascade NOTICE, count went from 25 to 27 tables.

### 2026-08-07 — Email queue moved from a standalone page into a popup dialog

- Follow-up user request: the `/quotes/email-queue` page (added the same session, item 7 below) is now a `Dialog` (`email-queue-dialog.tsx`, `size="lg"`), not a separate route. The standalone page/route directory was deleted.
- Two trigger points, sharing one implementation: `EmailQueueButton` (a ready-made toolbar button, used on the quotes list) and `EmailQueueDialog` directly with a custom `trigger` (used inline inside `quote-detail.tsx`'s failed-email alert, replacing the text link that used to navigate away).
- Polling (every 10s) now only runs while the dialog is actually open, not for as long as some page happened to be mounted — a real behavior improvement over the old standalone page, not just a visual change.
- **Verified**: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; the production build confirms `/quotes/email-queue` no longer exists as a route, and `/quotes`/`/quotes/[id]` both shrank slightly (the dialog is shared/bundled once instead of a duplicated page).

### 2026-08-07 — Quotes UX feedback: navigation, email visibility, version grouping, contact filter, a new RichTextEditor component

- Eight items of direct user feedback on the Quotes module (Stage 1/2), addressed in one session:
  1. **Back button on the quote detail page** (`quote-detail.tsx`) — was missing; added, copying `contact-detail.tsx`'s exact pattern (an explicit `ArrowLeft` + "Back to quotes" button above the header card, alongside the existing breadcrumb).
  2. **"Mark as sent" renamed to "Send Quote"** — accurate now that Stage 2 actually sends email; the surrounding doc comment (which explained the old, deliberately-hedged label) was updated to match.
  3. **Local MailDev SMTP not receiving mail** — root cause: `.env`'s `SMTP_HOST` was still blank (the earlier MailDev-in-dev-compose session documented the value to set but didn't set it in the user's own `.env`). Fixed to `SMTP_HOST=localhost`; **requires restarting the API process** to pick up the env change (Node reads `process.env` once at boot).
  4. **Version grouping in the quotes list** — `@spine/ui`'s `Table`/`DataTable` gained a new optional `groupBy`/`groupPrimary`/`groupMemberLabel` mode (full expandable-group support, not just a badge, per explicit user request over the simpler alternative): rows sharing a key collapse into one expandable group, sorting/pagination operate on groups rather than raw rows. `QuotesService.list()` now computes `rootQuoteId` per row (walks each row's `parentQuoteId` chain up to its version-1 ancestor, in-memory over the already-fetched page — not a recursive SQL CTE) so `quotes-table.tsx` can group by it, with the newest version always the primary/collapsed row and older versions expandable underneath, each labeled `v{N}`.
  5. **Filter by contact** — the server already supported `GET /quotes?contactId=`, just never exposed in the UI. Added a `Combobox` filter to `quotes-explorer.tsx` (an "All contacts" option plus every non-archived contact), driving the existing server-side filter rather than a client-side one.
  6. **Rich text for Notes/Terms & Conditions** — a new `@spine/ui` component, `RichTextEditor` (+ its read-only counterpart `RichTextContent`), built on Tiptap (bold/italic/bullet+numbered lists/links only — headings/tables/images explicitly out of scope, per the user's own choice between the two options offered). Stores/emits plain HTML, matching the existing `text` columns. `quote-builder.tsx`'s Notes/Terms fields and `quote-detail.tsx`'s display both now use it in place of a plain `Textarea`/`<p>`. New design-system doc page at `apps/design-system/src/app/components/rich-text-editor/page.tsx`, per CLAUDE.md's rule that every `@spine/ui` change ships its doc page.
     - **A real XSS gap was found and fixed while building this**: `RichTextContent`'s first draft rendered stored HTML via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` with a comment claiming it was safe "since this HTML always originates from this editor's own output" — false, and caught before shipping: `quotes.notes`/`termsAndConditions` are plain `z.string()` in their zod schema with no server-side HTML validation, so a direct API write (bypassing the editor entirely) could inject arbitrary HTML/script. Fixed by sanitizing with DOMPurify at render time, restricted to the exact allowlist the editor's own toolbar can produce. Confirmed separately (not just asserted) that the *editor's own* HTML-loading path (`content`/`setContent`) is safe by construction — Tiptap parses HTML through ProseMirror's schema-based parser, which structurally drops any tag/attribute outside the loaded extensions' schema (verified by reading ProseMirror's own parsing approach, not assumed) — so only the read-only `RichTextContent` path needed the explicit sanitization fix.
     - The quote PDF renderer needed a matching fix: `quote-pdf.template.tsx` previously printed `quote.notes`/`termsAndConditions` as raw plain text — once these fields became HTML, that would have literally printed `<p><strong>...` tags into every PDF. New `apps/api/src/modules/pdf/rich-text-to-pdf.tsx` walks the same small allowed tag set (via `node-html-parser`, a lightweight parser — not `jsdom`, since only a handful of tags ever need handling) into React-PDF's own `Text`/`View`/`Link` primitives (React-PDF's `<Text>` can't render raw HTML). Verified live: rendered a real quote with bold text, a bullet list, and a link in its notes/terms, extracted the resulting PDF's text with `pypdf`, and confirmed the formatting landed correctly with no leaked markup.
  7. **"Was the email sent?" visibility** — `quotes` gained `emailStatus` (`pending`/`sent`/`failed`), `emailError`, and `emailSentAt` columns (migration `0013_sweet_toro.sql`, applied). `QuotesService.send()` sets `pending` right before enqueuing; `quote-email.worker.ts` now wraps its existing send logic to write `sent`/`failed` + the error message on every attempt (see that file's own comment on why "every attempt," not only the final retry — pg-boss's `work()` handler receives a plain `Job<T>` with no `retryCount`/`retryLimit`, only `JobWithMetadata<T>` has that, and pg-boss exposes no way to fetch it from inside a running handler, so genuinely distinguishing "this was the last retry" isn't possible from here; a status that briefly reads `failed` mid-retry then flips back to `sent` on eventual success is the honest tradeoff this API shape allows). The quote detail page shows a live status line (polls every 4s while `pending`, stops once terminal) and a **new `/quotes/email-queue` page** (linked from the Quotes list) lists every not-yet-resolved `quote-send-email` job org-wide — `QueueService.listPendingJobs()` reads `pgboss.job` directly (pg-boss has no public "peek without claiming" API; `fetch()` would dequeue jobs as a side effect, which a passive status view must not do) rather than claiming jobs.
  8. **Draft "Number" column confusion** — the plain word "Draft" in the Number column duplicated the Status column's own "Draft" badge and read like it might literally be the quote's reference number. Replaced with an italicized, clearly-not-a-value placeholder ("— (unsent draft)").
- **Verified live** against the real local dev Postgres (a temporary in-process script, deleted after the run): `rootQuoteId` resolves correctly via `list()`; a quote with bold/list/link notes and terms renders a valid PDF with the formatting intact and no raw HTML (confirmed via `pypdf` text extraction, not just "didn't throw"); `send()` sets `emailStatus: "pending"`; the enqueued job is visible via `QueueService.listPendingJobs()` against the real `postgres-js` driver (the production driver — the script's first draft used `node-postgres` instead and hit a real driver-shape mismatch, `result.map is not a function`, which would NOT have been caught by typecheck alone since both drivers satisfy the same TypeScript types differently at runtime; switching the verification script to the actual `createDb()` the app uses caught this before it could have been a false-confidence bug); `enrichEmailQueueJobs` correctly resolves a job back to its quote number/contact name.
- **Also verified**: `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` clean across `@spine/shared`, `@spine/ui`, `@spine/api`, `@spine/web`, `@spine/design-system` after every change in this entry. `docker ps` confirms the pre-existing MailDev dev-infra container is up and reachable at `localhost:1080`/`1025`.
- **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing (no browser automation tooling in this environment) — the grouped-table expand/collapse interaction, the rich text editor's toolbar buttons, the contact filter Combobox, and the email-queue page's auto-refresh were not clicked through an actual UI session, only confirmed to compile/build correctly and (where server logic was involved) exercised directly against the real database as described above. The user's own MailDev fix (item 3) specifically requires them to restart their locally running `pnpm --filter @spine/api dev` process to pick up the corrected `SMTP_HOST` — not something this session could do on their behalf.
- New dependencies: `@tiptap/react`, `@tiptap/starter-kit`, `@tiptap/extension-link`, `@tiptap/pm`, `dompurify` (+ `@types/dompurify`) in `packages/ui`; `node-html-parser` in `apps/api`.

### 2026-08-07 — MailDev added to local dev infra (docker-compose.dev.yml)

- User-requested follow-up to Quotes Stage 2's own email work — during that stage, an SMTP catcher was spun up ephemerally just to verify delivery, then torn down; this session promotes that into a real, documented, optional dev-infra service so anyone can see quote emails locally without hunting for their own mail-catcher tool or real SMTP credentials.
- `docker-compose.dev.yml` gained a third service, `maildev` (`maildev/maildev` image), matching the existing `db`/`minio` services' exact conventions (`restart: unless-stopped`, host-bound `127.0.0.1` ports — `1025` for SMTP, `1080` for its web UI). Entirely optional: leaving `SMTP_HOST` unset in `.env` is still the default, and email sending still degrades gracefully exactly as before (quote issues, job queues for retry) — this only adds a place to *see* it land instead of only inferring success from pg-boss job state.
