- **The image's own baked-in Docker healthcheck was found to be broken in this environment** (a `wget` probe against its `/api/healthz` endpoint that reports `ExitCode: 1` / "Connection refused" from inside the container, regardless of setting `MAILDEV_WEB_PORT` explicitly — tried first, didn't fix it) — the service itself is fully functional throughout (confirmed via a real SMTP send landing and being visible in the web UI, and `GET /api/healthz` succeeding from the host), it's specifically the in-container probe that fails, most likely an image-specific `localhost`/interface-binding quirk not worth chasing further for a dev-only convenience container. Disabled the healthcheck (`healthcheck: { disable: true }`) rather than ship a container that permanently reports "unhealthy" in `docker ps` despite working correctly — nothing else in the compose file depends on it passing.
- `.env.development.example`'s SMTP block now documents the MailDev path (`SMTP_PORT=1025`, `SMTP_FROM=quotes@spine.local` pre-filled to match MailDev's defaults; `SMTP_HOST` stays blank by default, set it to `localhost` to opt in) and `README.md`'s infra section now lists MailDev alongside Postgres/MinIO with both its ports and an explicit note that it's optional.
- **Verified live**: `pnpm dev:infra:up` starts the container cleanly; its web UI responds `200` at `http://localhost:1080`; a real message sent via Nodemailer against `localhost:1025` was confirmed received in the container's own logs (`Received: quotes@spine.local -> customer@example.com`) — proving the whole chain (compose service → SMTP port → the exact library `EmailService` uses) actually works, not just that the container starts. Re-verified after the healthcheck fix that the container no longer reports "unhealthy" and the SMTP path still works.
- **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing (no browser automation tooling in this environment) — MailDev's web UI was checked for a `200` response and its own container logs, not visually inspected in a real browser session.

### 2026-08-07 — Quotes (F3) Stage 2 of 3: PDF rendering, email sending, first real job queue (pg-boss)

- Second of three Quotes stages (see the Stage 1 entry immediately below for the data model/CRUD it builds on). Covers S-03.3 (branded PDF preview/download) and S-03.4 (email the quote with a link) — with the "secure link" itself explicitly deferred to Stage 3 per the task brief, since the public accept/reject route doesn't exist yet; Stage 2's email currently has no link at all, just the PDF attached, and says nothing dishonest about a link that doesn't exist. Does **not** touch Jobs/Invoices (still empty stub modules) or build any part of Stage 3 (public link, accept/reject, view/accept/reject events, owner notifications).

- **First real job queue in this codebase: `pg-boss`.** New `apps/api/src/modules/queue/` (`QueueModule`, `@Global()`, and `QueueService`). pg-boss manages its own Postgres schema (a dedicated `pgboss` schema, auto-created against the same `DATABASE_URL` this app already requires) — confirmed live: after boot, `\dn` shows a new `pgboss` schema with `job`/`queue`/`schedule`/etc. tables, no separate migration or new required env var needed. `QueueService` starts pg-boss in `onModuleInit` and stops it gracefully in `onModuleDestroy`, matching NestJS's own lifecycle hooks — this runs in the **same process** as the HTTP API (`main.ts` is unchanged; no new bootstrap path), so `pnpm --filter @spine/api dev`/`start` starts the worker automatically. This is a deliberate single-process design, appropriate for this stack's single-host Docker Compose deployment model (CLAUDE.md), not a fleet that would need a dedicated worker pod. `QueueService.send()` enqueues with `retryLimit: 3, retryDelay: 5, retryBackoff: true` (exponential backoff starting at 5s); `QueueService.work()` is a thin `createQueue` + `work` passthrough so callers never import `pg-boss` types directly.

- **PDF rendering** (`apps/api/src/modules/pdf/`): `QuotePdfService.render(orgId, quoteId)` renders a quote to a `Buffer` via `@react-pdf/renderer` (chosen per the task brief and CLAUDE.md's stack table specifically for "byte-identical across environments" — pure JS-to-PDF layout, no headless browser/Chromium version drift). `quote-pdf.template.tsx` is driven **entirely** by the quote's own stored snapshot (`quotes`/`quote_lines` rows: subtotal/discount/tax/total, every line's snapshotted name/qty/price/tax, notes, terms) — it never reads `catalog_items`, per CLAUDE.md's "documents snapshot their data" rule. Every money value goes through `toDecimalString` from `@spine/shared` (the same formatter the web app itself uses), so the PDF can never silently misplace a decimal point. Branding pulls the org's `business_profile` settings key (`{name, logoUrl?, address?, industry?}`, per `settings.service.ts`'s documented shape) for the header — name, address, and (if a logo was uploaded) the logo image itself, fetched from S3/MinIO directly via `FilesService`'s own S3 client (not by following the `GET /files/:id` redirect chain over HTTP, which would be fragile inside a server process) and embedded as a base64 data URI. Logo fetch failures degrade to a text-only header (never fail PDF generation), matching CLAUDE.md's graceful-degradation posture for object storage. Exposed as `GET /quotes/:id/pdf` (`@OrgRoles(["owner","staff"])`, matching every other quote route — quotes have no Field access per §2.1), streaming `Content-Type: application/pdf` inline. Renders **live** on every call (not served from the persisted copy, even for a `sent` quote) since a sent quote's snapshot is immutable, so a live render and the persisted send-time copy are byte-identical — one code path stays correct for both drafts (no persisted copy exists yet) and sent quotes without a branch.
  - **Verified live**: generated a real PDF from a real draft quote (3 line items: a heading, two priced item lines with tax/discount, a free-text line, plus notes and terms) against the local dev Postgres/MinIO. Confirmed via `file`: `PDF document, version 1.3, 1 pages`, byte signature `%PDF-1.3`, 3657 bytes (non-empty, structurally valid).
  - `apps/api/tsconfig.json` gained `"jsx": "react-jsx"` (this is the first `.tsx`/JSX file anywhere in `apps/api` — a NestJS/CommonJS app, unlike `apps/web`). `react`/`@types/react` were added as direct dependencies of `@spine/api` (previously only reachable transitively via `apps/web`), since `apps/api` is its own package and needs them declared for its own build/typecheck to resolve `@react-pdf/renderer`'s JSX.

- **PDF persistence decision**: read `files.service.ts` in full before deciding. Chose to **persist the PDF generated at send time** through the existing `FilesService`/S3/MinIO plumbing, not regenerate on every download — a new nullable `quotes.pdf_file_id` column (FK to `files.id`, `onDelete: set null`, migration `apps/api/drizzle/0012_curly_tyrannus.sql`) stores it. Reasoning: "documents snapshot their data" is a strong theme in this codebase (CLAUDE.md), and while a live re-render of an already-`sent` quote is byte-identical *today* (its snapshot is immutable), that equivalence only holds as long as the PDF template code itself never changes — persisting the exact file emailed means a future template change can never retroactively alter what an already-sent quote's PDF looked like historically. Persistence is attempted by `QuoteEmailWorker` (below) even when SMTP is unconfigured — a "Download PDF" affordance should still get the as-issued snapshot even when email was never possible — and is best-effort (logged, not fatal) so a storage failure never blocks the email attempt or vice versa. **Verified live**: after a real `send()`, `quotes.pdf_file_id` was populated with a real `files` row, retrievable via `GET /files/:id` (302 to a working signed MinIO URL).

- **Email sending**, wired into the *existing* `POST /quotes/:id/send` — no new endpoint, per the task brief and the hook-point comments already left in `quotes.service.ts`/`quotes.controller.ts` by Stage 1. New `apps/api/src/modules/email/` (`EmailService`, Nodemailer/SMTP against the exact env vars already documented in `.env.example` — `SMTP_HOST`/`PORT`/`USER`/`PASSWORD`/`FROM`; `isConfigured()` checks `SMTP_HOST` without constructing a transporter). New `apps/api/src/modules/quotes/quote-email.worker.ts` (`QuoteEmailWorker`, a pg-boss consumer registered via its own `onModuleInit` — not a controller) does the real work per job: render+persist the PDF (above), then, if SMTP is configured **and** the contact has an email on file, send the PDF as an attachment; otherwise throws (letting pg-boss's own retry/backoff handle transient failures, and letting a permanently-missing SMTP config exhaust its retries into a terminal `failed` state rather than retrying forever).
  - `QuotesService.send()` now enqueues a `quote-send-email` pg-boss job **after** its transaction commits (number allocated, status flipped to `sent` — the irreversible part is already durable at that point), and does **not** await the job's own completion. Enqueuing itself is wrapped in try/catch — even a pg-boss connection failure at enqueue time can't fail an already-committed send. This is the graceful-degradation rule from CLAUDE.md/`.env.example` ("if SMTP is down, documents still issue and email queues for retry") implemented for real, not just documented.
  - **Verified live, both outcomes**:
    - **SMTP unconfigured** (the actual local-dev default — `.env`'s `SMTP_HOST` is blank): called `POST /quotes/:id/send` against a real quote. The request returned successfully and instantly with `status: "sent"`, a real allocated `number` (`QUO-0001`), well before pg-boss's ~2s poll interval could even run the job — proving the HTTP response never waited on the email attempt. Watched the job in `pgboss.job` directly: it went `created` → `retry` (error: `"SMTP is not configured — cannot send quote email"`) → retried twice more on the configured exponential backoff → terminal `failed` state after exactly 3 attempts (`retry_count = 3 = retry_limit`). No crash, no hang, no infinite retry loop. `pdf_file_id` was still populated despite the email failure, confirming PDF persistence and the email attempt are independent, as designed.
    - **SMTP configured and reachable**: spun up an ephemeral local MailDev SMTP server (`docker run maildev/maildev`, ports 1025/1080) purely for this verification, pointed `SMTP_HOST=localhost SMTP_PORT=1025` at it for one API run, and sent a second real quote. The job reached pg-boss's terminal `completed` state, and MailDev's own server log recorded receipt: `"Received: quotes@acmefab.test -> jane.buyer@example.com, Subject: Acme Fabrication Co. sent you a quote (QUO-0002)"` — confirming the business name (from `business_profile`), the contact's real email, and the quote number all flowed correctly end-to-end into an actually-delivered email. MailDev container was torn down afterward; this was **not** a persistent addition to the dev stack, and no real-world/internet SMTP provider was exercised — only this ephemeral local server, which is as far as verification can honestly go in this environment.

- **Web** (`apps/web/src/app/quotes/quote-detail.tsx`): added a "Preview PDF" (draft) / "Download PDF" (sent) button next to Edit/Mark as sent, opening `GET /quotes/:id/pdf` directly in a new tab (the browser's own cookie session authenticates it, same pattern `business-logo-upload.tsx` already uses for `GET /files/:id` — `apiClient` isn't used here since it always JSON-parses responses and this route streams binary PDF). Updated `handleMarkAsSent`'s toast copy, which previously said "No email has been sent — that's coming in a later update" (now false): it honestly reflects that sending is **queued**, not synchronously guaranteed (`"${number} is now issued, and emailing it to ${contactName} has been queued."`) — since `send()` never awaits the email job, the toast cannot honestly claim delivery succeeded, only that issuing succeeded and sending was queued. `types.ts` gained `pdfFileId: string | null` on the `Quote` interface.

- **What was NOT built in Stage 2** (see "Next up" for the full, still-accurate picture): no admin-facing surface for a permanently-failed email job (pg-boss's own `pgboss.job` table is the only place that's visible right now); no dead-letter queue; no Resend/other email driver (SMTP/Nodemailer only, per scope); no accept/reject link in the email body (Stage 3's public route doesn't exist yet — the task brief explicitly allowed this); demo-data seeding (`DemoDataService`) now transitively exercises this whole pipeline too, since it calls the real `QuotesService.send()` rather than hand-inserting rows — not a regression, just worth noting since every future demo seed will now generate real PDFs and enqueue real (harmlessly-failing-without-SMTP) email jobs.

- `pnpm --filter @spine/shared typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck/lint/build`, and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck/lint/build` all pass. New dependencies: `@react-pdf/renderer`, `pg-boss`, `nodemailer` (+ `@types/nodemailer`), `react` (+ `@types/react`) — all added to `apps/api/package.json` (the PDF template is API-only; nothing in `packages/shared` needed it). Migration `apps/api/drizzle/0012_curly_tyrannus.sql` (single column + FK) generated via `drizzle-kit generate` and applied against local dev Postgres via `drizzle-kit migrate`.

### 2026-08-07 — Quotes (F3) Stage 1 of 3: data model, core CRUD, line items, versioning

- Third real business module in Phase 1 · Sales Spine, built against Contacts/Catalogue's established conventions (org-scoped schema, `AuditLogService` writes, `@OrgRoles`/`IdempotencyInterceptor` on mutating routes, hand-written web `types.ts` mirroring API JSON since `packages/sdk` is still empty). **Deliberately staged into three parts at the user's request** — this entry covers Stage 1 only: data model + core CRUD + line items + versioning (S-03.1, S-03.2, S-03.8, S-03.9, S-03.11). Stage 2 (PDF + email, S-03.3/S-03.4) and Stage 3 (public accept/reject link + view/accept/reject events + owner notifications, S-03.5/S-03.6/S-03.7) are explicitly **not** built — see "Next up" below for exactly what each covers. S-03.10 (quote→job/invoice conversion) and S-03.12 (mobile) are out of scope for all of Phase 1 per the PRD's own phase breakdown, not just this stage.
- **Schema** (`apps/api/src/db/schema/quotes.ts`, new file): `quotes` (org-scoped, `contactId` FK `onDelete: restrict`, nullable `number` populated only on send, `status` enum `draft/sent/accepted/rejected` — **no stored "expired" state**, computed at read time from `validUntil < now()` instead, see the schema file's own long comment for the full reasoning against a cron-driven status flip; `parentQuoteId` self-FK + `version` int for versioning per CLAUDE.md; `validUntil` date; `currency` + four bigint total columns always server-computed from lines, never client-supplied; `discountRequiresApproval` bool + `approvedByUserId`/`approvedAt` for S-03.9, computed and snapshotted at write time against the org's threshold so a later threshold change never retroactively changes whether an already-sent quote needed approval; `notes`/`termsAndConditions`; `sentAt`) and `quote_lines` (`quoteId` FK cascade, `sortOrder`, `type` enum `item/heading/text` for S-03.2, nullable `catalogItemId` FK `onDelete: set null` as **provenance only, never re-read for pricing**, full snapshot of name/description/quantity/unitPrice/discountPercentage/taxRatePercentage per CLAUDE.md's "documents snapshot their data" rule, plus server-computed `lineSubtotalAmount`/`lineTaxAmount`/`lineTotalAmount`). Discount is **percentage-only** (0-100, `real`), not a fixed-amount override — documented choice, matching `catalog.ts`'s own `defaultTaxRatePercentage` precedent. `quantity` is `real`, not bigint (a quantity isn't money). Registered in `apps/api/src/db/schema/index.ts`.
- **Migration**: generated via `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:generate` as `apps/api/drizzle/0010_fast_mad_thinker.sql` and **applied against the local dev Postgres** (`spine-dev-db-1`) with `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:migrate` — confirmed via `drizzle-kit migrate`'s own success output.
- **Shared zod schemas** (`packages/shared/src/schemas/quotes.ts`, new file): `quoteLineInputSchema` (one permissive shape covering all three line types rather than a discriminated union, matching the schema's "one table, one shape" design), `createQuoteSchema`/`updateQuoteSchema`. Money totals, `status`, `number`, `discountRequiresApproval`, `approvedByUserId`/`approvedAt` are all deliberately **absent** from the input schema — they're server-computed/transitioned via dedicated service methods, never client-writable fields, per CLAUDE.md's money rule. No boolean-`.default()`-inside-`.partial()` pitfall here (documented inline, following catalog.ts's own precedent) since no top-level boolean-with-default field exists on this schema.
- **`NumberingService.allocateNumber(tx, orgId, documentType)`** (new method, `apps/api/src/modules/numbering/numbering.service.ts`) — the first real caller of the gapless-numbering rule from CLAUDE.md: `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` row-locks the org's `number_sequences` row for `documentType` inside the caller's own open transaction, so a concurrent `send` on a second quote blocks until the first transaction commits/rolls back — no two documents of the same type in an org can ever receive the same number, and none is skipped. Creates a sequence row inline using this module's own `NUMBERING_DEFAULTS` if the org skipped the numbering onboarding step, rather than throwing.
- **API module** (`apps/api/src/modules/quotes/`, previously an empty `@Module({})` stub, now wired): `QuotesService` (list/getOne/create/update/send/revise/approveDiscount/delete, all org-scoped, every mutation inside `db.transaction(...)` with an `AuditLogService.record()` call in the same transaction) and `QuotesController` at `/quotes`. **Every route — including GETs — carries `@OrgRoles(["owner", "staff"])`** at the controller level, unlike Catalogue/Contacts: per PRD §2.1's permission table, "Create/edit quotes" is Owner ✅ / Staff ✅ / Field **❌** (no view access at all — verified by reading §2.1 directly, not assumed from Catalogue's different "Field: View" row for "Manage contacts & catalogue"). `POST /quotes/:id/approve-discount` further narrows to `@OrgRoles(["owner"])` at the method level (confirmed `nestjs-better-auth`'s `Reflector.getAllAndOverride([handler, class])` semantics mean handler-level metadata wins over the controller-level default, so this correctly restricts just that one route). `QuotesModule` imports `AuditModule` + `NumberingModule` + `OrgModule` (for `SettingsService`) and exports `QuotesService`.
- **Endpoints**: `GET /quotes` (status/contactId filters, returns computed `isExpired`/`isSuperseded` per row), `GET /quotes/:id` (includes `lines`, plus `isSuperseded`/`supersededByQuoteId`), `POST /quotes` (create draft, `number: null`), `PUT /quotes/:id` (**draft only** — a sent quote is immutable per CLAUDE.md, `update()` throws `BadRequestException` otherwise; full-replace on `lines`, matching Contacts' own nested-array convention), `POST /quotes/:id/send` (Stage 1's "issue" moment: allocates the number via `NumberingService.allocateNumber` and flips `draft → sent` inside one transaction; **no email side effect** — the controller/service both carry comments marking this as Stage 2's hook point; blocks if `discountRequiresApproval && !approvedByUserId`), `POST /quotes/:id/revise` (S-03.8: copies every line + field into a new draft row with `parentQuoteId`/`version + 1`, without touching the original row at all; blocks if the quote is still a draft, or has already been revised into a newer version), `POST /quotes/:id/approve-discount` (S-03.9, owner-only, sets `approvedByUserId`/`approvedAt` once, rejects if already approved or doesn't need approval), `DELETE /quotes/:id` (**draft only** — nothing references a draft yet; a sent/accepted/rejected quote can never be deleted, only stay in its status, per CLAUDE.md — mirrors Contacts/Catalogue's own hard-delete-when-safe reasoning).
- **S-03.9's threshold** is backed by the generic `settings` table (`SettingsService`, key `discount_approval_threshold`, shape `{ percentage: number }`), exactly mirroring `default_tax_rate`'s existing convention — no new dedicated table for one number. Default threshold (when unset) is 20%. `computeQuoteTotals()` in `quotes.service.ts` computes a blended discount percentage across all item lines (`discountAmount / preDiscountSubtotal * 100`) and compares it against the threshold on every create/update, storing the result in `discountRequiresApproval` rather than deriving it fresh at read time (see the schema comment on why this is a snapshot, not a live computation).
- **Tax/money math**: line totals use the existing shared `computeLineTax` (packages/shared/src/tax/tax.ts) — discount is applied to the pre-tax line amount before tax is computed (tax charged on the discounted price), rounded once at line level (`roundHalfUp`), then summed — never re-rounded at the document level. Verified against real numbers (see below).
- **Audit log**: extended `AuditEntityType` with `"quote"`/`"quote_line"`. Every create/update/send/revise/approve-discount/delete writes an `audit_log` row with `contactId` populated (a quote always has one, unlike Catalogue's `contactId: null`).
- **Web** (`apps/web/src/app/quotes/`, new directory): `page.tsx` (list route, stat cards: Total/Draft/Sent/Accepted, real counts derived client-side from the fetched list per this codebase's no-fabricated-stats rule), `quotes-explorer.tsx` (status filter including a client-side "Expired" filter matching on the API's computed `isExpired`), `quotes-table.tsx` (flat table, no view switcher, matching Catalogue's own proportionate-scope precedent), `quotes-skeleton.tsx`. **The quote builder is a full page, not a Drawer** (`/quotes/new`, `/quotes/:id/edit` via `quote-builder.tsx` + `quote-edit-loader.tsx`) — deliberate choice per the task brief's own prompt to reason about it: a multi-line editable table with add/remove/reorder, a contact picker, and live totals needs real width/height that a Drawer's fixed-width panel would cramp. `quote-line-editor.tsx` (add item/heading/text lines, per-line quantity/price/discount/tax for item lines, remove, reorder via up/down buttons — no drag-and-drop library pulled in, since a bounded per-quote line list doesn't need one and buttons stay fully keyboard-operable for free; a Combobox to prefill an item line from an existing catalog item, snapshotting its fields immediately rather than keeping any live reference) computes an instant client-side total preview using the **same shared `computeLineTax`** the server uses, so the number shown while typing matches what the server will store — the server still recomputes and is the sole source of truth on every save. `contact-picker.tsx` (Combobox over `/contacts`, archived contacts excluded). `quote-detail.tsx` (read-only view with line breakdown + totals; "Edit" for a draft; **"Mark as sent"**, not "Send" — the button/toast copy explicitly says no email has been sent, so Stage 1 never implies functionality that doesn't exist yet; "Revise" for a sent/accepted/rejected, not-yet-superseded quote; an owner-only "Approve discount" affordance shown when `discountRequiresApproval && !approvedByUserId`, gated by fetching `/org/members` client-side and matching the current session's `user.id` against the roster's `role` — the only existing endpoint that exposes a member's own org role to a non-owner, confirmed unrestricted by `@OrgRoles` in `members.controller.ts`; server-side enforcement is the real gate regardless, via `@OrgRoles(["owner"])` on `POST /quotes/:id/approve-discount`). Added a `/quotes` entry to `apps/web/src/lib/nav-sections.tsx`'s "Sales" section.
- **Verified live end-to-end against the local dev Postgres**, bypassing HTTP auth by driving `QuotesService` directly in-process against a real seeded org/contact (no browser automation tooling exists in this environment — see the standard caveat below): created a 3-line draft (heading + a 10-unit item with 10% discount/8% tax + a text note) and confirmed exact totals (subtotal 1500.00 → discount 150.00 → tax 108.00 on the discounted 1350.00 → total 1458.00, matching hand-calculated expected values); created a second draft with a 50% line discount and confirmed `discountRequiresApproval: true`; confirmed `send()` **rejects** with the correct message before approval; confirmed `approveDiscount()` then `send()` succeeds and allocates `QUO-0001`; sent the first draft too and got `QUO-0002` (gapless, sequential, confirming the row-lock allocator works under real sequential use); confirmed `update()` on the now-sent quote **throws** ("Only a draft quote can be edited..."); confirmed `revise()` creates a new draft (`version: 2`, correct `parentQuoteId`, 3 lines copied) and the original quote's `getOne()` now reports `isSuperseded: true`/`supersededByQuoteId` correctly; confirmed a second `revise()` call on the same original **throws** ("already been revised"); confirmed `list()` returns the right `isExpired`/`isSuperseded` flags across all four quotes; confirmed a draft `delete()` succeeds and a sent-quote `delete()` **throws**. Confirmed every action produced the expected `audit_log` row (`created`/`sent`/`revised`/`discount_approved`/`deleted`, all with `entity_type: 'quote'` and a real `contact_id`). Test data and audit rows were deleted afterward and the `quote` number sequence reset to keep the dev DB clean for future manual testing.
- **Also verified**: `pnpm --filter @spine/shared typecheck` (clean), `pnpm --filter @spine/shared test` (22/22 existing tests still pass, untouched by this change), `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck` (clean), `pnpm --filter @spine/api lint` (clean after removing one unused import), `pnpm --filter @spine/api build` (clean), `pnpm --filter @spine/api test` (no unit tests exist yet for this module — none were added in Stage 1; the live in-process smoke test above substitutes for now, but proper `*.test.ts`/`*.integration.test.ts` coverage per CLAUDE.md's testing-expectations section is still owed, most importantly a ledger-integrity-style test for `computeQuoteTotals`'s rounding behavior and the discount-threshold boundary), `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck` (clean), `pnpm --filter @spine/web lint` (clean), `pnpm --filter @spine/web build` (clean — confirms `/quotes`, `/quotes/new`, `/quotes/:id`, `/quotes/:id/edit` are all real routes in the production build output). **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing was performed (no browser automation tooling exists in this environment) — the quote builder's line-editor interactions (add/remove/reorder buttons, catalog-item prefill via Combobox, live total preview, DatePicker) and the detail page's action buttons have not been exercised through an actual UI session, only confirmed to compile, lint, and build cleanly against the real schema/API shape, with the underlying service logic verified directly as described above.
- **Not built** (Stage 1 scope boundary, all explicitly deferred to Stage 2/3 per the task brief — see "Next up" for the full breakdown): PDF rendering/preview (S-03.3), email sending (S-03.4), the public accept/reject link (S-03.5/S-03.6), view/accept/reject event tracking, owner notifications on view/accept (S-03.7), and quote→job/invoice conversion (S-03.10, blocked on Jobs/Invoices not existing yet regardless of this stage). No unit-test file was added for `computeQuoteTotals`/`computeLineTax` usage or the discount-threshold logic — flagged above as still owed.
- **Post-build correctness review, same session — four real bugs found and fixed before this stage was considered done**, via `code-review` against `quotes.service.ts`/`quotes.controller.ts`/`quotes.ts`/`quotes` zod schema/`numbering.service.ts`, each confirmed against the actual code (not taken on faith) and then fixed:
  1. **Currency mismatch**: `computeQuoteTotals` computed tax/totals treating every line as the quote's own `currency`, but persisted whatever `unitPriceCurrency` the client sent (`unitPriceCurrency` is an independently-settable optional field on `quoteLineInputSchema`, with no validation tying it to the document currency). A line could end up mislabeled with a currency its amount was never actually computed in. Fixed: the persisted `unitPriceCurrency` is now always forced to the quote's own `currency`, never trusted from the client — correct per CLAUDE.md's v1 no-multi-currency non-goal, since a line can never legitimately differ from its own document's currency.
  2. **Stale discount approval survives a line edit**: `update()` recomputed `discountRequiresApproval` on every line change but never cleared `approvedByUserId`/`approvedAt` — an owner's approval of a 25% discount would silently still count as approval after staff raised it to 40%, since `send()` only checks `discountRequiresApproval && !approvedByUserId`. Fixed: any edit to `lines` now clears both fields, requiring fresh approval before the draft can be sent again (S-03.9).
  3. **`send()`/`revise()`/`approveDiscount()` raced on their own status guards**: each read-checked the quote's status *before* opening its transaction, then wrote unconditionally inside it — two concurrent calls (e.g. two staff both clicking "send") could both pass the guard. For `send()` specifically this meant two gapless numbers allocated but only one stored (the loser's orphaned, a visible gap in the sequence) and two "sent" audit rows for one send; for `revise()`, two competing next-version drafts (`quotes_parent_idx` is a plain, non-unique index, so nothing at the DB level blocked it either). Fixed: all three methods now re-select the quote with `.for("update")` *inside* the transaction and run every guard against that locked row, mirroring `NumberingService.allocateNumber`'s own established row-lock pattern.
  4. **`AuditLogService.diffFields` crashed on `update()`'s own real code path** (not just a review nit — this one only surfaced by actually exercising `update()` against a live line edit during the fixes above): `input` still contained `lines` (an array with bigint `unitPriceAmount` fields) when passed into `diffFields(existing, input)`. Since `quotes` has no `lines` column, this always produced a nonsensical `"lines: undefined -> [...]"` change entry *and* that entry's bigint failed to `JSON.stringify` into `audit_log.changes` (a jsonb column) — every `PUT /quotes/:id` that touched lines would throw. Fixed by destructuring `lines` out before diffing, mirroring exactly how `contacts.service.ts`'s own `update()` already excludes `contactPersons`/`addresses` for the identical two reasons (not a real column, not diffable/serializable as a scalar).
  - **Re-verified live** against the real local dev Postgres after all four fixes, via a temporary in-process script (`QuotesService` instantiated directly, not through HTTP) exercising: a line with a deliberately mismatched `unitPriceCurrency` (confirmed normalized to the quote's currency in the persisted row), approve → edit lines → confirm `approvedByUserId` cleared → confirm `send()` now correctly re-blocks on the stale-cleared approval, a normal `send()` (confirmed real gapless number `QUO-0001` allocated) followed by a second `send()` on the same now-sent quote (confirmed cleanly rejected, proving the in-transaction re-check fires), and a `revise()` followed by a second `revise()` on the same quote (confirmed cleanly rejected). All six checks passed. The script and its test rows were deleted/cleaned up afterward — nothing left in the dev DB. `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` re-run clean across `@spine/shared`/`@spine/api`/`@spine/web` after the fixes (same clean result as the pre-fix run above, now covering the corrected code).
  - True concurrent-request races (two simultaneous HTTP calls hitting the row lock at the same instant) were not reproduced under actual concurrency — verifying the lock exists and the guards now run inside it, plus the sequential double-call rejections above, is the practical verification available without a load-testing harness in this environment; the row-lock pattern itself is identical to `NumberingService.allocateNumber`'s own already-relied-upon mechanism.

### 2026-08-07 — Demo data extended to cover Quotes (F3 Stage 1)

- Same session as Stage 1's post-build review — the user asked for demo quotes before moving to Stage 2, both so the onboarding "load demo data" flow keeps its promise (§S-12.8's demo/sample data offer) and so Quotes has something real to look at without hand-creating documents first.
- **`quotes` gained an `isDemo` boolean column** (`apps/api/src/db/schema/quotes.ts`, migration `apps/api/drizzle/0011_rare_magneto.sql`, applied to the local dev Postgres), matching `contacts.isDemo`/`catalogItems.isDemo`'s exact convention (`index("quotes_org_demo_idx")` included). `quote_lines` needs no column of its own — it cascades with its quote, same reasoning `leads`/`activities`/`contact_persons`/`addresses` already established relative to `contacts`.
- **Reuses real `QuotesService` methods rather than hand-inserting rows** — a deliberate departure from Contacts/Catalogue's own "direct insert, skip the service" seeding style. `QuotesService.create()` gained a new `isDemo = false` parameter (threaded through the private `persistDraft`, never exposed via `createQuoteSchema`/the controller — not client-settable) so `DemoDataService.seedQuotes()` can call the exact same totals/discount-threshold/audit-log logic a real create does. `QuotesService.revise()`'s insert was also fixed to carry `isDemo: existing.isDemo` forward — previously it silently didn't, which would have left a demo quote's revision as a non-demo row `deleteAll` couldn't find (caught during this session's own live verification, not shipped).
- **Five seed quotes** (`DEMO_QUOTES` in `demo-data.service.ts`), spanning every state Stage 1's own public methods can actually reach — no `accepted`/`rejected` quotes are fabricated, since those transitions only exist via Stage 3's not-yet-built public accept/reject action, and hand-writing that status would misrepresent what this stage of the product can do:
  - 2 plain drafts (one of which is deliberately left **unapproved above the default 20% discount-approval threshold**, S-03.9 — its blended discount, computed the same way `QuotesService` computes it — is ~21.3%, comfortably over 20% but not so aggressively discounted that it reads as unrealistic; sending it correctly still fails until approved, exercising the approval gate honestly rather than bypassing it).
  - 2 sent quotes (numbered for real via `NumberingService.allocateNumber`, so demo data shows genuine `QUO-000N` numbers, not fake ones).
  - 1 sent-then-revised quote (S-03.8), showing versioning/supersession in the demo set — a real second `send()`-eligible draft exists with `parentQuoteId` pointing at the original, matching what a real revise produces.
  - Line items are drawn from `DEMO_CATALOG_ITEMS` by array index and resolved to real ids **by name**, not by array position — a multi-row `INSERT ... RETURNING` has no documented guarantee of preserving VALUES-list order in Postgres, so trusting `insertedItems[i]` to correspond to `DEMO_CATALOG_ITEMS[i]` would have been a latent correctness bug under a future Postgres version or planner change, not just a hypothetical one.
- **`seedQuotes` runs after the existing contacts/catalog-items `db.transaction(...)` commits, not inside it** — `QuotesService.create`/`.send`/`.revise` each open their own internal transactions, which cannot nest inside an already-open one. A failure partway through the 5 seed quotes leaves whichever ones already succeeded in place rather than rolling back the whole batch; judged acceptable for demo data specifically (re-running seed only ever adds more rows, and `deleteAll` cleans up by `isDemo` regardless of how many quotes made it through) — this tradeoff would NOT be acceptable for a real financial document flow, but nothing here posts to the ledger.
- **`deleteAll` now deletes demo quotes FIRST, before demo contacts** — `quotes.contactId` is `onDelete: "restrict"` (per quotes.ts's own schema comment), so the pre-existing contact-then-catalog-item deletion order would have thrown a raw Postgres FK-violation error the moment a demo contact with a demo quote was deleted, the instant this feature landed, if quotes weren't deleted first. Caught and fixed during this session's own live verification pass (see below), not shipped broken. `status()` also now checks for a demo quote in addition to a demo contact/item, for consistency (defensive — every current seed run produces all three together, so this doesn't change observed behavior today, but keeps the "any demo data exists" check honest if that ever changes).
- **`WelcomeStep`'s doc comment updated** (`apps/web/src/app/onboarding/steps/welcome-step.tsx`) to describe the 5 demo quotes instead of saying Quotes isn't seeded — no UI copy change was needed (the button/screen text was already generic "load demo data," not enumerating modules).
- **Verified live**, not just typechecked: a temporary in-process script (`QuotesService`/`DemoDataService` instantiated directly against the real local dev Postgres, deleted after the run — no trace left in the repo or the dev DB) exercised the full seed → verify → delete cycle end to end and caught two real bugs before they shipped (both described above: `revise()` not carrying `isDemo` forward, and `deleteAll`'s FK-violation ordering). Confirmed: `status()` flips true after seed and false after delete; exactly 6 demo quote rows exist after seeding (5 seed entries + 1 revision row); 3 are `sent` with real sequential `QUO-000N` numbers; 1 has `parentQuoteId` set; the unapproved high-discount draft exists and correctly still blocks `send()` until approved; `deleteAll` completes without a foreign-key error and leaves zero demo quotes, contacts, or catalog items behind. `pnpm typecheck`/`lint`/`build` re-run clean across `@spine/shared`/`@spine/api`/`@spine/web` after every change in this entry (matching the pre-change clean baseline from Stage 1's own entry above).
- **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing (no browser automation tooling in this environment) — the onboarding "load demo data" button and the Settings "delete all demo data" button were not clicked through an actual UI session; only the underlying service methods they call were exercised directly, as described above.
- Stage 2/3 scope (PDF/email, public accept/reject link, notifications) is unaffected by this session — still exactly as described in "Next up" from Stage 1's own entry.

### 2026-08-07 — Quotes table row-actions menu (user-reported gap)

- User reported: the quotes list table had no per-row "more" menu, unlike Contacts/Catalogue — Stage 1's `quotes-table.tsx` only linked the Number column to the detail page, with no `QuoteRowActions`-equivalent component at all (a real gap against the established pattern, not a misunderstanding — Catalogue/Contacts both ship one).
- New `apps/web/src/app/quotes/quote-row-actions.tsx`, following `CatalogRowActions`' exact structure: a dropdown with **View** (redundant with the Number-column link, but expected for anyone scanning the row menu rather than clicking the cell directly), **Edit** (draft only — routes to `/quotes/:id/edit`, matching `QuoteDetail`'s own `canEdit` guard), and **Delete** (draft only, behind the same `AlertDialog` confirmation pattern, calling the pre-existing `DELETE /quotes/:id`). Unlike Catalogue, there's no Archive/Restore action — quotes have no such state (a draft is either edited/sent/deleted; a sent quote is immutable and can only be revised via the detail page's own "Revise" button, already built in Stage 1).
- `quotes-table.tsx` gained a trailing `actions` column (empty header, right-aligned, matching `catalog-table.tsx`'s exact convention) rendering `QuoteRowActions`; `quotes-explorer.tsx` gained a `handleDeleted` callback (reload the list + show a toast via the pre-existing, previously-unused `useEntityToast` hook from `entity-toast.tsx`) wired through as the new `onDeleted` prop both components now require.
- **Verified**: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; the production build's `/quotes` route grew from 3.37 kB to 4.23 kB, consistent with the new component actually being bundled in. **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing — the dropdown/confirmation dialog/toast were not clicked through an actual UI session, only confirmed to compile and build against the real API shape, matching every prior session's honesty convention in this file.

### 2026-08-07 — Demo data (onboarding load + Settings delete) and a dev DB reset script

- `contacts.isDemo`/`catalog_items.isDemo` (nullable-`false`-default `boolean`, indexed alongside `orgId`) added to both schemas — migration `apps/api/drizzle/0009_loud_calypso.sql`, applied against local dev Postgres. Marks rows created by the demo-data seeder so a later bulk-delete only ever touches those, never real records.
- New `apps/api/src/modules/demo-data/` module: `DemoDataService.seed(orgId, actorUserId)` inserts 14 sample contacts (mixed customer/vendor/both, company/individual) and 15 sample catalogue items (construction/fabrication-flavoured — labour, materials, electrical, plumbing, equipment rental, etc.), all tagged `isDemo: true`, with the same `audit_log` "created" entries a real user create would produce. **Every demo contact also gets one contact person, one address, one lead (varied stage/value/source), and one activity** (call/meeting/note/task, some overdue-and-done, some upcoming), so the contact detail page's Persons/Addresses/Leads/Activities tabs have real content instead of an empty shell. Catalogue items pick up the org's actual `currency`/`default_tax_rate` settings rather than hardcoding USD. `deleteAll(orgId, actorUserId)` hard-deletes every `isDemo`-tagged contact/catalog item (defensively skips any contact that somehow gained ledger references first); `leads`/`activities`/`contact_persons`/`addresses` carry no `isDemo` column of their own — they FK to `contacts.id` with `onDelete: "cascade"`, so deleting a demo contact already removes them transitively, verified live (14 leads + 14 activities gone after deleting 14 demo contacts, zero orphans). `status(orgId)` returns `{ hasDemoData: boolean }`. Controller: `GET /demo-data/status` (any member), `POST /demo-data/seed` (owner+staff, matching Contacts/Catalogue's own create bar), `DELETE /demo-data` (owner-only, matching `SettingsController`'s "only the owner changes org-wide config" rule — bulk delete is at least as consequential).
- **Onboarding**: `WelcomeStep`'s "Just show me around" button now calls `POST /demo-data/seed` before marking onboarding skipped (`onboarding/page.tsx`'s new `handleUseDemoDefaults`), replacing the prior placeholder behavior (which only applied system defaults) documented in the component's own comment as deferred until Contacts/Catalogue existed — they now do.
- **Settings**: new "Demo data" pane (`apps/web/src/app/settings/panes/data-pane.tsx`, wired into `settings-dialog.tsx`'s section list) shows whether demo data is loaded and offers "Delete all demo data" behind an `AlertDialog` confirmation (irreversible, so gated per that component's own doc comment). Calls `DELETE /demo-data`.
- **Dev DB reset**: `apps/api/src/db/reset.ts` (`pnpm --filter @spine/api db:reset`, or `pnpm db:reset:dev` from the root) — `TRUNCATE ... RESTART IDENTITY CASCADE` across every app table in one statement, for the common "wipe my local data, keep the schema/container" case. Deliberately CLI-only, not an API endpoint or app-UI action (it drops every org/user, not just demo data — see the app-facing, org-scoped `demo-data` module above for that). Faster than the pre-existing `pnpm dev:infra:reset`, which tears down and recreates the whole Postgres container.
- Verified live end-to-end against local dev Postgres: signed up a test org, `POST /demo-data/seed` → 14 contacts + 15 catalog items created, `GET /demo-data/status` → `true`, `DELETE /demo-data` → both counts removed, status back to `false`. `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint` clean across all 6 packages.

### 2026-08-07 — Catalogue (F2): goods/services with default price, unit, tax rate and account mapping

- Second real business module in Phase 1 · Sales Spine, built directly against Contacts & CRM (F1)'s established conventions (org-scoped schema, soft-delete via `archivedAt`, `AuditLogService` writes, `@OrgRoles`/`IdempotencyInterceptor` on mutating routes, no role restriction on GETs, zod `createXSchema`/`updateXSchema = createXSchema.partial()` shared between API and web). Covers PRD F2's MUST/SHOULD/MAY scope and S-02.1 → S-02.4.
- **Schema** (`apps/api/src/db/schema/catalog.ts`, new file): `catalog_items` table — `name`, `description`, `unit` (free text, not an enum — service/fabrication businesses use widely varied units and PRD doesn't call out a fixed list), `category` (free text, MAY-level per PRD, no separate categories table), `defaultPriceAmount`/`defaultPriceCurrency` (bigint minor units + currency code, the exact `leads.valueAmount`/`valueCurrency` shape, per CLAUDE.md's non-negotiable money rule), `defaultTaxRatePercentage` (a plain `real`, not bigint — a rate, not money, matching `tax.controller.ts`'s existing `DefaultTaxRate.percentage: number` shape; there is no `tax_rates` table yet, that's FEAT-08/Phase 4, explicitly out of scope), nullable `incomeAccountId`/`expenseAccountId` FKs to the existing `accounts` table (`onDelete: "restrict"`, mirroring `journal_lines.accountId`'s own FK behavior), and `archivedAt` for soft-delete. Indexes on `orgId`, `(orgId, category)`, `(orgId, name)`, and both account FKs. Registered in `apps/api/src/db/schema/index.ts`.
- **Migration**: generated via `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:generate` as `apps/api/drizzle/0008_flat_ma_gnuci.sql` (checked `_journal.json` first — prior idx was 7, no collision) and **applied against the local dev Postgres** (`spine-dev-db-1`, brought up via `pnpm dev:infra:up`) with `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:migrate` — verified live with `\d catalog_items` inside the container, confirmed all columns/indexes/FKs match the schema.
- **Shared zod schemas** (`packages/shared/src/schemas/catalog.ts`, new file): `createCatalogItemSchema`/`updateCatalogItemSchema`. Deliberately has **no** `z.boolean().default(...)` field, to avoid repeating the pre-existing `.partial()`-doesn't-neutralize-`.default()` bug already documented for `contacts.ts` in this changelog — noted inline in the schema file's own comment as a guardrail for whoever adds a boolean flag here later. `defaultPriceAmount` uses `z.coerce.bigint()`, matching `createLeadSchema.valueAmount`'s own convention.
- **API module** (`apps/api/src/modules/catalog/`): `CatalogService` (list/getOne/create/update/archive/restore/delete, all org-scoped) and `CatalogController` at `catalog-items` (`GET /catalog-items`, `GET /catalog-items/:id`, `POST /catalog-items`, `PUT /catalog-items/:id`, `POST /catalog-items/:id/archive`, `POST /catalog-items/:id/restore`, `DELETE /catalog-items/:id`). Every mutating route carries `@OrgRoles(["owner", "staff"])` + `@UseInterceptors(IdempotencyInterceptor)`; GETs carry neither, per §2.1's "Manage contacts & catalogue: Owner/Staff mutate, Field reads" permission row. `CatalogModule` (previously an empty `@Module({})` stub) now wires `CatalogController`/`CatalogService`, imports `AuditModule`, and exports `CatalogService` for a future Quotes module to depend on. `AppModule` already imported `CatalogModule` from a prior session — no change needed there.
- **`POST /catalog-items` is self-contained by design** (S-02.4 — "add an item inline while building a quote"): it takes only the org id and the create payload, no quote/document context of any kind, so a future Quotes line-item builder can call it directly from an inline "+ New item" affordance without any change to this endpoint. Documented in `CatalogService.create`'s own comment.
- **Audit log**: extended `AuditEntityType` (`apps/api/src/modules/audit/audit-log.service.ts`) with `"catalog_item"`. Every create/update/archive/restore/delete writes an `audit_log` row with `contactId: null` (catalog items have no owning contact — the column exists specifically for the five Contacts entity types, per `audit.ts`'s schema comment, now updated to explain the non-Contacts case). There is no `GET /catalog-items/:id/history` endpoint or web history view — out of this task's scope, matching how Contacts' own audit rollout started with writes-only-plus-one-endpoint rather than a system-wide view; see "Next up" for the still-open system-wide rollout.
- **`DELETE /catalog-items/:id` is intentionally unguarded** right now — unlike `ContactsService.delete()` (which blocks on `journal_lines` references before allowing a hard delete), nothing FKs to `catalog_items` yet since Quotes/Invoices (the future line-item holders) don't exist. This is flagged both in `CatalogService.delete()`'s own comment and in "Next up" below for whoever builds Quotes/Invoices next.
- **Web** (`apps/web/src/app/catalog/`, new directory): `page.tsx` (route, `?new=1` auto-opens the create Drawer, matching `contacts/page.tsx`), `catalog-explorer.tsx` (client state: status filter, load/refresh, toasts), `catalog-table.tsx` (the only list view — no card/list-view switcher like Contacts, since F2's scope is a flat CRUD list, kept proportionate per the task brief), `catalog-item-drawer.tsx` (one Drawer handling both create and edit — pass `item` to edit, omit to create; unlike Contacts there's no separate full detail page, Edit opens the same Drawer in place), `catalog-form.tsx` (name/description/unit/category/price/currency/tax-rate/income-account/expense-account; pre-fills currency from `GET /org/settings/currency` and tax rate from `GET /org/tax/default-rate` **only for a brand-new item** — an existing item's stored values always win; account pickers call the pre-existing `GET /org/accounts` endpoint, grouped by type, filtered to `income`/`expense`), `catalog-row-actions.tsx` (Edit/Archive-Restore/Delete dropdown + delete confirmation, mirroring `contact-row-actions.tsx`), `catalog-skeleton.tsx`, `entity-toast.tsx` (duplicated from Contacts' own small hook rather than extracted — no cross-module coupling reason to share a ~20-line hook yet), `types.ts` (hand-written `CatalogItem`/`Account`/`AccountsByType` types mirroring the API's JSON shape, same convention as `contacts/types.ts` since `packages/sdk` is still empty).
- Reused `@spine/ui`'s existing `MoneyInput` component (already built, previously only used in `apps/design-system`'s docs and nowhere in a real feature) for the default-price field — right-aligned, currency/minor-unit-aware, rejects keystrokes producing more fractional digits than the currency supports.
- Added a `/catalog` entry to `apps/web/src/lib/nav-sections.tsx`'s "Sales" section, alongside Contacts, with a `Package` icon (lucide-react).
- **Verified**: `pnpm --filter @spine/shared typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @spine/api lint`, `pnpm --filter @spine/api build`, `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @spine/web lint`, and `pnpm --filter @spine/web build` all pass (the web build confirms `/catalog` is a registered route in the production build output). Also started the built API (`pnpm start`) against the local dev Postgres and confirmed `GET /catalog-items` returns `401` (auth-gated, as expected without a session cookie) rather than a 404 or a startup crash — positive confirmation the module is wired into the running app, not just type-clean. **Not verified**: no browser-interactive testing was performed (no browser automation tooling exists in this environment) — the create/edit Drawer, account-picker population, and MoneyInput behavior have not been exercised through an actual UI session, only confirmed to compile, lint, and build cleanly against the real schema/API shape.
- **Not built** (explicitly out of scope per the task brief): Quotes (F3) itself, a `tax_rates` table / multi-rate tax-group CRUD (FEAT-08), a `GET /catalog-items/:id/history` endpoint or web history view, and any delete-guard on `catalog_items` (nothing references it yet to guard against). Line-level price override (S-02.2) needed no work here — the model already keeps `defaultPriceAmount` uncoupled from any document; a future quote/invoice line simply stores and can override its own price at add-time, which nothing in this schema prevents or entangles.

### 2026-08-06 — Formal WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit-and-fix pass across `@spine/ui`

- The audit-and-fix pass from "Next up" above (`A formal accessibility (WCAG 2.1 AA) pass per component`), now resolved and removed from that list. Scope: all 69 components in `packages/ui/src/components/`, plus the design-token layer they draw from (`packages/config/tailwind-preset.css`) and `docs/DESIGN.md`'s spec. `apps/api` was not touched (out of scope per the task brief); `apps/design-system` doc pages were updated only where a component's API/behavior genuinely changed.
- **Contrast, token-level (the highest-leverage fixes — one CSS value change cascades to every consumer)**: computed real WCAG contrast ratios (relative-luminance formula, not eyeballed) for every text/background token pair declared in `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css`, in both light and dark mode. Six light-mode pairs failed the 4.5:1 body-text minimum and are now fixed at the token level (dark mode was already compliant everywhere checked):
  - `--color-neutral-500` (backs `--color-on-surface-muted`, used across 43 component files, and `--color-draft`): `#8a8578` → `#726c5a`. Was 3.68:1 on `--color-surface` and 3.26:1 on `--color-draft-bg` — both real, live failures on every muted/secondary text label and every "Draft" status chip in the app. Now 5.24:1 / 4.64:1 (worst case).
  - `--color-paid`: `#1a7f4b` → `#177044` (was 4.44:1 on `--color-paid-bg`, now 5.40:1) — StatusChip's "Paid" chip renders at 12px, so the 4.5:1 threshold applies in full (no large-text exception).
  - `--color-category-teal`: `#0f7c7c` → `#0d6e6e` (was 4.41:1 on its `-bg`, now 5.33:1) — `calendar.tsx` renders event-title text in this color.
  - `--color-category-pink`: `#c4408a` → `#a63571` (was 3.93:1, now 5.19:1) — same calendar usage.
  - `docs/DESIGN.md`'s own color spec table updated to match every changed hex, with an inline comment recording the before/after ratio, so the doc and the shipped CSS custom properties stay in sync (they'd drifted apart before: the doc asserted "financial-status colors are independently verified per mode for 4.5:1" while `draft`/`paid`/`category-teal`/`category-pink` in light mode did not actually clear it).
- **A real, live dark-mode bug in Toast/Tooltip — not a contrast tweak, a broken pairing**: `toast.tsx` and `tooltip.tsx` both used `bg-neutral-900` + hardcoded `text-white`/`text-neutral-300`/`text-neutral-400`, intending permanently-dark chrome regardless of the app's theme (matching `docs/DESIGN.md`'s own toast/tooltip spec: `backgroundColor: neutral-900, textColor: #FFFFFF`). But `--color-neutral-900` is theme-*reactive* — it's also `--color-on-surface`, so in dark mode it resolves to `#f3f2ee` (near-white). White text on that background computed to **1.12:1** — every toast and every tooltip in the entire app was effectively unreadable in dark mode. Fixed by introducing three new theme-*invariant* tokens (`--color-inverse-surface: #211f1a`, `--color-inverse-on-surface: #ffffff`, `--color-inverse-on-surface-muted: #d3d0c9`, declared once in `:root` with no dark-mode override, so they hold the same value in both themes) and repointing both components at them instead of the reactive `neutral-900`/hardcoded whites. Also added a missing focus-visible ring to `ToastClose` while in that file (it had none).
- **Other real per-component fixes** (grouped by category; each verified against Radix's own source/types first, per the task's "don't add ARIA that duplicates what Radix already provides" instruction, so these are gaps Radix genuinely leaves to the wrapper):
  - *Forms*: `FormField`'s render-prop previously dropped `required` entirely — a screen reader user had no way to know a field was required beyond a purely visual asterisk. Now passes `aria-required`/`required`/`aria-invalid` through to the control, and the visual asterisk is `aria-hidden` (decorative only, not the sole carrier of the information). `HelperText` now sets `role="alert"` when rendering an error, so validation messages are announced when they appear. `Input`/`Textarea`/`MoneyInput` now self-set `aria-invalid` from their existing `error` prop when the caller doesn't pass `aria-invalid` explicitly (previously `error` only changed the border color).
  - *Custom listbox-style pickers with no Radix Combobox primitive underneath* (`Combobox`, `MultiSelect` — composed from Popover + cmdk/a hand-rolled listbox, not Radix's own Combobox, which doesn't exist) and `DatePicker`: trigger buttons were missing `aria-expanded`/`aria-haspopup`, so assistive tech had no way to know these were disclosure triggers or whether they were open. Fixed on all three.
  - *`calendar-mini.tsx` (hand-rolled, no Radix primitive)*: day-grid cells and the prev/next-month buttons had zero focus-visible treatment at all; no `aria-current="date"` for today; no `aria-selected` for the selected day (color alone conveyed selection); day buttons' only accessible name was the bare day-of-month number ("14"), no month/year/weekday context. Added `role="grid"`/`role="gridcell"`, full `aria-label`s (e.g. "Tuesday, August 4, 2026"), `aria-current`/`aria-selected`, and focus-visible rings throughout.
  - *`calendar.tsx` (hand-rolled Outlook-style scheduler, no Radix primitive)*: already had solid keyboard support in most places (roving-tabindex month grid with arrow keys, `role="button"`+`onKeyDown` event blocks with full `aria-label`s, `aria-pressed` color swatches) — two real gaps found and fixed: the month-view day-number button and per-day event-chip buttons had no focus-visible ring of their own (relying on inconsistent browser defaults against tinted backgrounds).
  - *`Sidebar`'s `SidebarNavItem` and `AppShell`'s `AppShellSidebar`*: when the sidebar is collapsed to its icon-only rail, the text label was fully unmounted (`{!collapsed && <span>...}`), leaving a collapsed nav item with zero accessible name — an icon with nothing for a screen reader to announce. Fixed in both call sites by keeping the label in the DOM with `sr-only` instead of unmounting it.
  - *`Table`'s sortable column headers*: no `aria-sort` on the `<th>` (so the current sort direction wasn't exposed to assistive tech) and no focus-visible ring on the sort button. Both fixed. `Pagination`'s prev/next/page-number buttons had no focus-visible ring at all — fixed.
  - *`Breadcrumb`*: the current/last crumb had no `aria-current="page"` (only a visual font-weight difference); its links had no focus-visible ring. Both fixed.
  - *Missing focus-visible rings on several Radix-wrapped triggers that had `outline-none`/`focus:outline-none` with no replacement*: `NavigationMenuTrigger`, `MenubarTrigger` (both real gaps — Tab-focusing an unopened trigger had zero visible indicator), `AccordionTrigger`, `TabsTrigger`/`TabsContent`, `PasswordToggleFieldToggle`, `ResizableHandle` (a 1px-wide drag handle, given `focus-visible:bg-primary` rather than a ring — a ring reads oddly on something that thin; `react-resizable-panels` itself already supplies `role="separator"`/`aria-valuenow`/keyboard arrow-key resizing, confirmed by reading its source, so only the missing visual indicator needed fixing).
  - *`Spinner`*: `aria-label="Loading"` was set directly on the bare `<svg>` (via lucide's `Loader2`) — an SVG has no implicit ARIA role, so an `aria-label` there isn't reliably exposed as an accessible name by every screen reader. Now wrapped in a `role="status"` span carrying the label (default "Loading", now an overridable `label` prop), with the icon itself `aria-hidden`.
  - *`Alert`*: all four variants (info/warning/critical/success) share the exact same `text-on-surface` text color — only the background tint differs — and `icon` was optional with no default; a `grep` across `apps/web` confirmed every real call site (`team-pane`, `signup`, `contacts-explorer`, `contact-detail` ×2, `contact-form`, `login`, `business-profile-step`, `invite-team-step`) omits the icon prop, meaning every error/warning banner in the shipped app was conveying its severity by background color alone. Fixed by giving `Alert` a default icon per variant (Info/AlertTriangle/AlertCircle/CheckCircle2) that only a `null` icon prop suppresses, plus `role="alert"` for `critical` and `role="status"` for the other three so banners are actually announced.
  - *`Carousel`*: no `role="region"`/`aria-roledescription="carousel"` on the root and no `role="group"`/`aria-roledescription="slide"` on items (a screen reader user had no indication this was a carousel or which slide was showing); embla-carousel's scroll-snap animation duration didn't respect `prefers-reduced-motion` (it's a JS/physics-driven scroll, not a CSS transition, so it's invisible to Tailwind's motion tokens and needed handling via `matchMedia` + Embla's own `duration` option). Both fixed.
- **Verified — real, not claimed**: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck` (pass), `pnpm --filter @spine/ui lint` (pass), `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck` (pass), `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system build` (pass, all 83 static routes generated). Also ran `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck` as an extra check since `apps/web` is a real `@spine/ui` consumer and `Alert`/`Spinner` both gained new (backward-compatible, additive-only) behavior — passes clean, confirming no regression. Contrast ratios were computed with a real relative-luminance/WCAG-formula script (not estimated), run against every token pair actually used as text-on-background or text-on-tinted-background in the codebase, cross-checked by grepping for which components actually consume `category-pink`/`category-teal`/etc. before deciding a fix was in scope.
- **Not verified, stated plainly, matching this repo's established honesty convention**: no browser-automation tooling exists in this environment, so nothing above was interactively exercised — no real screen reader was run against any component, no keyboard-only pass was done in an actual browser, and no visual regression screenshot comparison was taken. All findings came from reading component source directly against Radix's own primitive source/types (to avoid duplicating ARIA Radix already supplies correctly) and computing contrast mathematically from the actual hex values in `tailwind-preset.css`.
- **Design-system doc pages updated** (only where API/behavior genuinely changed, per CLAUDE.md's rule — internal-only fixes with no visible API surface change, e.g. adding a focus ring class or an `aria-invalid` default, were left undocumented since nothing about how a consumer uses the component changed): `apps/design-system/src/app/components/alert/page.tsx` (new default-icon-per-variant behavior, `role` mapping), `.../spinner/page.tsx` (new `label` prop, `role="status"` wrapper), `.../form-field/page.tsx` (render-prop now also passes `aria-invalid`/`aria-required`/`required`, `HelperText`'s new `role="alert"`). No new components were added; no new component-page nav entries were needed.
- **Deferred, stated explicitly rather than silently dropped**: (1) `Carousel`'s prev/resize drag handles inside `calendar.tsx`'s time-grid event blocks (pointer-only resize, no keyboard equivalent) — judged acceptable since the same result (changing an event's start/end time) is fully reachable via the keyboard-operable event-editor dialog, but flagged here rather than asserted as a non-issue without discussion. (2) No automated accessibility-testing tooling (axe-core/jest-axe) was installed — genuinely out of scope per the task brief ("do NOT... install unless clearly trivial and in scope"); **recommended as a follow-up** so future component changes get a regression net instead of relying on a fresh manual audit each time. (3) `calendar.tsx`'s full arrow-key grid navigation (month view) was already present from a prior session and re-verified working correctly by reading it, not changed. (4) Touch-target sizing (WCAG 2.5.5, AAA in 2.1 / 2.5.8 in 2.2) was explicitly out of scope for this 2.1 AA pass and wasn't audited — `@spine/ui` targets web only right now per CLAUDE.md (mobile is a separate not-yet-built Expo app), so this wasn't treated as a blocking gap.
- Stories: resolves the "A formal accessibility (WCAG 2.1 AA) pass per component" line from "Next up" (removed below); not tied to a specific numbered PRD story (a cross-cutting design-system quality pass, not a feature).
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation (`@spine/ui` and `docs/DESIGN.md` are both Phase 0 deliverables; Phase 1 · Sales Spine work is unaffected).

### 2026-08-06 — CI pipeline (Postgres-backed `test:integration`) + onboarding/Settings business logo real upload (S-12.2's last remaining half)

- Two small, independent items pulled off "Next up" in the same session.
- **CI pipeline** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, new — no `.github/` directory existed before). Runs on every push to `main` and every PR: checkout → `pnpm/action-setup` → Node 20 (`actions/setup-node` with pnpm cache) → `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` → `pnpm lint` → `pnpm typecheck` → `pnpm build` → `pnpm test` (unit tests, all packages via Turborepo) → `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:migrate` → `pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration`. A `postgres:16` service container (matching the PRD's pinned Postgres version, `docker-compose.dev.yml`'s own image) provides the database, with a `pg_isready` healthcheck so the job doesn't race a not-yet-ready container. `DATABASE_URL`/`AUTH_SECRET`/`APP_URL` are set as job-level env vars (a throwaway CI-only secret string for `AUTH_SECRET`, not a real credential) — confirmed by reading both integration test files (`apps/api/src/db/ledger-integrity.integration.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/auth/signup.integration.test.ts`) first that neither needs anything beyond a reachable `DATABASE_URL` (both already fall back to a `localhost:5432` default if unset) and that `auth.ts` only reads `AUTH_SECRET` (any string works for a test run, Better Auth doesn't validate its strength). `db:migrate`/`test:integration`'s existing `dotenv -e ../../.env` prefix was checked directly (via a local `mv .env` experiment) to confirm `dotenv-cli` silently skips loading when the file is absent rather than erroring — so no `.env` file needed to be fabricated in CI; the job-level env vars flow through untouched.
- **Verified**, not just written: ran the exact CI steps locally. `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm build` (all packages, via `turbo run <task>`) each pass clean from a fresh state (`apps/web/.next`/`apps/design-system/.next` removed first). Most importantly, ran `DATABASE_URL="postgres://spine:spine@localhost:5432/spine" AUTH_SECRET="ci-test-secret-not-for-production-use-only" APP_URL="http://localhost:3000" pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration` against the real local dev Postgres (`docker-compose.dev.yml`'s `db` service, already running, migrations already applied) — both integration test files passed (5 tests total: the 4-test ledger-integrity suite, the 1-test signup suite), confirming the exact env-var shape the new workflow provides is sufficient, not just plausible. Did not spin up an actual `postgres:16` container fresh (no Docker-in-Docker attempted here) — the local dev Postgres is also version 16 per `docker-compose.dev.yml`, so this is a close proxy for the CI service container, not a byte-identical re-run of the workflow file itself; genuine GitHub Actions execution (secrets, runner environment, Actions cache behavior) is unverified, stated plainly, since that requires actually pushing and watching a run.
- **Business logo real upload** (S-12.2's remaining half, called out in the prior session's "Next up"): `apps/web/src/app/onboarding/steps/business-profile-step.tsx`'s "Logo URL" text field (paste-a-URL, explicitly marked as an interim placeholder in that file's own doc comment) is replaced with a real upload control, `BusinessLogoUpload` (new, `apps/web/src/app/onboarding/steps/business-logo-upload.tsx`). Since `BusinessProfileStep` is shared verbatim between the onboarding wizard and `apps/web/src/app/settings/panes/business-pane.tsx` (confirmed by reading both call sites before touching anything), this one change covers both surfaces — no separate Settings-side work needed.
- **Reused the existing Contacts upload plumbing rather than rebuilding it**: `BusinessLogoUpload` follows the exact pick → crop → `POST /files` flow `ContactLogo`/`LogoCropDialog` already established for contact/contact-person photos — file picker → object URL → `LogoCropDialog` (crop step) → `cropAndResizeImage` (canvas crop+downscale) → `apiClient.upload("/files", file)` (multipart, existing endpoint, unchanged). **Two files with zero Contacts-specific logic were promoted out of `apps/web/src/app/contacts/` into `apps/web/src/lib/`** (`crop-and-resize-image.ts`, `logo-crop-dialog.tsx`) so both the Contacts and onboarding/Settings call sites import the same shared implementation instead of a duplicated copy — confirmed via `grep` first that only `contact-logo.tsx` imported either file before moving them, so `contact-logo.tsx`'s two import lines are the only fix-ups the move required. `LogoCropDialog` gained one new optional prop, `cropShape?: "round" | "rect"` (default `"round"`, preserving Contacts' existing circular-avatar behavior unchanged) — a business logo is a square mark, not a face, so it crops with `cropShape="rect"` while contacts keep the circular crop.
- **No API/schema change**: `business_profile`'s settings-blob shape is unchanged — `logoUrl` was always "a URL string, however it got there" (previously hand-typed, now the `GET /files/:id` URL of the uploaded file, assembled client-side as `` `${NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL}/files/${fileId}` ``, matching exactly how `ContactLogo`'s `<AvatarImage src>` already resolves a `fileId` into a URL). `PUT /org/settings/business_profile` (pre-existing, unchanged) still just stores whatever string comes through.
- **Verified**: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, `build` each run clean (fresh `.next` removed first). Grepped the compiled production bundle (`apps/web/.next/static/chunks/`) and confirmed the literal strings `"Upload business logo"` and `"Logo uploaded"` (from `BusinessLogoUpload`'s own markup) appear in the shipped chunk — direct static proof the new component is wired into the built onboarding page (and, since it's the same shared `BusinessProfileStep`, the Settings business pane too). Confirmed via `twMerge`'s conflict-resolution behavior (read `packages/ui/src/lib/cn.ts`) that the new component's `rounded-md` override on `Avatar`'s base `rounded-full` class resolves correctly rather than both classes fighting.
- **Not verified, stated plainly**: no browser-automation tooling is available in this environment — clicking the logo square, watching the crop dialog open with `cropShape="rect"`, dragging/zooming, and a real end-to-end upload landing in MinIO and rendering back were not interactively exercised, matching every prior session's honesty pattern on this point. The genuine GitHub Actions run (pushing and watching `.github/workflows/ci.yml` execute against a real ephemeral `postgres:16` service container) is likewise unverified — only a close local proxy (existing dev Postgres, same major version) was run.
- Stories: closes the CI-pipeline line and the "logo file upload" half of S-12.2 from "Next up" above (both fully resolved now, no remaining gap on either).
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation (CI and the onboarding wizard are both Phase 0 deliverables; Phase 1 · Sales Spine work continues unaffected).

### 2026-08-06 — Edit everywhere on Contacts (persons/addresses/leads/activities) + the first real audit-log write instrumentation and a History tab

- Two pieces, requested together: (1) closing the "no way to edit an existing contact person/address/lead/activity" gap left open by every prior Contacts session — only add and delete existed on all four sub-entity sections — and (2) the codebase's first real writes to `audit_log` (previously zero writers anywhere, table schema-only, per "Next up" above), scoped to Contacts, plus a `GET /contacts/:id/history` endpoint and a web History tab reading real entries.
- **Part 1 — edit dialogs.** `contact-persons-section.tsx`, `addresses-section.tsx`, `leads-section.tsx`, `activities-section.tsx` (all `apps/web/src/app/contacts/`) each gained an edit affordance: a `Pencil` icon-button per list row, opening the **exact same Dialog** the section already used for "Add X" (per the task brief's explicit instruction not to build a second form), keyed off one new `editingId: string | null` piece of state — `null` means adding, a set id means editing that row. Dialog title and the primary button's label flip ("Add contact person" → "Edit contact person", "Add" → "Save changes") but the fields/layout are identical, confirmed by construction since it's literally the same JSX, not a parallel copy.
  - Contact persons/addresses reuse the existing whole-array-replace `PUT /contacts/:id` (unchanged endpoint) — `toInput()` now includes each row's `id` (previously omitted, since add/remove never needed it), and a new `handleSave()` either appends a new entry (add) or replaces one array element in place by id (edit) before calling the same `persist()` helper both flows already shared. `PersonInput`/`AddressInput` types were introduced with `id` as **optional** (a new row has none until the array-replace PUT assigns it), fixing a TypeScript error the naive change first produced (`Property 'id' is missing` — the add-path literal had no `id` field, the edit-path spread did).
  - Leads/activities call the pre-existing `PUT /leads/:id`/`PUT /activities/:id` endpoints directly (confirmed both already existed in `leads.controller.ts`/`activities.controller.ts` before touching anything, per the brief) — `handleSave()` branches on `editingId` to `PUT` vs the existing `POST`.
  - Idempotency: no new endpoints were added by Part 1 (edit reuses `PUT /contacts/:id`, `PUT /leads/:id`, `PUT /activities/:id`, all already wired with `@UseInterceptors(IdempotencyInterceptor)` before this session) — confirmed rather than assumed by reading each controller first, and re-confirmed live (see Verified: a replayed `Idempotency-Key` on a `PUT /contacts/:id` returned the exact stored response, not a re-applied write).
  - Select-inside-Dialog z-index (the prior session's `z-popover`/`z-modal` fix) was not touched — the edit dialogs are the same `DialogContent` JSX as the add dialogs that already exercised it (addresses' type Select, leads' stage Select, activities' type Select), so no new exercise was needed beyond what already existed; not independently re-verified interactively (no browser tooling — see Not verified below), same as every prior z-index claim in this file.
- **Part 2 — `AuditLogService`** (`apps/api/src/modules/audit/audit-log.service.ts`, new; `apps/api/src/modules/audit/audit.module.ts` now provides/exports it instead of being a literal `@Module({})` stub). `record(tx, {orgId, actorUserId, action, entityType, entityId, contactId, changes})` inserts one row into `audit_log`; `tx` is typed `Database | Transaction` (`Transaction` is a new type alias in `apps/api/src/db/client.ts`, `Parameters<Parameters<Database["transaction"]>[0]>[0]` — drizzle-orm's `tx` callback parameter is structurally a different type than `Database` itself, so a type that only accepted `Database` wouldn't type-check when called from inside a `db.transaction(async (tx) => ...)` block) so every call joins whatever transaction its caller is already inside, never a separate unguarded write. `action` is a short verb (`created`/`updated`/`archived`/`restored`/`deleted`/`removed`/`marked done`/`marked undone`); `entityType` is one of `contact`/`contact_person`/`address`/`lead`/`activity`; `changes` is a plain `{field, from, to}[]` — a shallow diff, not a full snapshot, per the task brief's own preference. A static `AuditLogService.diffFields(before, input)` helper (shared by every call site) implements exactly the brief's suggested rule: for each key present in `input` (an `undefined` field means "not touched," matching the codebase's existing partial-update convention), if it differs from `before`'s value, record one `{field, from, to}` entry — deliberately not a generic deep-diffing library.
- **Denormalized `audit_log.contact_id`** (new nullable FK, `ON DELETE CASCADE` to `contacts.id`, migration `apps/api/drizzle/0007_real_galactus.sql`, idx 7 — confirmed as the correct next index against `_journal.json`'s last entry, idx 6, before generating, per this session's explicit caution about the earlier migration-numbering collision). Chosen over joining across four different sub-entity tables to answer "which contact does this row belong to" — populated on every audit row this pass writes (equal to `entityId` for `entityType: "contact"` rows; the sub-entity's own `contactId` otherwise; `null` only for a hard-deleted contact's own final "deleted" entry, see below). Three new indexes: `audit_log_org_idx`, `audit_log_contact_idx`, `audit_log_entity_idx` (composite on `entity_type, entity_id`).
- **Contact-person/address diffing** (`contacts.service.ts`'s `update`, the whole-array-replace method): reads the existing `contactPersons`/`addresses` rows *before* the transaction (needed to diff by id — the array-replace re-insert always mints brand-new row ids even for entries the client sent an existing `id` on, so post-insert ids can't be compared against pre-update ids). A new private `recordArrayDiff()` positionally pairs each `input[i]` entry against the corresponding freshly-inserted `after[i]` row (both arrays are built from the same input array in the same order) to know the new row's real id for `entityId`, while matching against `before` by the input entry's own `id` field: a match with changed fields → `updated`; an entry with no `id` or an unrecognized `id` → `created`; a `before` row with no matching input entry → `removed`. `create`'s equivalent path (no diffing needed, everything is new) records one `created` entry per inserted person/address directly.
- **The contact's own scalar-field diff** (`contacts.service.ts`'s `update`) reads the row *before* the update (`existing`, already fetched for the 404 check) and diffs it against `input` — with `contactPersons`/`addresses` explicitly destructured out first, since they're not real columns on `contacts` and diffing them naively against `existing` (which has no such keys) produced a nonsensical `"contactPersons: null → [...]"` noise entry, caught live during verification (see Verified) and fixed before this entry was written, not left in.
