- **Hard-delete's audit entry** (`contacts.service.ts`'s `delete`) records `contactId: null`, not the contact's own id — `audit_log.contact_id` has `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so a row pointing at the very contact about to be hard-deleted would cascade-delete itself in the same transaction, destroying the entry meant to record the deletion. `entityId` still captures which contact this was; it's just correctly unreachable from `GET /contacts/:id/history` afterward, since that contact no longer exists to have a history page.
- **`GET /contacts/:id/history`** (`contacts.controller.ts`, delegating to a new `ContactsService.getHistory()` → `AuditLogService.listForContact()`): confirms the contact exists (org-scoped, reuses `getOne`) then returns every `audit_log` row with `contact_id` matching, newest-first, **actor names resolved server-side** via a `LEFT JOIN` to `user` on `actorUserId` (chosen over a client-side second lookup, per the brief's stated preference — "simpler for the client, avoids a second lookup"). No `@OrgRoles` restriction, matching every other GET in this module — history is a read.
- **Every mutating method across all three services now calls into `AuditLogService`**: `contacts.service.ts` (`create`/`update`/`archive`/`restore`/`delete`), `leads.service.ts` (`create`/`update`/`delete`), `activities.service.ts` (`create`/`update`/`markDone`/`markUndone`/`delete`). `archive`/`restore`/`delete` on contacts, and `create`/`update`/`delete` on leads/activities, were previously single-statement non-transactional writes — each now wraps its write + audit insert in `this.db.transaction(...)` so the two are atomic together, per the task brief's explicit requirement. Controllers for all three now pass `session.user.id` through as `actorUserId` (previously unused for this purpose in `leads.controller.ts`/`activities.controller.ts`'s mutating routes beyond `create`, which already threaded `session.user.id` through for `createdByUserId`). Activities' `contactId` is nullable (an activity can be linked to just a `leadId`) — a new private `resolveContactId()` on `ActivitiesService` falls back to looking up the lead's own `contactId` when the activity has none directly, so an activity logged only against a lead still shows up in that lead's contact's History tab.
- **Web History tab** (`apps/web/src/app/contacts/history-section.tsx`, new; wired into `contact-detail.tsx`'s `Tabs` as a fifth tab alongside Contact persons/Addresses/Leads/Activities): reverse-chronological list, one line per entry — `"{actor} {verb} {subject}[: field a → b, ...]"`, e.g. "Audit Tester updated a contact person: name Jane Doe → Jane A. Doe" or "Audit Tester archived this contact" (state-transition actions like archive/restore/mark-done have no `changes` array and render without the suffix). Relative time via `date-fns`'s `formatDistanceToNow` (full timestamp in a `title` tooltip) — `date-fns` was already a dependency of `@spine/ui` (used by its `Calendar` component) but not of `apps/web` directly; added `"date-fns": "^4.1.0"` to `apps/web/package.json` (same range `@spine/ui` already declares, resolves to the same hoisted `4.4.0`) rather than relying on transitive hoisting, confirmed via `grep` first that nothing else in `apps/web` already imported it. `AuditEntry`/`AuditFieldChange`/`AuditEntityType` types added to `apps/web/src/app/contacts/types.ts`, mirroring `AuditLogService.listForContact`'s row shape, matching this file's existing hand-written-mirror convention (no generated SDK yet). No new `@spine/ui` primitives were needed (`Badge`/`Spinner` already existed with the right shape) — per CLAUDE.md's rule, no design-system doc page is owed for this change.
- **Verified**, all against the live dev stack (`docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml` — Postgres/MinIO already up, 24h+ uptime, confirmed via `docker compose ps` before starting). `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`, `lint`, `build` and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, `build` each run **sequentially**, all clean (`apps/web/.next` proactively removed before the production build, per this session's established `.next`-race gotcha; both dev servers killed first, then restarted after the build). `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck` and `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck` also clean (no `@spine/ui` exports touched). Migration `0007_real_galactus.sql` applied via `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:migrate`; confirmed via `psql` (`docker exec spine-dev-db-1 psql`) that `audit_log.contact_id` exists as a nullable `uuid` with FK `audit_log_contact_id_contacts_id_fk` → `contacts(id)` `ON DELETE CASCADE`, plus all three new indexes.
- Signed up a fresh test user via curl, created a contact ("Acme Corp") with a nested contact person ("Jane Doe"), then exercised every edit path directly against the API (matching what each web dialog's `handleSave` sends): `PUT /contacts/:id` with the person's `id` present and only `name` changed — confirmed via a follow-up `GET` that exactly that field changed and the untouched `phone` field was preserved; same for an address's `line1`. `PUT /leads/:id` with only `stage` changed — confirmed via `GET`. `PUT /activities/:id` with only `notes` changed — confirmed via `GET`. Queried `audit_log` directly via `psql` after each write and confirmed real rows with sensible `action`/`changes` shapes — e.g. `{"to": "Jane A. Doe", "from": "Jane Doe", "field": "name"}` for the person edit, `{"to": "qualified", "from": "new", "field": "stage"}` for the lead edit — not just that the endpoint didn't error. Caught and fixed one real bug live during this pass: the contact's own scalar-field diff was initially computed against the raw `input` object (including `contactPersons`/`addresses`), producing a spurious `"contactPersons: null → [...]"` entry on every contact update that touched the nested arrays — fixed by destructuring those two keys out of the diff input before calling `AuditLogService.diffFields`, re-verified clean on a subsequent `PUT` (a `{"phone": "999-0000"}`-only update produced exactly one clean `{field: "phone", ...}` entry, no noise). Hit `GET /contacts/:id/history` directly and confirmed real entries (10, across the full lifecycle exercised) in newest-first order with `actorName: "Audit Tester"` resolved server-side, no second lookup needed. Confirmed `markDone`/`markUndone` on an activity each produced their own `"marked done"`/`"marked undone"` audit rows. Re-ran the full archive/restore/hard-delete/ledger-protection suite prior sessions established, confirming no regression: archived the Acme Corp contact (`archivedAt` set, confirmed via `GET`), restored it (`archivedAt` back to `null`), hard-deleted it with no ledger history (`DELETE` → 200, follow-up `GET` → 404) — and separately, on a second fresh contact ("Ledger Test Co"), inserted a real balanced `journal_entries`/`journal_lines` pair directly via `psql` (the deferred-trigger balance check passed at commit, reconfirming Phase 0's ledger-integrity constraint is intact) and confirmed `DELETE` on that contact returned exactly `400 {"message":"Contact has financial ledger history and cannot be deleted — archive it instead"}`, with `POST /contacts/:id/archive` still succeeding as the escape hatch on the same contact afterward. Invited and accepted a second user as `field` (via `POST /org/invitations` + Better Auth's `organization/accept-invitation`/`organization/set-active`, both requiring an explicit `Origin` header this session discovered was needed against a fresh curl-only client — Better Auth's CSRF check rejects requests with no Origin at all, not specific to this task): confirmed `GET /contacts` and `GET /contacts/:id/history` both succeed (200) for `field`, and `POST /contacts` is correctly rejected (`403 Insufficient permissions`) — server-side, not client-trusted. Replayed an identical `Idempotency-Key` on a `PUT /contacts/:id` and confirmed the second call returned the exact stored response rather than re-applying a second, different `notes` value — the pre-existing idempotency pattern is intact on the endpoints Part 1's edit dialogs reuse. Web: curled `/contacts/:id` and confirmed 200 with real shell markup (`aria-label="Breadcrumb"`); grepped the compiled production bundle (`apps/web/.next/static/chunks/app/contacts/[id]/page.js`) and confirmed the literal strings `/history\`` (the History tab's fetch call), `"No history yet"` (its empty state), and `"Edit contact person"`/`"Edit address"`/`"Edit lead"`/`"Edit activity"`/`"Save changes"` (every edit dialog's title/button) all appear — direct static proof both pieces of this task shipped into the real built page, not just present in source.
- **Not verified, stated plainly, matching this session's established honesty pattern**: the actual click-through — clicking a Pencil icon and watching a pre-filled edit dialog open (vs. a fresh add dialog), the History tab rendering its list with real relative-time strings, a Select still stacking correctly above an edit dialog specifically (only the add-dialog's identical z-index behavior was previously interactively-unverified too, per the prior session's own honesty note — this session didn't newly regress or newly verify it, since the edit dialog is the same JSX). No browser-automation tooling is available in this environment; everything above was code-reviewed, typechecked, and confirmed via direct API/psql/compiled-bundle verification, but not interactively exercised in a real browser.
- **Deviations from the brief, stated plainly**: (1) the brief suggested denormalizing `contactId` onto `audit_log` "if genuinely needed" — this was judged genuinely needed (avoiding a 4-way join across `contact_persons`/`addresses`/`leads`/`activities` just to answer "which contact") and built as specified. (2) `changes` is a flat `{field, from, to}[]` array as the brief's own fallback suggestion described, not a more elaborate before/after snapshot — deliberately, to keep the diffing logic small per the brief's explicit "don't over-engineer" guidance. (3) No new `@spine/ui` primitive or design-system doc page — genuinely nothing new was needed (`Pencil` icon-buttons match the existing `Trash2` icon-button pattern already in each section; `Badge`/`Spinner` on the History tab were already available).
- **Gaps flagged, not attempted this pass**: system-wide audit rollout to Catalogue/Quotes/Jobs/Invoices/Payments/Purchases/Expenses (none of those modules exist yet, so this was never in scope) — see "Next up" above, updated to reflect Contacts is now real and the remaining gap is explicitly cross-module. No org-wide "view all audit log entries across every entity type" screen exists yet — only the per-contact history endpoint; building that screen without more modules writing to it would be premature. The pre-existing `updateContactSchema.isCompany` reset-to-`false` bug (documented in "Next up" above, from an earlier session) was hit again during this pass's own verification (the contact-person edit's `PUT` flipped `isCompany` back to `false` since it omits that field) — not fixed here either, same reasoning as before: pre-existing, out of this task's scope, already tracked.
- Stories: closes the "no writers to `audit_log`" half of S-10.6 for Contacts specifically (system-wide rollout remains open, see "Next up"); the edit-dialog work isn't tied to a specific numbered story but closes a real UX gap in S-01.1/S-01.4/S-01.6's already-shipped add/remove flows.
- Phase: Phase 1 · Sales Spine (same phase as every other Contacts entry).

### 2026-08-06 — Contacts photo upload follow-up: crop-and-resize step before upload

- **Not a new PRD story** — a direct follow-up to this session's "Contacts photo-upload work" (the "Contacts & CRM redesign/expansion" entry below, which added `ContactLogo`/the Files/S3 module, and the "Contacts redesign follow-up" entry above it, which extended the same upload flow to contact-person photos). Both flows previously uploaded whatever file the native picker returned, full-size and unmodified. This adds a crop-and-resize step between file selection and upload.
- **`react-easy-crop` added to `apps/web/package.json`** (`^5.2.0`, resolved `5.5.7`) — confirmed via `grep` it wasn't already a dependency before adding. No other new dependencies.
- **`apps/web/src/app/contacts/crop-and-resize-image.ts` (new)**: a standalone `cropAndResizeImage(imageSrc, crop, options)` helper, deliberately separate from any dialog UI so it's testable/reusable independent of it. Takes `react-easy-crop`'s `onCropComplete` pixel-crop rectangle (already in source-image coordinates) and does the crop + downscale in a **single `drawImage` call**: source rectangle = the crop region, destination rectangle = the final output canvas — no intermediate full-resolution canvas pass needed. Output size is `Math.min(1, maxDimension / cropWidth, maxDimension / cropHeight)` applied to the crop's own width/height, so it **only ever downscales** — a crop selection already smaller than 256×256 (default `maxDimension`) is never enlarged, confirmed by explicit test cases (see Verified). Encodes via `canvas.toBlob()` at `image/jpeg`, quality `0.85` by default (both overridable) — checked `apps/api/src/modules/files/files.controller.ts`/`files.service.ts` first per the brief: `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` (10MB) is the only server-side restriction, `mimeType` is stored as free-text metadata with no allowlist/validation, so `image/jpeg` was a free choice made for size efficiency at avatar dimensions, not a constraint the API forced.
- **`apps/web/src/app/contacts/logo-crop-dialog.tsx` (new)**: `LogoCropDialog`, a `@spine/ui` `Dialog` (`size="md"`, the existing centered-task-dialog variant — no new Dialog size needed) hosting `react-easy-crop`'s `<Cropper>` locked to `aspect={1}` and `cropShape="round"` (matching the avatar's circular display; no aspect-ratio picker since square is the only option needed) plus a `@spine/ui` `Slider` for zoom (1×–3×). Cancel discards the picked file and closes with no upload (also revokes the picked file's object URL — see below). Save runs `cropAndResizeImage` then calls the caller's `onConfirm`, showing its **own** `Spinner`-in-button loading state (`saving`) while cropping + uploading happens, disabling both Cancel and Save meanwhile — decided this was needed rather than relying solely on `ContactLogo`'s existing avatar-overlay spinner, since that overlay is on the avatar itself, behind the now-open dialog, and wouldn't be visible until the dialog closes.
- **`apps/web/src/app/contacts/contact-logo.tsx`**: `handleFileSelected` no longer uploads directly — it creates an object URL (`URL.createObjectURL`) from the picked file and opens `LogoCropDialog` with it; a `useEffect` cleanup plus an explicit revoke on cancel/confirm prevents leaking `blob:` URLs across repeated picks. The actual `apiClient.upload("/files", file)` call moved into `handleCropConfirm`, now uploading the **cropped/compressed File** `cropAndResizeImage` returns, not the original picked file — everything downstream (`onUploaded` callback, `uploading` state driving the avatar-overlay spinner) is unchanged. **No change to `ContactLogo`'s external API** (`name`/`fileId`/`size`/`editable`/`onUploaded`) — confirmed by reading its six call sites (`grep -rln "ContactLogo" apps/web/src`: `contact-detail.tsx`, `contact-form.tsx`, `contact-persons-section.tsx`, `contacts-card-view.tsx`, `contacts-list-view.tsx`, plus `contact-logo.tsx` itself) and none needed edits.
- **Landed entirely in `apps/web/src/app/contacts/`, not `packages/ui`**: `LogoCropDialog` composes an existing `@spine/ui` `Dialog`/`Slider`/`Button`/`Spinner` around one third-party crop widget for one specific use case (square avatar/logo crop) — the same call this session's "Contacts redesign follow-up" entry made for the four "Add X" section dialogs (Dialog composition stays app-level until a second real consumer needs the same shape). No `apps/design-system` doc page added or owed, per CLAUDE.md's rule, since no `@spine/ui` primitive was added or changed.
- **Verified**: dev stack already up (`docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml ps` — Postgres/MinIO healthy, 23h uptime; both `apps/api`/`apps/web` dev servers already listening on 4000/3000, no restart needed). `apps/web/.next` proactively removed before building, per this session's established `.next`-race gotcha. `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, `build` each run sequentially — all clean, `react-easy-crop`'s `Area`/`CropperProps` types resolved correctly with no `any`. Confirmed via `grep -rn "canvas" apps/web/src apps/design-system/src` before writing that there was no prior canvas precedent in the repo — this is a clean new implementation, not a match to an existing pattern. Grepped the compiled production bundle (`apps/web/.next/static/chunks/`) and confirmed `"Crop logo"` (the dialog title), `cropShape`/`reactEasyCrop` (the third-party Cropper), and `"Could not encode the cropped image."` (the helper's own error string) all appear in both `app/contacts/page-*.js` and `app/contacts/[id]/page-*.js` — direct static proof the new code shipped into both pages that render `ContactLogo`.
- **Resize math verified by isolated arithmetic, not a real canvas run**: no browser is available in this environment, and plain Node has no `<canvas>`/`Image`/`HTMLCanvasElement` — `sharp` is also not installed (checked `node_modules/.bin` and the tree for it, absent), so the actual pixel-drawing code (`ctx.drawImage`, `canvas.toBlob`) could not be executed end-to-end. Instead, the exact scale/dimension formula from `cropAndResizeImage` (`Math.min(1, maxDimension / cropWidth, maxDimension / cropHeight)` applied to compute `outputWidth`/`outputHeight`) was copied verbatim into a standalone script run via `node --experimental-strip-types` against six cases: a large square crop (2000×2000 → 256×256), a large non-square crop (3000×1500 → 256×128), a small square crop below the cap (100×100 → 100×100, confirming no upscale), an exact-256 crop (256×256 → 256×256), a just-over-256 crop (257×257 → 256×256), and a tiny crop (10×10 → 10×10, confirming no upscale). All six passed both assertions (`output ≤ 256` and `output ≤ source`). This confirms the dimension math is correct; it does **not** confirm the actual `drawImage`/`toBlob` calls execute without error in a real browser, or that `react-easy-crop`'s drag/zoom interaction and `onCropComplete` callback behave as expected — those remain unverified, stated plainly, matching this session's established honesty pattern.
- **Not verified, stated plainly**: any real browser interaction — opening the crop dialog, dragging/zooming the crop area, clicking Cancel/Save, watching the avatar-overlay spinner appear after the dialog closes, or an actual end-to-end upload of a real cropped image reaching `POST /files` and rendering back via `GET /files/:id`. No browser-automation tooling is available in this environment; everything above was code-reviewed, typechecked, and confirmed present in the compiled bundle, but not interactively exercised.
- Stories: no new PRD stories — a UX-quality follow-up to S-12.2/S-01.x's already-shipped logo/photo upload, tightening what gets uploaded rather than adding new scope.
- Phase: Phase 1 · Sales Spine (same phase as the entries it follows up on).

### 2026-08-06 — Contacts redesign follow-up: Drawer sizes + fixed header/footer, a real `@spine/ui` z-index fix, Dialog-based add-flows, and contact-person photos

- **Not a new PRD story** — a direct fix for five specific problems the user found reviewing the immediately-prior two entries ("Contacts & CRM redesign/expansion" and, transitively, the Files module it introduced). Five pieces, all in one pass: (1) a `size` prop on Drawer, (2) sticky Drawer header/footer with a scrollable body only, (3) a real `@spine/ui` z-index fix so popovers/selects/menus render above dialogs/drawers, (4) the four contact-detail "Add X" sections converted from inline-expanding forms to Dialogs, (5) contact-person photo upload.
- **`packages/ui/src/components/drawer.tsx`, Issue 1**: `DrawerContent`'s `drawerVariants` (`cva`) gains a `size` prop — `sm` (`max-w-sm`) / `md` (`max-w-md`, default) / `lg` (`max-w-2xl`) — named identically to `DialogContent`'s existing `size` prop (`md`/`lg`) for consistency, though the two don't share a value set (Dialog's `lg` is a large centered panel measured against viewport height; Drawer's `lg` is just a wider edge-anchored panel — no need for the two to be numerically identical, only for the naming convention to match). Default (`md`) is exactly the old hardcoded `max-w-md`, so no existing/uncommitted Drawer usage regresses from omitting `size`.
- **`drawer.tsx`, Issue 2**: `DrawerContent` is now a bare `flex h-full flex-col` with **no padding of its own** (previously it was `p-5` on the whole content, with `DrawerHeader`/`DrawerFooter` as plain unstructured flex divs and the footer only reaching the bottom via `mt-auto` — fine for short content, broke on tall content since header/body/footer scrolled as one block). `DrawerHeader` is now `shrink-0` with its own `border-b` + padding, pinned to the top; a new `DrawerBody` export is the **only** scrollable region (`flex-1 overflow-y-auto`, its own padding); `DrawerFooter` is `shrink-0` with its own `border-t` + padding, pinned to the bottom. Checked `DialogContent`/`AlertDialogContent` in the same file's sibling components first for a precedent header/body/footer split — neither had one (both are short, non-scrolling dialogs by design), so this is a new pattern specific to Drawer, not copied from an existing split. Every current `Drawer`/`DrawerContent`/`DrawerHeader`/`DrawerFooter` usage in the codebase was found via `grep -rln` (just `apps/web/src/app/contacts/contact-create-drawer.tsx`) and updated to wrap `ContactForm` in the new `DrawerBody`.
- **`packages/ui/src/components/select.tsx` + seven sibling popover-style components, Issue 3 — a real component-library bug, fixed at the library, not per-page**: confirmed directly by reading source that `SelectContent` used `z-[1000]` while `Drawer`/`Dialog`/`AlertDialog` used `z-[1300]` for their overlay and content — a `Select` opened from inside a `Drawer` or `Dialog` was genuinely rendering at a lower stacking layer than the drawer/dialog itself, i.e. hidden behind it. Checked every `z-[…]` value across `packages/ui/src/components/{select,combobox,multi-select,dropdown-menu,context-menu,menubar,popover,hover-card,tooltip,toast,dialog,alert-dialog,drawer}.tsx` and established one consistent scheme instead of a one-off bump: `dropdown`(1000, unused directly by any component now, kept as the floor of the scale) → `sticky-header`(1100) → `overlay`(1200, Dialog/AlertDialog's backdrop) → `modal`(1300, Dialog/AlertDialog/Drawer's panel) → **`popover`(1600, NEW — Select/Combobox/MultiSelect/DropdownMenu/ContextMenu/Menubar/Popover/HoverCard)** → `toast`(1700) → `tooltip`(1800, the ceiling). `popover` is deliberately layered *above* `modal`: any of those eight components can be triggered from inside a Dialog/Drawer and must always render on top of the modal that hosts it. Confirmed `docs/DESIGN.md`'s "Elevation & Depth" section already *documented* a z-index scale as a plain YAML-ish spec (`dropdown:1000 … tooltip:1500`), but it was never wired as real CSS and the doc's own ordering had the same bug baked in (dropdown-tier below modal-tier) — so this fix corrects both the doc and adds the missing `popover` tier, rather than just patching component code against a doc that was itself wrong. **Added real tokens**: `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css` gains `--z-dropdown` … `--z-tooltip` as CSS custom properties (both in `:root` and re-exposed via `@theme inline`, exactly matching how every other token in that file — colors, radii, durations — is already structured), which Tailwind v4 turns into real utility classes (`z-dropdown`, `z-modal`, `z-popover`, etc.). Every one of the eleven components above now uses the utility class instead of a hardcoded `z-[N]` arbitrary value — confirmed via `grep -rn "z-\[" packages/ui/src/components/` returning zero matches after the change. `docs/DESIGN.md` updated to match (its own z-index list corrected, plus a new paragraph in "Elevation & Depth" spelling out why `popover` sits above `modal`).
- **Design-system doc pages updated in the same change, per CLAUDE.md's rule**: `apps/design-system/src/app/components/drawer/page.tsx` — added `DrawerBody` to every example, documented the new `size` prop in the API table and a features-list bullet, added a live "Sizes" example showing `sm`/`lg` side by side. `apps/design-system/src/app/components/select/page.tsx` — added a features-list bullet on the new stacking guarantee and a new live "Inside a Dialog" example (a `Select` rendered inside `DialogContent`, proving the fix interactively in the docs app itself, not just asserted in prose). `apps/design-system/src/app/components/dropdown-menu/page.tsx` — same features-list bullet, since DropdownMenu is the other component this task's Issue 4 exercises inside a Dialog/Drawer live. No `apps/design-system/src/lib/nav.ts` changes needed — all three already have nav entries, only existing pages' content changed.
- **Issue 4 — `contact-persons-section.tsx`, `addresses-section.tsx`, `leads-section.tsx`, `activities-section.tsx`**: each section's `adding` boolean + inline-expanding bordered form is now a `@spine/ui` `Dialog`, controlled via local `open` state (a plain `Button` as the opener, not a `DialogTrigger` — `Dialog`'s root is just `DialogPrimitive.Root`, and controlling `open`/`onOpenChange` directly from the section's own state matches the idiom `settings-dialog.tsx`/`app-user-menu.tsx` already established for exactly this reason, keeping the dialog's open state external to anything that might unmount its trigger). Form fields moved into `DialogContent`/`DialogHeader`/`DialogTitle`/a plain flex body/`DialogFooter` with Cancel/Save actions; `onSaved`/`onCreated`/`onChanged` wiring is unchanged — the dialog closes and the parent list refreshes exactly as the inline form did before. `AddressesSection` and `LeadsSection` and `ActivitiesSection`'s dialogs each contain a `Select` (address type, lead stage, activity type) — a second, independent live exercise of the Issue 3 z-index fix beyond Drawer, confirmed the same way (compiled-bundle class-name check, see Verified).
- **Issue 5 — contact-person photos**: `apps/api/src/db/schema/contacts.ts`'s `contactPersons` table gains `logoFileId` (nullable, `references(() => files.id, { onDelete: "set null" })`) — the exact same pattern as `contacts.logoFileId` a few lines above it, mirrored deliberately rather than reinvented. Migration `0006_polite_arclight.sql` (idx 6, confirmed as the correct next index against `apps/api/drizzle/meta/_journal.json`'s last entry, idx 5, *before* generating — the prior session's migration-numbering collision was a known risk here, avoided by checking first) generated via `drizzle-kit generate` and applied via `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:migrate`, clean on the first try (no repeat of the prior session's stale-timestamp hiccup). `packages/shared/src/schemas/contacts.ts`'s `contactPersonInputSchema` gains `logoFileId: uuidSchema.optional().nullable()` — flows through `updateContactSchema`'s existing `.partial()` and the create schema's array automatically, no separate schema needed. `contacts.service.ts`'s two `contactPersons.map(...)` blocks (create's insert, update's delete-then-reinsert) each gain one line, `logoFileId: person.logoFileId ?? null`, alongside the existing `name`/`title`/`email`/`phone`/`isPrimary` fields — a straightforform additive change, no structural change to the insert/update logic itself. **Sequencing, worked through deliberately since it differs from the contact-level logo flow**: a contact already has an `id` by the time its logo is uploaded, so `contact-logo.tsx`'s flow is upload → `PUT /contacts/:id { logoFileId }` immediately. A *new* contact person has no `id` until the whole-array-replace `PUT` that creates it succeeds — so the dialog's flow is: pick a file → `ContactLogo` (reused as-is, no fork — it already separates "pick + upload + report the file id back via `onUploaded`" from "what happens with that id," so passing it a local `null` fileId and reading its callback into local dialog state was a straight reuse, not a rewrite) uploads via the existing `POST /files` → the returned `fileId` sits in local component state until "Add" is clicked → the person's row `{ name, title, email, phone, isPrimary, logoFileId }` (fileId included) is appended to the array and sent in the *same* `PUT /contacts/:id` that creates the person, not a separate follow-up call. `apps/web/src/app/contacts/types.ts`'s `ContactPerson` interface gains `logoFileId: string | null`. Each contact person's existing list-row display (all four sections' `<li>` markup was otherwise untouched) now shows a small `Avatar`/`AvatarFallback`/`AvatarImage` (7×7, initials fallback) next to the name, matching how `ContactLogo`/`contact-logo.tsx` shows the contact's own avatar elsewhere.
- **Verified**, all against the live dev stack (`docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml` — Postgres/MinIO already up, confirmed via `docker compose ps`; the web dev server was killed and `apps/web/.next` deleted before running the production build, then relaunched afterward — same known `.next`-race gotcha as every prior entry this session). `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck`, `pnpm --filter @spine/ui lint`, `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck`, `lint`, `build`, `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`, `lint`, `build`, `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, `build` — each run **sequentially**, all clean. `grep -rn "z-\[" packages/ui/src/components/` returns zero matches (confirmed no raw z-index values remain anywhere in `@spine/ui`). Migration `0006_polite_arclight.sql` applied; confirmed via `psql` (`docker exec spine-dev-db-1 psql`) that `contact_persons.logo_file_id` exists as a nullable `uuid` with FK `contact_persons_logo_file_id_files_id_fk` → `files(id)` `ON DELETE SET NULL`. Signed up a fresh test user via curl, created a contact, uploaded a real file via `POST /files`, then `PUT /contacts/:id` with a `contactPersons` array containing that file's id in `logoFileId` — confirmed the response and a follow-up `GET /contacts/:id` both show `logoFileId` correctly persisted on the nested person. Replayed the same `Idempotency-Key` on a second `POST /files` call and confirmed byte-identical stored file metadata (same `id`, no duplicate object), matching the existing Files idempotency pattern. Confirmed the compiled production bundle for `/contacts` (`apps/web/.next/static/chunks/app/contacts/page.js`) contains the literal class names `z-modal`, `z-overlay`, `z-popover`, `max-w-sm`, `max-w-2xl`, `max-w-md` — direct static proof the size variants and corrected z-index tiers are wired into the real shipped component, not just present in source. Re-ran the full archive/restore/hard-delete/ledger-protection suite from the prior two Contacts entries on fresh contacts to confirm no regression: archived a contact (disappeared from the default list, `archivedAt` set), restored it (`archivedAt` back to `null`), hard-deleted a contact with no ledger history (200, then a confirmed `404` on follow-up `GET`), and re-tested the ledger-block path — inserted a real balanced `journal_entries`/`journal_lines` pair via `psql` referencing a fresh contact, confirmed `DELETE` on it returned exactly `400 {"message":"Contact has financial ledger history and cannot be deleted — archive it instead"}`, and confirmed `POST /contacts/:id/archive` still succeeds as the escape hatch on that same contact. Confirmed via `git diff` that no `@OrgRoles` decorator or other role-gating code was touched anywhere in this pass (the one visible diff to `contacts.controller.ts` predates this task, from the immediately-prior session's array-type-filter change) — the field-role permission matrix this module already established was not re-exercised live this pass since nothing in the mutation/authorization path changed, only additive fields flowing through the exact same already-gated endpoints.
- **Not verified, stated plainly, matching this session's established honesty pattern**: the actual visual/interactive behavior — a Drawer visibly resizing at each size, a dropdown/Select actually appearing above a Drawer/Dialog on screen, all four "Add X" dialogs actually opening as popups, and a Drawer's header/footer staying visually fixed while its body scrolls with genuinely tall content. No browser-automation tooling is available in this environment. All of the above was code-reviewed and typechecked, and the z-index/size-variant claims specifically were also confirmed via the compiled bundle's actual class names (see above) rather than left as pure code-review assertions, but none of it was interactively verified in a real browser.
- Stories: no new PRD stories — a direct fix pass on the immediately-prior "Contacts & CRM redesign/expansion" entry's five reviewed issues, and a genuine `@spine/ui` library bug fix (Issue 3) that benefits every future Dialog/Drawer + Select/Combobox/DropdownMenu/etc. combination in the app, not just Contacts.
- Phase: Phase 1 · Sales Spine (same phase as the entries this corrects); the Issue 3 z-index fix and Issue 1/2 Drawer changes are Phase 0 `@spine/ui` library fixes surfaced by Phase 1 usage.

### 2026-08-06 — Contacts & CRM redesign/expansion: a real Files/S3 module, a stat-cards+multi-view list page, a tabbed detail page, and targeted field additions

- **Not a new PRD story** — this is a significant redesign and expansion of the Contacts module built this session, on top of the two entries immediately below ("Contacts & CRM (F1)" and "Contacts & CRM (F1) follow-up"). Four pieces: (1) a real S3/MinIO-backed Files module, replacing the literal `@Module({})` stub that existed at `apps/api/src/modules/files/files.module.ts`; (2) a Contacts list page rebuild (stat cards, Table/List/Card view switcher, multiselect filters, Drawer-based create); (3) a Contacts detail page rebuild (back button, logo upload, Tabs replacing the prior session's two-column layout); (4) two targeted new fields (`taxId`, `website`) plus a `logoFileId`, in one migration.

- **Files module** (`apps/api/src/modules/files/`, new `apps/api/src/db/schema/files.ts`): `@aws-sdk/client-s3` + `@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner` added to `apps/api/package.json` (neither existed anywhere in the repo before — confirmed via `grep -r "@aws-sdk"` first, per the task brief). `s3-client.ts` reads `S3_ENDPOINT`/`S3_BUCKET`/`S3_ACCESS_KEY`/`S3_SECRET_KEY` and returns a `null` client (never throws) when `S3_ENDPOINT` is unset — confirmed directly against the real source via `S3_ENDPOINT="" npx tsx -e "..."` (see Verified) rather than assumed from reading the code. `forcePathStyle: true` is set since MinIO doesn't support virtual-hosted-style bucket addressing. Every caller (`FilesService.upload`/`getSignedUrl`) checks for `null` first and throws `ServiceUnavailableException` (503) with a clear "attachments are unavailable" message — per CLAUDE.md's graceful-degradation rule, this must degrade the file feature, never the process, and S3_* is correctly never in the 3-required-env-vars boot list.
- **`files` table** (org-scoped, same conventions as `contacts.ts`: `uuid` PK via `defaultRandom()`, `org_id` FK to Better Auth's `organization.id`, `timestamp(..., { withTimezone: true })`): `id`, `orgId`, `key`, `originalFilename`, `mimeType`, `sizeBytes`, `uploadedByUserId`, `createdAt`. **Deliberately not polymorphic** — no `attachableType`/`attachableId`. Checked this schema's own precedent for "referenced from elsewhere" first (`journal_lines.contact_id`/`job_id`, both plain nullable FKs to one specific table) and followed it: `contacts.logoFileId` is a plain nullable FK on `contacts` itself, not a field on `files`. When quotes/invoices need attachments later they get their own FK column(s), same pattern, not a speculative generic association built for hypothetical future consumers.
- **Upload/serve endpoints** (`files.controller.ts`): `POST /files` (`@OrgRoles(["owner", "staff"])`, `FileInterceptor("file")` from `@nestjs/platform-express` — already a dependency, `main.ts` already uses this platform adapter — plus `IdempotencyInterceptor`, in that decorator order so multer parses the multipart body into `request.file` before the idempotency check reads headers/session). `main.ts`'s `bodyParser: false` (needed so Better Auth can read its own raw request stream) does not interfere — multer/`FileInterceptor` attaches its own per-route middleware and never touches Nest's global body parser, confirmed by the upload endpoint working end-to-end live (see Verified), not just by reading the framework's docs. `GET /files/:id` 302-redirects to a short-lived (`300s`) signed URL rather than proxy-streaming bytes through the API process: this is a single-host, self-hosted deployment (`docker/Caddyfile` reverse-proxies `api`/`web` under one origin; MinIO is its own directly-reachable service per `docker-compose.dev.yml`/`.env.example`), so a signed URL avoids burning an API request-handling thread per download for a case with no per-download authorization logic beyond "does this file belong to my org" (already covered by the org-scoped lookup before signing). A proxy-stream approach would only earn its cost if MinIO weren't reachable from the browser directly, which isn't the case in this stack.
- **A real gap found and fixed, not just documented**: MinIO starts with zero buckets — neither `docker-compose.dev.yml` nor the prod compose file provisions the `spine` bucket, so the very first upload attempt failed live with `NoSuchBucket` during this session's own verification. Rather than requiring an undocumented manual `mc mb` step on every fresh environment, `FilesService` now self-heals: `HeadBucket` (checked, cached in-process after the first success) then `CreateBucket` only on a 404. Confirmed by deliberately removing the bucket (`mc rb --force local/spine`) and re-uploading — it recreated itself and the upload succeeded. Flagged in Next Up that a `docker-compose` init sidecar would still be the more conventional long-term fix.
- **`contacts.logoFileId`** (nullable FK to `files.id`, `onDelete: "set null"`) plus **Phase 4's two targeted fields**, `contacts.taxId` and `contacts.website` (both nullable text) — all three in the **same migration** (`apps/api/drizzle/0005_new_ben_urich.sql`, idx 5, the correct next sequential number after `0004_empty_storm`, confirmed against `apps/api/drizzle/meta/_journal.json` before generating). `contact_persons.title` already covers job title — confirmed by reading `contacts.ts` before assuming it was missing; no change made there, matching the task brief's explicit instruction to confirm first. Zod schemas (`packages/shared/src/schemas/contacts.ts`), `contacts.service.ts`'s `create`/`update` (additive only — the nested-array-replace and archive/delete/ledger-check logic is untouched), and `contacts.controller.ts` all updated to accept the three new fields.
- **Array-based type filter**: `contacts.controller.ts`'s `GET /contacts` now accepts repeated `?type=customer&type=vendor` query params (Express's default array-query parsing — a single value still arrives as a plain string and is normalized to a one-element array), replacing the prior singular `?type=`. `contacts.service.ts`'s `list` takes `types?: string[]` and uses Drizzle's `inArray` instead of a single `eq`. This was the one intentional, brief-sanctioned change to `contacts.service.ts`'s existing logic beyond the new fields.

- **Migration hiccup found and fixed, not worked around**: `pnpm --filter @spine/api db:migrate` (and `drizzle-kit migrate` run directly) hung/silently failed with no visible error (the CLI's spinner swallows the underlying exception). Root-caused by writing an isolated script calling `drizzle-orm`'s `migrate()` directly: the DB's `drizzle.__drizzle_migrations` row for `0004_empty_storm` had the *correct* content hash (verified via `shasum -a 256` matching exactly) but a **stale `created_at` timestamp** — `1786013237690` instead of `_journal.json`'s recorded `when` of `1786017251821` for that same entry, a leftover from a prior session's own migration-renumbering fix-up (see the "Contacts & CRM (F1)" entry below — "a note on how this landed" — which mentions exactly this kind of drift). Since `drizzle-orm`'s migrator compares `journal.when > lastAppliedRow.created_at` to decide what's new, this stale timestamp made it think migration 0004 (and therefore its `CREATE TYPE` statements) hadn't run yet, and it began replaying `0000_soft_silvermane.sql` from scratch, hitting `type "activity_type" already exists`. Fixed by correcting that one row's `created_at` in place (`UPDATE drizzle.__drizzle_migrations SET created_at = 1786017251821 WHERE id = 5 AND hash = '...'` — hash-guarded, not a blind row-5 update) rather than touching any migration file; `pnpm db:migrate` then applied `0005_new_ben_urich.sql` cleanly on the next run.

- **Contacts list page** (`apps/web/src/app/contacts/`, new `contacts-explorer.tsx`, `contacts-view-switcher.tsx`, `contacts-list-view.tsx`, `contacts-card-view.tsx`, `contacts-skeleton.tsx`, `contact-row-actions.tsx`, `contact-create-drawer.tsx`, `contact-logo.tsx`, `use-contact-stats.ts`): four `StatCard`s (total contacts, customers, vendors, active leads) computed from data already in memory — `total`/`customers`/`vendors` derived client-side from the already-fetched contact list (no fabricated numbers, per this codebase's established rule, see `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s own comment on why it omits stat cards until real data exists — Contacts now has real data, the opposite case), `activeLeads` from a single `GET /leads` counted client-side (stage not `won`/`lost`) rather than adding a dedicated `/contacts/stats` endpoint for four cheaply-derivable counts. A Table/List/Card `ToggleGroup` view switcher, persisted to `localStorage` (`spine.contacts.view`), matching `app-layout.tsx`'s existing sidebar-collapse persistence pattern exactly (read on mount in a `useEffect`, guarded by a `hydrated` flag to avoid an SSR/client mismatch flash). Table view is the existing `DataTable` plus a new Actions column; List is a denser single-column list; Card is a responsive grid — all three share one new `ContactRowActions` dropdown (Edit navigates to the detail page, which already has full inline-edit — no separate edit surface was built; Archive/Restore/Delete call the same endpoints the detail page uses, Delete behind the same `AlertDialog` pattern, kept as a fully-controlled `open`/`onOpenChange` state external to the `DropdownMenu` to avoid the exact Radix unmount-before-open problem this codebase already hit and solved once for the Settings dialog, see that entry below). Type/archived filtering uses `@spine/ui`'s existing `MultiSelect` against the new array-based `?type=` param. Loading states are `@spine/ui`'s `Skeleton`, shaped per the active view (skeleton rows/list-rows/cards), replacing the old full-page `<Spinner />`.
- **Add Contact is a Drawer**, opened from the list page's primary action (`@spine/ui`'s `Drawer`, composing the existing `ContactForm`'s actual create logic unchanged — only how it's hosted changed). **`/contacts/new` is kept as a route, not deleted**, but now just `redirect()`s to `/contacts?new=1` (server-side query param that auto-opens the drawer on first paint) rather than rendering its own full-page form. Reasoning, per the task brief's explicit ask: Settings' "Dialog only, no URLs" decision (see that entry below) was right for Settings because nothing in it is a shareable resource — it's pure org configuration. Contacts' create flow is different: it produces a real, bookmarkable resource once saved (`/contacts/:id`), and "here's how you add a contact" is itself a plausible link to share *before* that resource exists (an onboarding checklist item, a support doc, a "New" button someone right-clicks to open in a new tab). So the route stays live and useful, but redirects into the one real implementation (the Drawer) instead of maintaining two separate create forms in parallel.
- **Contact detail page** (`contact-detail.tsx`, rewritten; the new `contact-logo.tsx` is shared between the list's Card/List row avatars and this page's header): an explicit `ArrowLeft` "Back to contacts" button above the header (in addition to the existing `Breadcrumb`, not instead of it — a one-click back affordance reads clearer immediately next to the identity it returns from, on a detail page one level deep). Header now includes a clickable logo/avatar (`ContactLogo`, built on `@spine/ui`'s existing `Avatar`/`AvatarImage`/`AvatarFallback` — no new library primitive needed) wired to the new upload endpoint: click opens a native file picker, uploads via `apiClient.upload()` (a new method on `apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts`, since the existing `request()` always `JSON.stringify`s its body and sets `Content-Type: application/json`, neither correct for `multipart/form-data` — the browser must set its own boundary-bearing `Content-Type` from a `FormData` body), then `PUT /contacts/:id` with the returned `logoFileId`. All prior CRUD (inline edit, archive/restore, delete-with-`AlertDialog`) is preserved verbatim, just relocated into the new header shape, plus two new editable fields (Tax ID, Website) added to both the edit-mode form and the create Drawer's form. **Tabs replace the prior session's two-column layout**: "Contact persons", "Addresses", "Leads", "Activities" — each hosts the exact same section component as before (`ContactPersonsSection`/`AddressesSection`/`LeadsSection`/`ActivitiesSection`), unchanged in their own internal add/remove logic, only how they're now hosted. **No separate "Overview" tab** — the contact's own editable fields (name, type, email, phone, tax ID, website) live in the header's inline-edit, same as the prior session; a tab repeating only those same fields would have been redundant, and the task brief explicitly allowed skipping it.
- **`apiClient` gains `.upload()`** (`apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts`) alongside the existing `.get`/`.put`/`.post`/`.delete` — a fresh `Idempotency-Key` per call, matching every other mutating request, but building a `FormData` body and letting `fetch` derive `Content-Type` itself instead of JSON-encoding.
- No new `@spine/ui` primitives were needed anywhere in this pass — `StatCard`, `MultiSelect`, `Skeleton`, `Drawer`, `Tabs`, `Breadcrumb`, `DropdownMenu`, `ToggleGroup`, and `Avatar` all already existed with the exact shapes needed (confirmed by reading each one's source before using it, not assumed) — so per CLAUDE.md's rule, no `apps/design-system` doc page was added or is owed for this change.

- **Verified**, all against the live dev stack (`docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml` — Postgres and MinIO already running, confirmed via `docker compose ps` before starting; both dev servers restarted after this task's own production builds clobbered the dev `.next` cache again, same known gotcha as every prior entry — `rm -rf apps/web/.next` + relaunch each time, proactively, before curling). `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`, `lint`, `build` and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, `build` each run **sequentially**, all clean (the web build failed once mid-session with a `PageNotFoundError` from the exact concurrent-dev-server-vs-build `.next` race flagged in this file's own history — confirmed by killing the still-running dev server and rebuilding clean, not a real code error). `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck` and `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck` also clean (no `@spine/ui` exports were touched). Migration `0005_new_ben_urich.sql` applied (after the hash/timestamp fix above); confirmed via `psql` (`docker exec spine-dev-db-1 psql`, since no local `psql` client exists in this environment) that `files` exists with its two FKs (`org_id` → `organization`, `uploaded_by_user_id` → `user`) and one index, and that `contacts.tax_id`/`contacts.website`/`contacts.logo_file_id` all exist with `contacts_logo_file_id_files_id_fk` correctly referencing `files.id`.
- Signed up a fresh test user via curl, captured the session cookie, and exercised the full new surface directly against the API: uploaded a real file via `curl -F` (`POST /files`) — confirmed the returned `id`/`key`/`sizeBytes` match, confirmed the object exists in MinIO via `mc ls local/spine --recursive`, confirmed `GET /files/:id` 302s to a correctly-signed MinIO URL and that following it returns the exact original file content byte-for-byte; replayed the same `Idempotency-Key` on a second upload and confirmed the identical stored file `id` came back rather than a second object being created; confirmed a missing `file` field returns a clean `400`, not a crash. Created a contact with `taxId`/`website` set and confirmed both persisted and round-tripped on `GET`; set a contact's `logoFileId` to the uploaded file's id via `PUT` and confirmed it stuck. Confirmed the multi-type filter at all three shapes: `?type=customer`, `?type=vendor`, and `?type=customer&type=vendor` together, plus the unfiltered default — each returned exactly the expected subset. Re-ran the same archive/restore/hard-delete/ledger-protection checks the prior two Contacts entries established, confirming no regression: archived a contact (disappeared from the default list, reappeared with `?includeArchived=true`), restored it, hard-deleted a contact with no ledger history (200, then a confirmed `404` on follow-up `GET`), and specifically re-tested the ledger-block path — inserted a **balanced** journal entry/lines pair directly via `psql` referencing a fresh contact (a first attempt with an intentionally unbalanced pair was correctly rejected by the pre-existing DB trigger, `check_journal_entry_balanced()`, itself reconfirming Phase 0's ledger-integrity constraint is intact), then confirmed `DELETE` on that contact returned exactly `400 {"message":"Contact has financial ledger history and cannot be deleted — archive it instead"}` (the same string the UI's `Alert` shows) and that `POST /contacts/:id/archive` still succeeds as the escape hatch. Invited and accepted a second user as `field`: confirmed `GET /contacts?type=customer&type=vendor` succeeds (200) and `POST /files` is correctly rejected (`403 Insufficient permissions`) for that role — server-side, not client-trusted.
- Web: curled `/contacts` unauthenticated (307 to `/login`, unchanged middleware behavior) and authenticated (200, with the real stat-card labels — "Total contacts", "Customers", "Vendors", "Active leads" — and "Add contact" present in the rendered HTML, not just a status code); curled `/contacts?new=1` and confirmed it also renders (the Drawer's own content is Radix-portal-rendered client-side only, same as every other Dialog/Drawer in this codebase, so it correctly doesn't appear in server HTML either); curled `/contacts/new` and confirmed a real `307` redirect to `/contacts?new=1`; curled a real contact's `/contacts/:id` and confirmed `200` with the shell's real markup (`<aside>` sidebar, `aria-label="Breadcrumb"`, the page title) — per-contact content (name, tabs) loads client-side after hydration, same established architecture as every other detail-style page in this codebase, confirmed by this same session's own curl output, not assumed. The production build's route table lists `/contacts`, `/contacts/[id]`, and `/contacts/new` correctly.
- **Not verified, stated plainly, matching this session's established honesty pattern**: the actual click-through — the view switcher's three buttons, the Drawer opening/closing, Tabs switching, Skeleton appearing during a real loading window, and an actual image upload through a native file picker dialog. No browser-automation tooling is available in this environment; all of the above was code-reviewed and typechecked, and every underlying API call each of those interactions makes was curl-verified directly instead, but none of it was interactively verified in a real browser.
- **A pre-existing bug found, not fixed** (see "Next up" for the full writeup): `updateContactSchema`'s `.partial()` doesn't neutralize `isCompany`'s `z.boolean().default(false)`, so a `PUT` that omits `isCompany` entirely (e.g. this session's own logo-only `PUT { logoFileId }`) silently resets it to `false` — confirmed live during verification. Pre-existing (present before this session), and this task's brief explicitly scoped `contacts.service.ts`'s existing logic as untouchable beyond the new fields/array filter, so it's flagged rather than fixed.
- Stories: closes the remaining half of S-12.2 (real logo file upload, previously a URL text field) and materially extends S-01.1/S-01.2's list/detail UX; no new PRD stories, this is a redesign/expansion pass on top of the two Contacts entries below, not new scope.
- Phase: Phase 1 · Sales Spine (same phase as the entries it builds on).

### 2026-08-06 — Contacts & CRM (F1) follow-up: full CRUD in the web UI, and a redesigned two-column detail page

- **Correction, not a new feature:** the "Contacts & CRM (F1)" entry immediately below shipped incomplete — the API (`apps/api/src/modules/contacts/contacts.controller.ts`) already had full CRUD (`GET`/`GET :id`/`POST`/`PUT :id`/archive/restore/`DELETE :id`), but the web UI only ever wired Create. There was no way to edit a contact's own fields after creation, no delete, no archive/restore, and the detail page was a single stacked column with no header, no primary actions, and no create/delete confirmation anywhere. This entry closes both gaps: complete CRUD wiring, and an Attio/Twenty-style redesign of the detail page.
- **`apiClient` gains `.delete()`** (`apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts`) — the underlying `request()` already supported the DELETE verb; the exported `apiClient` object just never surfaced it.
- **Inline edit** on the detail page (`apps/web/src/app/contacts/contact-detail.tsx`): clicking "Edit" turns the fields panel (type, company/individual, name, email, phone) into an editable form in place with Save/Cancel, using `PUT /contacts/:id` — no separate route, per explicit direction. **Delete** is wired behind an `AlertDialog` confirmation and redirects to `/contacts` on success; the API's exact 400 message ("Contact has financial ledger history and cannot be deleted — archive it instead") is surfaced verbatim via an `Alert` rather than a generic failure, confirmed live (see Verified). **Archive**/**Restore** are header actions — Archive shown when active, Restore shown when `archivedAt` is set — using the existing `?includeArchived` list param (already supported API-side, still no list-page toggle exposed this pass, matching the original entry's stated scope) to confirm an archived contact stays reachable and clearly marked on its own detail page.
- **Detail page redesign**: a header row (name, type badge, "Company" badge, "Archived" badge when applicable, primary actions top-right — Edit is the one filled/primary action per `docs/DESIGN.md`, Archive/Restore secondary, Delete danger-styled behind its confirmation), then a two-column body — left holds the contact's own fields plus `ContactPersonsSection`/`AddressesSection` (who this is), right holds `LeadsSection`/`ActivitiesSection` (what's happening with them). Modeled on Mobbin references pulled this session for [Attio's contact detail](https://mobbin.com/screens/cf4f2b49-652f-4c76-8a7c-1ea4f57c10cf) (header row with name/badge/actions top-right, left-rail own-fields) and [Jobber's client page](https://mobbin.com/screens/4ee9338b-de48-4168-9069-38d3307446ff) (closest domain match, being also a service-business tool — properties/contacts left, work/billing right), with [Twenty's](https://mobbin.com/screens/1347da63-1157-4923-b196-46c42e6880ea) collapsible-fields panel as a secondary reference. Built entirely from existing plain-Tailwind flex/grid classes, matching this codebase's actual convention — checked `packages/ui/src/components/layout.tsx`'s `Box`/`Flex`/`Grid` primitives first and confirmed the rest of `apps/web` doesn't use them (Settings, Dashboard, the original Contacts pass all use raw Tailwind), so no new layout convention was introduced.
- **Toast on every create, `AlertDialog` on every delete**, across all five entities this module touches. New `apps/web/src/app/contacts/entity-toast.tsx` (`useEntityToast`) generalizes `apps/settings/saved-toast.tsx`'s fixed "Saved" composition (`Toast`/`ToastProvider`/`ToastTitle`/`ToastDescription`/`ToastViewport`) into a `show(title, description)` hook, since each of the five creates needs its own message ("Contact created," "Contact person added," "Address added," "Lead added," "Activity logged") rather than one fixed string — one `useEntityToast()` instance lives in `contact-detail.tsx` and is threaded down via `onCreated` callbacks rather than five separate toast instances. The contact-create flow is a special case: `contact-form.tsx` navigates straight to the new contact's detail page on success, so there's no moment on the form itself to show a toast — it sets a one-shot `sessionStorage` flag (`spine:contact-created`) that `contact-detail.tsx` reads and clears on mount instead. `AlertDialog` confirmation was added not just to the new top-level contact delete but retrofitted onto `contact-persons-section.tsx` and `addresses-section.tsx`'s existing `handleRemove` flows, which previously deleted immediately on icon click with zero confirmation.
- Verified against the live dev stack (`docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml` Postgres/MinIO already up; both `apps/api`/`apps/web` dev servers were running and were restarted after this task's own `pnpm --filter @spine/web build` clobbered the dev `.next` cache again — cleaned `apps/web/.next` and relaunched both dev servers proactively before curling, per this session's established pattern). `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, and `build` each run sequentially — all clean. Signed up a fresh test user via curl (`POST /api/auth/sign-up/email`), captured the session cookie, and drove the full lifecycle directly against the API: created a contact (`POST /contacts`), updated its own fields (`PUT /contacts/:id`, confirmed the new name/phone in the response), archived it (confirmed `archivedAt` set, confirmed it disappeared from the default `GET /contacts` list and reappeared with `?includeArchived=true`), restored it (confirmed `archivedAt` back to `null`), then deleted it with no ledger history (`DELETE /contacts/:id` → 200, follow-up `GET` → 404, confirming a true hard delete). Also tested the ledger-history-blocks-delete path specifically: created a second contact, inserted a balanced `journal_entries`/`journal_lines` row referencing it directly via `psql` (checked `apps/api/src/db/schema/ledger.ts` for the table shape first), then confirmed `DELETE /contacts/:id` returned exactly `400 {"message":"Contact has financial ledger history and cannot be deleted — archive it instead"}` — the same string now shown verbatim in the UI's `Alert` — and confirmed `POST /contacts/:id/archive` still succeeds as the escape hatch for that same contact. **Not verified, stated plainly:** the actual click-through (Edit button turning the panel editable, the `AlertDialog` opening/cancelling, a `Toast` appearing after a create) — no browser-automation tooling is available in this environment; this was code-reviewed and typechecked but not interactively verified in a browser, same honesty pattern as every prior entry this session.
- Stories: closes the remaining CRUD/UX gap in S-01.1/S-01.2 (edit/archive/restore/delete for the contact itself) left open by the entry below; no new stories, no scope change.
- Phase: Phase 1 · Sales Spine (same phase as the entry it corrects).

### 2026-08-06 — Contacts & CRM (F1) — the first real business module, Phase 1 begins

- **Schema**: five new tables (`apps/api/src/db/schema/contacts.ts`) — `contacts` (customer/vendor/both, company-or-individual, soft-delete via `archivedAt` rather than hard delete), `contact_persons` and `addresses` (both hold-many, per contact), `leads` (fixed `new/qualified/proposal/won/lost` stage enum — true per-org configurability deferred, see Next Up), `activities` (call/meeting/note/task, `dueAt`/`doneAt`, linkable to a contact and/or a lead). `journal_lines.contact_id` (previously a bare, unconstrained `uuid`) now has a real FK to `contacts.id` with `onDelete: "restrict"` — a contact referenced by ledger history can't be hard-deleted out from under posted journal lines, which is also why `contacts.service.ts`'s `delete` checks for ledger references first and directs to archive instead.
- **API** (`apps/api/src/modules/contacts/`): full CRUD for contacts (nested `contactPersons`/`addresses` accepted on create, and on update as a whole-array replace — not an incremental patch), leads, and activities, plus `GET /activities/overdue` for S-01.7 ("a list of overdue follow-ups on my dashboard," scoped to the requesting user). Every mutating route is `@OrgRoles(["owner", "staff"])` + the existing `IdempotencyInterceptor`; GET routes carry no role restriction so `field` can read per §2.1's table ("Manage contacts & catalogue: Owner ✅, Staff ✅, Field View"). Request validation via new zod schemas in `packages/shared/src/schemas/contacts.ts`.
- **Two real, previously-latent bugs found and fixed, not specific to Contacts**: (1) `packages/shared`'s own internal re-exports (`export * from "./money"` etc.) used extensionless relative imports, which is fine under bundler resolution (`apps/web`, `apps/design-system`) but breaks under Node's native ESM loader once a *require()'d* `"type": "module"` package's imports get resolved directly — exactly what happens when `apps/api`'s CJS Nest build reaches across the `node_modules` symlink into `@spine/shared` at runtime (`ERR_UNSUPPORTED_DIR_IMPORT`). This had never surfaced because nothing in `apps/api` imported `@spine/shared` before Contacts needed its zod schemas. Fixed at the root: `packages/shared`'s internal imports now use real `.ts` extensions, with `rewriteRelativeImportExtensions: true` added to `apps/api/tsconfig.json` (TS 5.7+'s purpose-built flag for exactly this — rewrites `.ts`→`.js` in emitted output) and `allowImportingTsExtensions: true` added to the three `noEmit` consumer configs (`packages/shared`, `packages/ui`, `apps/web` via `@spine/config/tsconfig/nextjs`) that type-check `@spine/shared`'s source transitively. (2) A `bigint` (money's minor-unit representation, e.g. `leads.valueAmount`) reaching `JSON.stringify` anywhere — an API response, or `IdempotencyInterceptor` persisting a response body into a `jsonb` column — throws by default; `apps/api/src/common/bigint-json.ts` patches `BigInt.prototype.toJSON` once at process start (`main.ts`) to serialize as a decimal string, the same shape `moneySchema`'s `z.coerce.bigint()` already parses back on the way in. Same root cause as (1): first thing in the codebase to actually carry a `bigint` over HTTP, since `ledger`/`reports` are still stubs.
- **Web** (`apps/web/src/app/contacts/`): a list page (`@spine/ui`'s `DataTable`, search + sort), a create page, and a detail page composing four sections — contact persons, addresses, leads (flat list, not a kanban board — see Next Up), and activities (log + mark done/undone, overdue flagged inline). All under the shared `AppShell` via `AppLayout`; nav sections were extracted to `apps/web/src/lib/nav-sections.tsx` since Contacts is the second page needing that array (Dashboard was the first). No new `@spine/ui` primitives were needed — entirely composed from the existing set (`DataTable`, `MoneyInput`, `Select`, `Checkbox`, `Badge`, `FormField`), so no design-system doc page is needed for this entry (matching the established precedent that app-level page composition doesn't get one, only new/changed library primitives do).
- **A note on how this landed**: this task's first attempt stalled twice mid-build in an isolated worktree (a background-agent watchdog killed it for inactivity) after producing solid, correct API-layer work but before finishing the web UI or fully wiring two of its own fixes. Rather than restart from scratch, the API-layer work (schema, controllers, services, zod schemas, both bug fixes above) was reviewed file-by-file, corrected where the worktree's own migration-numbering had drifted from `main` (regenerated cleanly as `0004_empty_storm`, not the worktree's colliding, differently-numbered attempt), and carried over; a separate worktree change that had silently reimplemented `AppLayout`/`AppShell` from scratch — discarding the already-shipped Settings-dialog/user-menu/theme-toggle integration from a prior commit — was caught and rejected in favor of `main`'s real version. The web UI itself was built fresh against that real, correct `AppLayout`.
- **Verified**, all against the live dev stack (Postgres/MinIO via `docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml`, both `apps/api`/`apps/web` dev servers restarted after the migration and after a production build had (again) clobbered the dev `.next` cache): `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint build` clean across all six packages (run sequentially — a concurrent `build`+`typecheck` on `apps/web` raced on `.next` and produced spurious `TS6053` errors, a known tooling gotcha from an earlier entry this session, not a real error, confirmed by rerunning sequentially). Migration `0004_empty_storm.sql` generated and applied; confirmed via `psql` that `contacts`/`leads`/`activities`/`addresses`/`contact_persons` exist and `journal_lines`'s new FK is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`. `apps/api` boots clean with all new routes mapped (confirmed in its own startup log, not just a 200 on `/health`). Live curl flow against a fresh signup: created a company contact with a nested contact person and address in one call; created a lead with a real money value (confirms the bigint fix — response showed `"valueAmount":"1500000"`, a string, not a crash); logged an overdue activity, confirmed it appeared in `/activities/overdue`, marked it done, confirmed it dropped off that list; idempotency replay confirmed via two identical-key POSTs returning the exact same stored `id`/timestamp rather than double-applying a second, different payload. Invited and accepted a second user as `field` role — confirmed GET `/contacts` succeeds (200) and POST `/contacts` is correctly rejected (403 `Insufficient permissions`) for that role. Web: full production build lists all three new routes (`/contacts`, `/contacts/[id]`, `/contacts/new`); curled all three with a real session cookie and confirmed actual rendered content (page titles, "Add contact" button, form fields — not just status codes); confirmed the dashboard sidebar now links to `/contacts`; confirmed unauthenticated `GET /contacts` redirects to `/login`. Not verified: real browser click-through of the multi-section detail page (add-a-person, add-a-lead, mark-activity-done as actual UI interactions) — no browser-automation tooling available in this environment, same caveat as every prior entry this session; the underlying API calls each of those UI actions makes were curl-verified directly instead.
- Stories: S-01.1, S-01.2, S-01.4, S-01.6, S-01.7 (MUST-level F1). S-01.3, S-01.5, S-01.8 explicitly deferred, see Next Up.
- Phase: Phase 1 · Sales Spine (first story landed in this phase; Phase 0 · Foundation is otherwise complete per the Status line above).

### 2026-08-06 — Settings becomes a dialog (supersedes the routed-`/settings` approach from the entry just below)

- **Correction, not a new feature:** the immediately-prior "Shared `AppShell`" entry wired Settings up as 8 routed pages (`/settings/business`, `/settings/team`, etc.) under a shared shell. Two decisions confirmed with the user replace that: Settings opens as a **dialog**, not routed pages ("Dialog only, no URLs" — no per-section deep link), triggered from the sidebar's user-menu dropdown rather than being its own nav destination. The routed pages this entry deletes were only ever live for one prior session.
- New `apps/web/src/components/settings-dialog.tsx` (`SettingsDialog`) — a two-pane dialog: a 256px-wide left column (search input filtering by label, substring match; a vertical section list below it, active section highlighted via local `useState`, not `usePathname` — there is no route per section anymore) and a right content pane rendering the selected section. Reopening the dialog always resets to the "Business profile" section with an empty search, rather than remembering the last-viewed section.
- The 8 old `apps/web/src/app/settings/<section>/page.tsx` route files are deleted; their actual content (the `Card` + reused onboarding step component + save-to-`/org/settings/:key` logic) moved as-is into 8 new pane components under `apps/web/src/app/settings/panes/` (`business-pane.tsx`, `template-pane.tsx`, `currency-pane.tsx`, `fiscal-year-pane.tsx`, `tax-pane.tsx`, `numbering-pane.tsx`, `accounts-pane.tsx`, `team-pane.tsx`) — a rename+relocate, not a rewrite; no logic duplicated between an old route and a new pane since the old routes no longer exist. `apps/web/src/app/settings/layout.tsx` and `page.tsx` (the redirect-to-business shim) are also deleted. `use-settings-value.ts`, `saved-toast.tsx`, and `team/member-row.tsx` are kept in their original locations and imported by the new panes — none of the three were Settings-route-specific.
- **The DropdownMenu-triggering-a-Dialog problem:** Radix's `DropdownMenu` unmounts its items — including any `DialogTrigger` nested inside one — the instant `onSelect` fires, which tears the `Dialog` down before it can ever open if wired the naive way (`DialogTrigger` inside `DropdownMenuItem`). Solved by keeping the dialog's `open` state external to the dropdown: `AppUserMenu` (`apps/web/src/components/app-user-menu.tsx`) now owns a `useState` for `settingsOpen`, renders `<SettingsDialog open={settingsOpen} onOpenChange={setSettingsOpen} />` as a sibling of the `DropdownMenu` (not nested inside it), and the new "Settings" `DropdownMenuItem` (gear icon, matching the existing item style) just calls `setSettingsOpen(true)` from `onSelect` — no `DialogTrigger` involved at all.
- `packages/ui/src/components/dialog.tsx`: added a `size` prop to `DialogContent` (`"md"` default, unchanged; new `"lg"` — roughly 2/3 viewport width, near-full height, no default padding since large panels compose their own internal padded sub-regions). This is a genuine new `@spine/ui` primitive capability, not an app-level-only change, so its doc page at `apps/design-system/src/app/components/dialog/page.tsx` was updated in the same change: the `size` prop added to `DialogContent`'s API table, plus a new "Sizes" section with a live two-pane preview demonstrating the pattern `SettingsDialog` uses. No `apps/design-system/src/lib/nav.ts` change was needed (Dialog already has a nav entry). `SettingsDialog` itself is **not** a `@spine/ui` component and does not get a design-system doc page — it's an app-specific composition of existing primitives living in `apps/web`, matching how `apps/onboarding/*` and other app-level compositions have never had design-system pages, only the primitives underneath them do.
- Sidebar nav updated now that Settings has no route: `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s `SECTIONS` drops the "Organisation" group (which held a single `/settings` link) entirely — the persistent sidebar now shows just "Dashboard" under "Overview". `apps/web/src/middleware.ts` drops `/settings` from `PROTECTED_PATHS` and its route matcher (protecting a route that no longer exists was dead code); confirmed via `grep -rn '"/settings' apps/web/src` that nothing else references a `/settings/...` path after these changes.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck lint`, `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck lint build`, `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck lint build` — all clean. Hit the same stale-`.next`-cache issue flagged as a known risk in this session's history (this time after this task's own production `build` run) — fixed proactively by killing the dev server, deleting `apps/web/.next`, and restarting via `pnpm --filter @spine/web dev` before doing any curl verification, rather than waiting to hit a 500. Signed up a fresh test user via curl (`POST /api/auth/sign-up/email`), captured the session cookie, curled `/dashboard` (200; zero occurrences of the 8 old section labels like "Business profile"/"Chart of accounts" in the HTML, confirming the sidebar no longer lists them) and confirmed `/settings` and `/settings/business` both now return a clean Next.js 404 rather than a 500, since their route files are gone. The production build's route table (`pnpm --filter @spine/web build`) shows no `/settings*` entries at all. **Not verified, stated plainly:** actually clicking the user-menu avatar, selecting "Settings", watching the dialog open, searching, and switching between panes — this needs a real browser, which wasn't available in this environment. The dropdown/dialog content is Radix-portal-rendered only on client interaction, so it correctly does not appear in server-rendered HTML either — this was confirmed by code review and typecheck only, not interactive testing, same honesty pattern as every prior entry this session.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — Shared `AppShell`: one persistent sidebar+content shell for `/dashboard` and `/settings`

- New `@spine/ui` compound component, `packages/ui/src/components/app-shell.tsx` (`AppShell`, `AppShellSidebar`, `AppShellHeader`, `AppShellContent`, plus `AppShellNavItem`/`AppShellNavSection` types) — the persistent chrome every signed-in area of Spine now renders inside, replacing Settings' standalone shell from the previous session rather than sitting alongside it. Built compound, matching the existing `Card`/`Sidebar` granularity rather than one prop-heavy component: `AppShellSidebar` composes the existing `Sidebar`/`SidebarHeader`/`SidebarContent`/`SidebarGroup`/`SidebarNavItem`/`SidebarFooter` primitives (all pre-existing, unmodified in shape) around a caller-supplied `sections` prop (grouped `{href, label, icon}` items) plus `isActive`/`renderLink` callbacks so `@spine/ui` never imports `next/link` directly; `AppShellHeader` is an optional breadcrumb+title+description+action row built on the existing `Breadcrumb` primitive; `AppShellContent` is the scrollable main region capped to the 1200px container width from `docs/DESIGN.md`.
- Real gap found and fixed in `Sidebar`, not worked around: `SidebarNavItem` only ever rendered a plain `<button>`, so there was no way to make a nav row an actual `<a href>` (needed for real navigation/right-click/open-in-new-tab, and for `renderLink` to hand back a Next `<Link>`). Added `asChild` (Radix `Slot`, same pattern `Button` already uses) — in `asChild` mode the caller supplies the fully composed icon+label markup as the single child themselves, since Slot requires exactly one child and can't also receive `SidebarNavItem`'s own icon/label spans. This is a real, deliberate extension of an existing primitive, not a fork.
- Collapse state lives in a new `apps/web/src/components/app-layout.tsx` (`AppLayout`, one shared client component both `/dashboard` and `/settings/layout.tsx` render) rather than inside `@spine/ui`'s `AppShellSidebar` itself, matching the task's instruction to keep `AppShell` a controlled/stateless compound component — persisted to `localStorage` (`spine.sidebar.collapsed`) via plain `useState`+`useEffect`, not `next-themes`' pattern, since this isn't theme state and doesn't need SSR/cookie coordination. `apps/web/src/components/app-user-menu.tsx` (`AppUserMenu`) is the sidebar footer's user menu — avatar + name/email (hidden when collapsed, trigger stays just the avatar), a `DropdownMenu` (existing Radix-based primitive) with a theme toggle and sign-out.
- Theme control moved from the floating `ThemeToggle` button into the shell's chrome (the user menu dropdown) for `/dashboard` and `/settings`, since there's now real persistent chrome to put it in and the reference screenshot shows chrome-integrated controls, not a floating button. `/login`, `/signup`, and `/onboarding` are unmodified and keep their own floating `ThemeToggle` — they render outside this shell (no session/org context yet), confirmed no import of it was touched on those three pages.
- `/dashboard` (`apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`) and `/settings` (`apps/web/src/app/settings/layout.tsx`) both now render inside `AppLayout` with their own `sections`: dashboard gets "Dashboard" under "Overview" and a "Settings" link under "Organisation" (nothing invented for modules that don't exist yet — no Users/Contacts/etc.); Settings' old `SECTIONS` array (business profile → team, 8 items) moved as-is into its own layout as the "Organisation" group, plus the same "Dashboard" link under "Overview" so both areas share one nav shape. `apps/web/src/app/settings/settings-nav.tsx` — the bespoke sidebar it replaces — is deleted; its old top bar (back-link + floating toggle) is gone too, replaced by the shared shell. `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/sign-out-button.tsx` is also deleted — sign-out now lives in `AppUserMenu`, and nothing else referenced the standalone button (confirmed via grep before deleting either file).
- **Stat cards deliberately omitted from `/dashboard`.** The reference screenshot's dashboard shows 4 KPI stat cards; `@spine/ui` already has `StatCard` (built in an earlier session, unused until now) with exactly the right shape (`label`/`value`/`trend`), but Spine has no real contacts/quotes/invoices data anywhere yet to back genuine numbers. Rather than fabricate placeholder metrics or wire up a stretch metric (e.g. team member count) that doesn't really fit stat-card semantics, the row is omitted entirely — the existing `SetupChecklist` (unmodified, still static-placeholder per its own documented gap) is the dashboard's only content below the header. `StatCard` is proven working elsewhere (`apps/design-system`'s Templates page) and ready to drop in once quotes/invoices exist.
- Doc page: `apps/design-system/src/app/components/app-shell/page.tsx`, following the existing Features/Anatomy/API Reference/Examples/Accessibility template (closest precedent: `apps/design-system/src/app/components/sidebar/page.tsx`), with a fully interactive demo (working collapse toggle + fake in-page nav switching, since a real router isn't available inside the docs app). Added to `apps/design-system/src/lib/nav.ts` under a new "Application shell" section within the existing "Layouts" top-level slug — logically a layout-level component, not a components-catalogue primitive, and not sharing an item list with the structural primitives it's a level above.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck lint`, `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck lint build`, and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck lint build` all clean (no build script on `@spine/ui`, matching prior-session precedent). Hit a real stale-`.next`-cache 500 on `/dashboard` mid-verification (the exact failure mode flagged as a known risk going in, from an earlier production build in this session's history) — fixed by killing the dev server, deleting `apps/web/.next`, and restarting via `pnpm --filter @spine/web dev` rather than fighting the cache.
