After the restart: signed up a fresh test user via curl (`POST /api/auth/sign-up/email`), captured the session cookie, curled `/dashboard` and `/settings/business` and confirmed 200 with the new shell's real markup present — grepped the HTML for `<aside>` (sidebar landmark), `<nav aria-label="Breadcrumb">`, `aria-current="page"` on the active nav item, and the signed-in user's actual name/email in the user-menu markup, not just a 200. Confirmed via `curl`+`grep` that `/components/app-shell` on the design-system app (port 3100) returns 200 with `AppShell`/`AppShellSidebar`/`AppShellHeader` text present. No browser-automation tooling was available in this environment — collapse-toggle animation, actual visual match to the reference screenshot's structure, and dark-mode rendering were not visually verified, only confirmed via source/build/curl, same honesty pattern as every prior entry this session.
- Stories: none directly numbered in the PRD for a shared app shell — this closes the "no shared header/nav shell exists" gap an earlier Settings-only entry explicitly flagged as deliberately out of scope for that pass.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — Real Settings app (S-10.1, S-10.2, S-10.3, S-10.5) — post-onboarding editing, team list + invite, and deactivate-without-delete

- `apps/web/src/app/settings/`: a real Settings area with a left `Sidebar` nav (business profile, industry template, currency & locale, financial year, tax, numbering, chart of accounts, team) and a content pane, plus its own minimal top bar (`layout.tsx` — back-to-dashboard link + the existing floating `ThemeToggle`). Scoped this shell to `/settings` only, not a global app-wide header shared with `/dashboard`: dashboard is still a single page with nothing to navigate between, so a global nav would be premature scaffolding — flagged explicitly rather than silently built. `/dashboard` gets a "Settings" button linking in; Settings gets "Back to dashboard".
- S-10.1 (business profile, industry template, currency/locale, financial year, tax, numbering, chart of accounts): each is its own page under `apps/web/src/app/settings/<section>/page.tsx` that mounts the *exact same* onboarding step component (`BusinessProfileStep`, `IndustryTemplateStep`, `CurrencyLocaleStep`, `FinancialYearStep`, `TaxRateStep`, `NumberingStep`, `ChartOfAccountsStep`), reading/writing the exact same `/org/settings/:key` (or `/org/tax/default-rate`, `/org/numbering/:documentType`, `/org/accounts/:id`) endpoints the wizard already used. This was mostly composition, confirmed by reading every step component's actual props before touching anything: all seven already took `onSkip?` as optional (nothing forced a "skip to dashboard" affordance to render), so zero forking was needed there. The one real gap was cosmetic, not structural — five of the seven step components (`IndustryTemplateStep`, `CurrencyLocaleStep`, `FinancialYearStep`, `TaxRateStep`, `NumberingStep`) hard-coded their button label to "Continue" (only `BusinessProfileStep` already had an optional `saveLabel` prop); added the same optional `saveLabel = "Continue"` prop to all five so Settings can pass `"Save"` without a wizard-flavoured button in a non-wizard screen — a minimal, additive, backward-compatible change per component, not a fork. `ChartOfAccountsStep`'s `onContinue` is left required (onboarding still needs it to advance) and Settings passes a no-op, since every rename already persists immediately via `onRename` and there's nothing to "continue" to.
- A shared `apps/web/src/app/settings/use-settings-value.ts` (identical logic to onboarding's local `useSettingsValue` hook, extracted so Settings' six generic-key pages don't each reimplement it) and `apps/web/src/app/settings/saved-toast.tsx` (a `Toast` confirming each save — Settings has no "advance to next step" to signal success, so every section needs its own explicit confirmation).
- S-10.2 (list + invite): `GET /org/members` is a brand-new endpoint (`apps/api/src/modules/org/members.controller.ts` + `members.service.ts`, the structural twin of `accounts.controller.ts`/`accounts.service.ts` per the task brief) — nothing in the codebase listed members before this; onboarding's `invite-team-step.tsx` only ever sent invites. `apps/web/src/app/settings/team/page.tsx` renders the list (`apps/web/src/app/settings/team/member-row.tsx`, one row per member: avatar initials, name/email, role, deactivate/reactivate action) and reuses `InviteTeamStep` directly underneath, same as onboarding. Added one optional prop to `InviteTeamStep` (`hideFooter?: boolean`) to suppress its wizard-flavoured "Skip — finish setup" / "Finish" footer button, which reads oddly with no wizard to finish — Settings calls its own `reload()` after each successful invite instead.
- S-10.3 (deactivate without deleting history): added `isActive`/`deactivatedAt` columns directly to Better Auth's `member` table (`apps/api/src/db/schema/auth.ts`, migration `apps/api/drizzle/0003_ancient_silver_fox.sql`) rather than using Better Auth's admin-plugin `user.banned` field — checked its actual schema (`better-auth/dist/plugins/admin/schema.mjs`) and confirmed `banned`/`banReason`/`banExpires` live on `user`, i.e. a global, instance-wide ban, not scoped to a single organisation's membership. Since deactivation here must only revoke *this org's* access and must never touch anything else keyed by `user_id` (journal lines, audit entries), a per-membership flag on `member` is the correct minimal addition; duplicating Better Auth's global ban would have been the wrong shape for a per-org action. `PUT /org/members/:id/deactivate` / `/reactivate` are owner-only (`@OrgRoles(["owner"])`) and idempotent (`IdempotencyInterceptor`, matching every other mutating endpoint). `MembersService.setActive` refuses to deactivate the sole active owner of an org (verified below) so an owner can't lock themselves and everyone else out of every owner-only endpoint. Deactivated members render with a `Badge` ("Deactivated") in the list per the reserved-`StatusChip`-for-financial-status-only rule, and the row's action button flips to "Reactivate"; deactivating goes through an `AlertDialog` confirmation step (existing component, not built for this) describing exactly what is and isn't affected.
- **Known gap, stated plainly:** the `isActive` flag is purely a data flag in this pass — nothing yet *enforces* it. `@OrgRoles` (from `@thallesp/nestjs-better-auth`) resolves role off the session's active-organization membership and has no awareness of the new column, so a deactivated member's existing session/JWT is not actually rejected by any endpoint yet; enforcing that would need a new guard checking `member.isActive` on every request, which touches every module and was judged out of scope for "the API endpoint and the Settings UI action" as scoped in the task brief. Flagging this explicitly rather than letting it look done — the flag flips and is visible, but doesn't yet block access on its own.
- Added `/settings` to `apps/web/src/middleware.ts`'s `PROTECTED_PATHS` and its matcher, same pattern as `/dashboard`/`/onboarding`.
- Deferred per the task brief (not built, not half-built): S-10.4 (logo/colours on documents — no document rendering exists yet) and S-10.6 (audit log UI — `audit_log` has zero writers anywhere in the codebase). See "Next up".
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck`/`lint`/`build` and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` all clean; `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck` also clean (no `@spine/ui` exports touched, so the design-system doc app is unaffected — correctly, since this task only added components/pages at the app level). All 8 `/settings/*` routes prerender in the production build. Ran the migration against the live dev DB (`pnpm db:migrate:dev`). Exercised the real flow end-to-end against the live API with curl: signed up a fresh owner (`POST /api/auth/sign-up/email`), confirmed `GET /org/members` returns exactly that owner; round-tripped `PUT`/`GET` on `/org/settings/business_profile`, `/org/tax/default-rate`, and `/org/numbering/invoice` (S-10.1/S-10.5) with matching values on read-back; invited a second user's email via `POST /org/invitations`, had that user sign up and accept via `POST /api/auth/organization/accept-invitation`, confirmed they appear in `GET /org/members` as `staff`; deactivated them via `PUT /org/members/:id/deactivate`, confirmed a second `GET` shows `isActive:false` and a `deactivatedAt` timestamp; replayed the identical `Idempotency-Key` on the same deactivate call and got back the exact same stored response (same `deactivatedAt` timestamp both times, not a re-executed write) — the existing idempotency pattern behaves as designed on a new endpoint; reactivated and confirmed the flag flipped back on a fresh `GET`; confirmed the "can't deactivate the sole owner" guard rejects with 400; confirmed a `staff` session gets 403 attempting to deactivate the owner (server-side role check, not client-trusted). Curled `/settings` and all 8 `/settings/<section>` routes on the web app both unauthenticated (307 to `/login`, matching existing middleware behavior) and with a valid session cookie (200, with real server-rendered shell content — nav, page titles — confirmed via grep on the actual HTML; per-section data itself loads client-side after hydration via `apiClient`, same architecture as onboarding, so member names etc. aren't in the initial server HTML, only after JS runs). No browser-automation tooling was available in this environment, so the confirmation step's visual appearance, dark-mode rendering, and sidebar active-state highlighting were not visually verified — only confirmed via source/build/curl.
- Stories: S-10.1, S-10.2, S-10.3, S-10.5.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — Fixed `dark:` utilities never tracking the real theme toggle, and made the toggle a floating button

- **Real bug, not just a follow-up:** the previous entry's dark-mode wiring set `next-themes`' `data-theme` attribute correctly and `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css`'s hand-written `:root[data-theme="dark"] { ... }` token block responded to it correctly — but Tailwind v4's generated `dark:` *utility classes* (used for the logo swap: `dark:hidden` / `hidden dark:block`) are a separate mechanism from that hand-written block and default to `prefers-color-scheme` unless a `@custom-variant dark (...)` is registered. No such registration existed, so `dark:` utilities anywhere in the monorepo were silently still following the OS setting, completely ignoring the in-app toggle — clicking the toggle changed every token-driven color correctly but never the logo. The previous entry's own verification didn't catch this because it checked the hand-written token block, not the generated utility-class behavior, and no browser was available to see the mismatch visually.
- Fixed in one place — `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css` now has `@custom-variant dark ([data-theme="dark"] &);` near the top, before the token declarations. This repoints `dark:` for every consumer of the shared preset (`apps/web`, `apps/design-system`, and anything in `packages/ui` that ever uses a `dark:` utility), not just the logo call sites, so this class of bug can't resurface elsewhere.
- `apps/web/src/components/theme-toggle.tsx`: per explicit request, changed from an inline icon button (sitting next to the logo in each page's header row) to a floating button — `fixed bottom-6 right-6 z-50`, a circular `rounded-full` surface with a border and shadow so it reads as an overlay control rather than part of the page flow. Left the `<ThemeToggle />` element in its existing JSX position in each of the four pages (`/login`, `/signup`, `/onboarding`, `/dashboard`) since `fixed` positioning makes DOM placement irrelevant to where it renders — moving it would have been pure churn for no visual change.
- Verified: `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`/`lint`/`build` and `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system build` all clean (the preset is shared, so both consumers were checked, not just the one that surfaced the bug). Confirmed the fix from source through render: found the correct `@custom-variant` syntax for Tailwind v4 by trial (an initial `&:where(A, B)` form failed to parse — Lightning CSS wanted `&` used differently; `([data-theme="dark"] &)` compiled cleanly). Restarted the `apps/web` dev server (a stray production `pnpm build` had clobbered its dev-mode `.next` cache, an unrelated tooling hiccup, not a symptom of this fix) and curled `/login` and `/signup`: rendered HTML shows the logo `<img>` tags with unchanged `dark:hidden`/`hidden dark:block` classes (markup itself needed no changes — only the CSS variant registration was broken) and the floating-button classes on the toggle. Fetched the actual compiled `/_next/static/css/app/layout.css` and confirmed the generated rules are now `.dark\:hidden:where([data-theme="dark"] *, [data-theme="dark"]) { display: none; }` and the `.dark\:block` equivalent — selecting on the attribute `next-themes` actually sets, not `prefers-color-scheme`. Same no-flash-script check as the prior entry reconfirmed the attribute/default-theme config is unchanged. Full visual click-and-watch-it-flip verification still wasn't possible without browser tooling — the compiled-CSS-selector check is the closest available substitute, same caveat as every prior entry this session.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — Wired real dark/light mode persistence into `apps/web`

- **Closed the gap flagged in the previous entry's "Next up":** `apps/web`'s logo used a CSS-only `prefers-color-scheme` fallback specifically because no live theme system existed yet in this app (only `apps/design-system` had one, via `next-themes`). This session ports that exact setup over rather than inventing a second strategy.
- New `apps/web/src/components/theme-provider.tsx` — a byte-for-byte port of `apps/design-system/src/components/theme-provider.tsx`: `next-themes`' `ThemeProvider` with `attribute="data-theme" defaultTheme="system" enableSystem`. Confirmed this is the same attribute `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css` already selects on (`:root[data-theme="dark"]`, with a `prefers-color-scheme` fallback for when no attribute is set) before wiring anything, so no new CSS strategy was introduced — `apps/web`'s Tailwind entry point (`apps/web/src/app/globals.css`) already pulls in that preset via `@import "@spine/config/tailwind"` and needed no changes.
- New `apps/web/src/components/theme-toggle.tsx` — a byte-for-byte port of `apps/design-system/src/components/theme-toggle.tsx`: icon button, `lucide-react`'s `Sun`/`Moon`, `useTheme()` from `next-themes`, a `mounted` guard rendering `Moon` until mounted to avoid hydration mismatch.
- `apps/web/package.json` gained `next-themes` (`^0.4.6`, matching `apps/design-system`'s exact version) and `lucide-react` (`^0.469.0`) as **direct** dependencies. Checked the precedent from the prior entry's `Stepper` promotion first: `packages/ui` depends on `lucide-react` directly and its internal components import from it freely, but that entry deliberately kept `apps/web`'s old wizard-stepper checkmark as a plain `"✓"` glyph specifically because `apps/web` did *not* declare `lucide-react` itself — reaching past `@spine/ui` for icons was treated as needing its own direct dependency, not a transitive one. Same rule applied here.
- `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` now wraps `{children}` in `<ThemeProvider>` (previously bare — no provider, no toggle, nothing).
- **No shared header/nav shell was built.** Considered it, since the same logo pairing was added to `/login`, `/signup`, and the onboarding shell in the last entry, and this task touches those same three spots plus `/dashboard`. Decided against it: each page currently arranges its logo differently (login/signup: a standalone `<Image>` pair above a `Card`; onboarding: the same pair above the `Stepper`, at the wizard-shell level so it's not duplicated per-step; dashboard: no logo at all today). A shared header would mean introducing a new layout convention (fixed height, container, a placeholder for future nav) not otherwise justified by this task's scope of "wiring dark mode in cleanly." Instead, the toggle was placed consistently: on `/login` and `/signup`, the existing two-`<Image>` block is now wrapped in a `flex justify-between` row with `ThemeToggle` on the right; same treatment on `apps/web/src/app/onboarding/page.tsx`'s top-level shell (once, not per-step); on `/dashboard` (no logo, no existing top row), a bare `flex justify-end` row holding just the toggle sits above its `Card`. **Flagging this as a deferred opportunity**: a real shared header (logo + toggle, maybe later nav) is the more scalable answer once a second page needs the same pairing again — same "promote on the second call site" reasoning the `Stepper` entry used.
- Verified against the live dev stack (Postgres/MinIO healthy via `docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml`; `apps/web` already running on :3000, `apps/api` on :4000 — neither needed restarting): `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, and `build` all clean; build output lists `/login`, `/signup`, `/onboarding`, `/dashboard` all present. Runtime: curled `/login` and `/signup` on :3000 — both contain `aria-label="Toggle theme"` in the rendered HTML, confirming the toggle actually renders (not just typechecks). `/onboarding` and `/dashboard` both 307-redirect to `/login` when curled unauthenticated, matching the existing middleware behavior documented in `dashboard/page.tsx`'s own comment — not a regression, just untestable via a bare curl without a session cookie this session didn't need to mint. Inspected the actual injected no-flash script in `/login`'s HTML and confirmed it's `next-themes`' standard script called with `("data-theme","theme","system",null,["light","dark"],null,true,true)` — i.e. attribute `data-theme`, default `system`, system-tracking on — exactly matching `ThemeProvider`'s config and the exact attribute `tailwind-preset.css` selects on. Full visual verification (actually seeing the toggle flip colors in a browser) wasn't possible without browser-automation tooling in this environment; the code-level and injected-script checks above are the closest available substitute, same caveat pattern as the previous entry.
- Deviations from the brief: none. The brief explicitly left the shared-header-vs-per-page decision to judgment with permission to defer if out of scope — chose per-page and documented the gap.

### 2026-08-06 — Promoted the wizard stepper into `@spine/ui`, and added the Spine logo to auth/onboarding pages

- **Stepper promoted from the onboarding wizard into `@spine/ui`.** The previous session's `WizardStepper` (`apps/web/src/app/onboarding/wizard-stepper.tsx`) had an explicit comment flagging it as the natural candidate for promotion once a second wizard-shaped flow showed up; the user asked for that promotion directly rather than waiting for a second call site. New `packages/ui/src/components/stepper.tsx` exports `Stepper`/`StepperStepDef`/`StepperProps`, kept as a plain function component (matching `badge.tsx`'s convention, no `forwardRef` — no call site needs a ref) rather than the `forwardRef` pattern `avatar.tsx` uses for its Radix-wrapped parts. Behavior/visuals are unchanged from the original (numbered circles, filled-primary + checkmark on complete, current step outlined and bold, connecting line, label hidden below `sm`) — this was a deliberate, already-reviewed design per the task brief, not a redesign. The one real change: the checkmark now uses lucide-react's `Check` icon (matching how `pagination.tsx`, `accordion.tsx`, `drawer.tsx`, etc. already import icons from `lucide-react` inside `packages/ui`) instead of the plain `"✓"` glyph the old component used specifically because `apps/web` didn't declare `lucide-react` as its own dependency — that constraint doesn't apply inside `packages/ui`, which already depends on it directly. No `orientation` prop was added — checked `separator.tsx`'s pattern for it, but the only call site (the onboarding wizard) never needs vertical, so a horizontal-only component avoided dead API surface per the task's explicit "don't over-engineer" guidance.
- Exported from `packages/ui/src/index.ts` in the existing alphabetical `export *` barrel, right after `status-chip` and before `switch`.
- `apps/web/src/app/onboarding/page.tsx` now imports `Stepper`/`StepperStepDef` from `@spine/ui` instead of the local component; the old `apps/web/src/app/onboarding/wizard-stepper.tsx` is deleted (confirmed via grep that no other file referenced it).
- New doc page `apps/design-system/src/app/components/stepper/page.tsx`, built on the standard 5-section template (`progress/page.tsx` and `pagination/page.tsx` used as structural references for voice/depth/the `ComponentPreview`/`CodeBlock` pattern) with an interactive Back/Next demo. Added to `apps/design-system/src/lib/nav.ts` under "Feedback & status" next to `Progress` (its closest conceptual neighbor in that section), not alphabetically — that section isn't in strict alpha order today (`StatusChip, Badge, Alert, Toast, Progress, Spinner, EmptyState, Skeleton`), so placement followed the existing curated grouping rather than imposing a new ordering rule. Accessibility section states plainly that the stepper is a status indicator, not a set of interactive controls — verified against the onboarding wizard's actual behavior (`apps/web/src/app/onboarding/page.tsx` has no click handler wiring an earlier step back into view; the only ways back are each step's own in-page "Back"/"Skip" actions) before writing the a11y notes, rather than inventing click-to-navigate semantics the component doesn't have. Documents `aria-label="Progress"` on the list and `aria-current="step"` on the active step.
- **Spine logo added to `/login`, `/signup`, and the onboarding wizard shell.** Three source files exist at `docs/spine-logo-horizontal.png`, `docs/spine-logo-horizontal-light.png` (both 1024×301), and `docs/spine-logo-vertical.png` (1024×1024, unused here — no vertical lockup call site). Read both horizontal PNGs directly (not just their filenames) to determine which is which: `spine-logo-horizontal.png` renders a dark navy/teal wordmark, correct for a light background; `spine-logo-horizontal-light.png` renders a near-white/transparent wordmark — "light" in the filename means "light-colored," i.e. the dark-background variant, not "for light mode." Guessing from the filename alone would have put an invisible (white-on-white) logo on light mode.
- Copied both PNGs into the new `apps/web/public/` directory (didn't exist before). `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` has no `next-themes`/`data-theme` wiring at all today (confirmed — no theme provider, no toggle; only `apps/design-system` has that per `CLAUDE.md`'s own "Next up" note above), so rather than half-wiring a theme system just for the logo, used the CSS-only `prefers-color-scheme` swap: two stacked `next/image` `<Image>` elements per page, one `className="dark:hidden"` (the dark-navy variant) and one `className="hidden dark:block"` (the light-colored variant). Confirmed `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css` already drives its dark tokens off `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` (and a `data-theme` override for when a real toggle exists later), so the `dark:` Tailwind variant already resolves correctly against system preference with zero new config.
- Sized 140×41px (the image's true 1024:301 aspect ratio, ~3.4:1, scaled — not distorted) per each `<Image>`'s explicit `width`/`height`, landing in the 120–160px "typical page-header wordmark" range. Added to `apps/web/src/app/login/page.tsx` and `apps/web/src/app/signup/page.tsx` above their `Card`, and once to the onboarding wizard's shared shell (`apps/web/src/app/onboarding/page.tsx`'s top-level `<main>`, above the `Stepper`) rather than per-step, since every step already renders through that one shell. No new dependency — `next/image` is already part of Next.js.
- Verified against the live dev stack (Postgres/MinIO healthy via `docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml`, `apps/web` already running on :3000, `apps/design-system` on :3100, `apps/api` on :4000 — none needed restarting): `pnpm --filter @spine/ui typecheck` and `lint` clean (no `build` script exists for `@spine/ui` — it's source-consumed, confirmed via `package.json`); `pnpm --filter @spine/design-system typecheck`, `lint`, and `build` all clean, `/components/stepper` route present in the build output; `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, and `build` all clean, `/login`/`/signup`/`/onboarding` routes present in the build output. Runtime: curled `/components/stepper` on :3100 — 200, confirmed real rendered lead text ("numbered progress indicator") and `aria-current` markup present, not just a shell. Curled `/login` and `/signup` on :3000 — both 200, both responses contain two `<img>` tags (`data-nimg`, Next's `<Image>` output) with `dark:hidden`/`hidden dark:block` classes and `src` pointing at `/_next/image?url=%2Fspine-logo-horizontal...png`, confirming the copied public assets actually render. For `/onboarding`, signed up a fresh test user via `curl POST http://localhost:4000/api/auth/sign-up/email`, captured the `Set-Cookie: better-auth.session_token=...` header, and curled `/onboarding` on :3000 with that cookie — 200 (past the session middleware), same two logo `<img>` tags present in the response.
- Deviations: none from the brief's structure. One judgment call — the "Feedback & status" nav placement (next to Progress) over strict alphabetical order, reasoned above.
- Gaps/deferred: nothing new deferred by this change; the pre-existing "dark/light mode persistence not wired into `apps/web`" gap (see Next up) still stands — this logo swap works without it because it rides on `prefers-color-scheme` directly, but a future real theme toggle in `apps/web` should switch these `Image` pairs to a `data-theme`-driven approach (or reuse whatever `apps/design-system`'s `next-themes` wiring does) instead of only following system preference.
- Stories: extends S-00.1/S-00.6 (component library + docs) and S-12.x (onboarding wizard polish) — not new PRD-numbered stories of their own.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — First-run onboarding wizard shell (API + web), and two real signup bugs fixed along the way

- **Bug fix #1 — chart of accounts was never seeded for a real signup.** `seedChartOfAccounts` was only ever called from the standalone seed script; the real `databaseHooks.user.create.after` hook in `apps/api/src/auth/auth.ts` created the org but never seeded its accounts, so every real user who signed up got zero accounts — silently breaking the ledger and this wizard's CoA-review step. Wired the seed call into the hook itself, right after `auth.api.createOrganization`. The seed script (`apps/api/src/db/seed/index.ts`) no longer calls `seedChartOfAccounts` explicitly since the hook now covers it — confirmed by re-running `db:seed:dev` and checking the seeded org still has a full CoA.
- **Bug fix #2 — `session.activeOrganizationId` stayed `null` after every signup.** `auth.api.createOrganization` creates the org/membership rows but never sets the *session's* active org — Better Auth's organization plugin marks that field `input: false` on session schema, so a `databaseHooks.session.create.before` mutation of the payload is silently dropped by Better Auth's own field filtering (confirmed by tracing `crud-org.mjs`/`organization.mjs` in `better-auth`'s compiled output and reproducing against a live signup with curl). Without this, `@OrgRoles()` (from `@thallesp/nestjs-better-auth`, which requires an active org on the session) would 403 every onboarding-wizard call for a brand-new owner. Fixed with a `databaseHooks.session.create.after` hook that looks up the user's org membership and directly `UPDATE`s the just-created `session` row's `active_organization_id` via Drizzle, bypassing the field filter entirely. Verified end-to-end: a fresh `sign-up/email` curl call now returns `get-session` with `activeOrganizationId` populated on the very first session.
- API: real NestJS controllers/services in `apps/api/src/modules/org/` (`SettingsController`/`SettingsService` over the existing generic `settings` key/value table — documented key shapes for `business_profile`, `industry_template`, `currency`, `financial_year_start`, `default_tax_rate`, `onboarding_status`, `dashboard_checklist_dismissed` inline in `settings.service.ts`; `AccountsController`/`AccountsService` for CoA list-grouped-by-type + rename-non-system-accounts; `InvitationsController` wrapping Better Auth's own `auth.api.createInvitation`), plus `apps/api/src/modules/numbering/` (`NumberingController`/`NumberingService` upserting `number_sequences` rows for the six canonical document types from §14.3 — quote/invoice/credit_note/receipt/expense/job; vendor bill excluded since its number is user-entered) and `apps/api/src/modules/tax/` (`TaxController`, a thin wrapper over `SettingsService` for the single default tax rate). Every mutating endpoint requires `@OrgRoles(["owner"])` — server-side, reading the session's actual org member role, never a client claim — and only owners can complete onboarding steps affecting org config.
- **First idempotency-key implementation in the codebase** — `apps/api/src/common/idempotency.interceptor.ts` plus a new `idempotency_keys` table (`apps/api/src/db/schema/idempotency.ts`), keyed on `(orgId, endpoint, key)`. A required `Idempotency-Key` header; a replayed request with the same key returns the stored response instead of re-executing; a concurrent duplicate gets a 409 rather than racing. Nothing else in the API had established this pattern yet (grepped for "idempotency" across `apps/api/src` — no hits before this), so this is a first-of-its-kind implementation worth reviewing rather than a copy of an existing convention. Applied to every mutating onboarding endpoint via `@UseInterceptors(IdempotencyInterceptor)`.
- First real Drizzle-client NestJS provider (`apps/api/src/db/db.module.ts`, `@Global()`, injected via a `DATABASE` symbol) — nothing previously injected `Database` into a controller/service; every prior touch went through the standalone seed script or a test file calling `createDb` directly.
- Web: the wizard itself at `apps/web/src/app/onboarding/` — linear, 8 steps (business profile → industry template → currency/locale → financial year → tax rate → numbering → chart-of-accounts review → invite team), preceded by a welcome screen offering manual setup / demo defaults / skip. Every step is its own component taking `initialValue`/`onSave` (`apps/web/src/app/onboarding/steps/*.tsx`) so Settings can reuse them unmodified later — not hard-coded to the wizard flow. "Skip to dashboard" is present on every step and persists `onboarding_status: {skipped: true}`; finishing persists `{skipped: false, completedAt}`. A composed `WizardStepper` (`apps/web/src/app/onboarding/wizard-stepper.tsx`) shows progress — no dedicated stepper primitive exists in `@spine/ui` (checked `packages/ui/src/components`), so this was composed from `cn`/tokens per the task's explicit instruction not to invent a new exported component for one call site.
- `apps/web/src/lib/api-client.ts` — first general-purpose browser-side fetch wrapper in `apps/web` (previously only `authClient`/`get-session.ts` existed), sends credentials + a fresh `Idempotency-Key` per mutating call.
- `apps/web/src/app/signup/page.tsx` now routes to `/onboarding` instead of `/dashboard` after a successful signup; `apps/web/src/middleware.ts` extended to gate `/onboarding/*` behind a session the same way `/dashboard/*` already was. Nothing redirects a returning user *back* into `/onboarding` — the dashboard page and middleware have no such check, confirmed by grep — so it's a one-time landing spot, not a gate.
- Dashboard setup checklist (S-12.9): `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/setup-checklist.tsx`, a dismissible `Card` with the three PRD-example items (first customer/item/quote). Dismissal persists as the `dashboard_checklist_dismissed` org setting. The three items are static, always-unchecked placeholders — contacts/catalog/quotes have no schema or UI anywhere in the codebase yet (checked `apps/api/src/modules` — empty stubs), so there's nothing real to check completion against; wiring real completion detection is explicit follow-up work once those modules exist, not faked here.
- Judgment calls made and reasoning: (1) **CoA hide/delete** — `accounts` has no `isHidden` column; added none. System accounts render read-only with a "System" badge, non-system accounts get inline rename only, matching the task's suggested fallback. (2) **Demo data** — `apps/api/src/db/seed` only ever seeded the chart of accounts (no contacts/catalog/quotes tables exist to seed sample rows into), so "load demo data" in the welcome step means skip the manual wizard and rely on the already-seeded defaults, not a fabricated sample-data corpus; documented inline in `welcome-step.tsx`. (3) **Logo upload** — no S3/MinIO upload endpoint exists anywhere reachable (`apps/api/src/modules/files` is an empty stub), so the business-profile step takes a logo URL text field instead of building upload plumbing from scratch. (4) **Idempotency pattern** — established fresh (see above), flagged for review rather than presented as an existing convention. (5) **Stepper** — composed inline rather than added to `@spine/ui`, per explicit instruction to flag rather than silently add a new library component.
- Verified end-to-end against the live dev stack (`docker compose -f docker-compose.dev.yml` — db/minio healthy throughout, never restarted): signed up multiple fresh users via curl and confirmed (a) the CoA-seeding fix — 29 accounts land per org with no separate seed-script call, confirmed via direct `psql`; (b) the active-org fix — `get-session` returns a populated `activeOrganizationId` on the very first session after signup; (c) every wizard-step endpoint — `PUT /org/settings/:key` (business profile, industry template, currency, financial year, onboarding status), `PUT /org/tax/default-rate`, `GET`/`PUT /org/numbering/:documentType` (confirmed defaults for all 6 document types when unconfigured, and a real upsert), `GET /org/accounts` (grouped by type) + `PUT /org/accounts/:id` (non-system rename succeeds; system-account rename correctly rejected with 400), `POST /org/invitations` (real `invitation` row created, confirmed via `psql`); (d) idempotency — replaying the same `Idempotency-Key` on a `PUT` returned the original response and did not apply a second, different payload, confirmed both via the HTTP response and a direct `psql` row count; (e) authz — unauthenticated calls get 401, `@OrgRoles(["owner"])` is present (compiled-output grep) on every mutating route; (f) web routing — `/onboarding` correctly 307-redirects to `/login` unauthenticated, renders real welcome-step HTML when authenticated, and `signup/page.tsx` routes to `/onboarding` (code-confirmed) rather than `/dashboard`; `/dashboard` renders with the `setup-checklist` client chunk present in its bundle. `pnpm --filter @spine/api typecheck lint build` and `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck lint build` all pass clean (run sequentially — running `build`+`typecheck` concurrently via `turbo` raced on the shared `.next` output and is a tooling artifact, not a real failure, confirmed by re-running each independently). Added `apps/api/src/auth/signup.integration.test.ts` covering both signup bug fixes directly against Postgres — passes alongside the pre-existing `ledger-integrity.integration.test.ts` (`pnpm --filter @spine/api test:integration`, 2 files / 5 tests, all green). No browser-automation tooling was available in this environment (per its constraints) — wizard step transitions/form interactivity were code-inspected and API-verified via curl, not interactively browser-driven; the rendered HTML for `/onboarding` and `/dashboard` was fetched and checked for real content, not just a 200 status.
- Stories: S-12.1 through S-12.7, S-12.9, S-12.10, S-12.11 (wizard shell, industry template picker, currency/FY/tax/numbering/CoA steps, dashboard checklist, reusable-from-Settings step components, always-skippable). S-12.8 (demo data) partially — offered as "use defaults" rather than a real sample-data corpus, per the documented judgment call above. S-12.3's downstream milestone/retention visibility effect is explicitly deferred to Phase 3, not built here.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — Web-side Better Auth wiring: sign-up/login UI, session middleware, and a base-URL fix
- `apps/web/src/lib/auth-client.ts`: a browser-side Better Auth client (`createAuthClient` from `better-auth/react`, plus the `organizationClient` plugin) pointed at `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` — the API's own origin, not apps/web's — since Better Auth's handler lives on the NestJS process (port 4000 in dev), not the Next.js one.
- `apps/web/src/lib/get-session.ts`: server-side session read for Server Components. There's no local Better Auth instance to call `auth.api.getSession()` against in apps/web — the instance lives in apps/api, a different app — so this forwards the incoming request's `Cookie` header (via `next/headers`) to the API's `GET /api/auth/get-session` and returns the session JSON, degrading to "logged out" rather than throwing if the API is unreachable.
- `apps/web/src/middleware.ts`: route protection via the same cookie-forwarding pattern — redirects `/dashboard/*` to `/login` when unauthenticated, and `/login`/`/signup` to `/dashboard` when a valid session cookie is already present.
- `apps/web/src/app/signup/page.tsx` and `.../login/page.tsx`: name/email/password (signup) and email/password (login) forms built entirely from `@spine/ui` (`FormField`, `Input`, `Button`, `Card`, `Alert`, `Spinner`) — no raw form elements. Both redirect to `/dashboard` on success; signup relies on the API's existing `databaseHooks.user.create.after` hook to auto-create the new org.
- `apps/web/src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`: a real placeholder (not a dead end) showing the session user's name/email in a `Card` plus a `SignOutButton` client component — deliberately minimal, no onboarding wizard, per explicit scope.
- **Found and fixed a real base-URL misconfiguration** in `apps/api/src/auth/auth.ts`: Better Auth's `baseURL` was set to `process.env.APP_URL` (`http://localhost:3000`, the web app's own origin) instead of where the API's own handler is actually reachable (`http://localhost:4000`, mounted at `/api/auth` by `@thallesp/nestjs-better-auth`). Introduced a new `API_URL` env var (server-reachable API base, used for `baseURL` and by `get-session.ts`/`middleware.ts`) and `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` (browser-reachable API base, used by the client). Also added `trustedOrigins: [APP_URL]` to the Better Auth config, which had been implicitly relying on Nest's CORS alone. Added both vars to `.env`, `.env.development.example` (both `http://localhost:4000`, since api/web run on separate ports natively in dev), and `.env.example` (both equal to `APP_URL`, since Caddy fronts api/web under one origin in the production stack).
- `apps/web/package.json`: added `better-auth@^1.6.0` (matching `apps/api`'s exact version) and `dotenv-cli`; `dev`/`build`/`start` now load the repo-root `.env` the same way `apps/api`'s scripts already do, so `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` is available at Next.js build/dev time (it previously wasn't loading root `.env` at all).
- Verified end-to-end against the live dev stack (Postgres/MinIO via `dev:infra:up`, migrations applied, both apps run natively): signed up a real user via `curl POST /api/auth/sign-up/email`, confirmed a `Set-Cookie: better-auth.session_token=...` response with correct CORS headers (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: http://localhost:3000`, credentials true); queried Postgres directly and confirmed an `organization` row and a `member` row with `role='owner'` were created for the new user; logged in as the same user via `curl POST /api/auth/sign-in/email` (separate session token issued) and confirmed `GET /api/auth/get-session` returns the session/user with that cookie; confirmed `GET /dashboard` redirects to `/login` (307) with no cookie and `GET /login` redirects to `/dashboard` (307) with a valid cookie, and that the authenticated dashboard response actually renders the signed-up user's name/email and a Sign out control. Also fetched the rendered `/signup` HTML directly and confirmed the name/email/password fields are present with correct types/autocomplete. `pnpm --filter @spine/web typecheck`, `lint`, and a full production `build` (including the new `/dashboard`, `/login`, `/signup` routes and the compiled middleware) all pass clean. No browser-automation tooling was available in this environment, so the sign-up/login forms' actual click-through/submit behavior in a real browser was code-inspected and HTML-verified, not interactively browser-driven — the underlying API calls those forms make were separately curl-verified end-to-end.
- Stories: Phase 0 exit criterion "a user can install via Docker, sign up..." (§12) — the sign-up/login half of that flow now has a real UI, not just the API; onboarding wizard (the rest of that exit criterion) remains open.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — Hand-rolled Outlook-style Calendar component
- `@spine/ui`'s `Calendar` (`packages/ui/src/components/calendar.tsx`): Day/Week/Month/Agenda views, click-drag to create an event, drag-move, drag-resize (Day/Week edges), all-day and multi-day events, side-by-side overlap layout, simple daily/weekly/monthly recurrence (end by date or occurrence count, whole-series edits only, no per-occurrence exceptions), and a fixed 8-color category palette — built from native pointer events (`onPointerDown`/`onPointerMove`/`onPointerUp` + pointer capture), not the HTML5 DnD API and not a wrapped calendar library, per explicit direction. `date-fns` added as `@spine/ui`'s first date-math dependency for week/month boundaries and recurrence-rule arithmetic (DST/month-length correctness delegated to it, never hand-rolled).
- Two pure-logic algorithms in the new `packages/ui/src/lib/calendar-utils.ts`: `expandRecurringEvent`/`expandEventsInRange` (walks a recurrence rule forward from its start date via `date-fns` interval addition, stopping at the count/date end condition or once occurrences pass the visible range, capped at 2000 occurrences against pathological rules) and `layoutOverlappingEvents` (single-pass greedy column assignment — sorted by start then by longer-duration-first, each event takes the lowest free column, and every event in a connected overlap cluster reports the cluster's peak concurrency as its `columnCount` so proportional widths stay consistent across the whole cluster, not just pairwise).
- The pre-existing month-grid `Calendar` (used internally by `DatePicker`) was renamed to `MiniCalendar` and extracted to its own file, `packages/ui/src/components/calendar-mini.tsx`, to free up the `Calendar` export name for the new scheduling component and avoid a self-import; `DatePicker` and the new `Calendar`'s toolbar mini-month-picker both consume it unchanged, per the reuse-don't-rebuild instruction. `MiniCalendarProps` renamed to match.
- New design tokens: an 8-color `category-*`/`category-*-bg` palette (blue/green/amber/red/purple/teal/pink/slate) added to `docs/DESIGN.md` and `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css` for both light and dark mode, deliberately a separate layer from the reserved paid/due-soon/overdue/draft financial-status palette even though some hues are shared — an event's color is a free user label, not a system-derived fact, so Calendar is the only component allowed to read these tokens for categorization.
- Full doc page at `apps/design-system/src/app/components/calendar/page.tsx` on the standard 5-section template (Features/Anatomy/API Reference/Examples/Accessibility), added to `nav.ts` under Data display, with job-site-flavored sample event data (site visits, quote reviews, invoice-due markers, a recurring daily standup and monthly invoicing run) per `docs/spine-prd.md`'s domain.
- Fixed three real bugs found during Playwright-driven interactive verification (not just typecheck/lint): (1) Month view's week rows were all rendering at the same vertical offset instead of stacking — a `grid-rows-[repeat(auto-fill,minmax(0,1fr))]` container without matching `grid-template-columns` doesn't reliably auto-place multi-item rows, replaced with a plain flex column of per-week grid rows; (2) drag-to-create never fired because the empty-slot `onPointerDown` guard (`e.target === e.currentTarget`) was always false — the hour-divider children absorbed every pointer event — fixed by making those dividers `pointer-events-none`; (3) a React 19 "setState while rendering a different component" warning from committing `onSlotCreate`/`onEventUpdate` (which set state on the parent `Calendar`) from inside a `setDrag` state-updater callback in `TimeGrid` — fixed by reading the finished drag from a ref instead of the updater's `prev` argument.
- Verified: `pnpm turbo run typecheck lint --filter=@spine/ui --filter=@spine/design-system` clean; `/components/calendar` returns 200; Playwright screenshots confirmed all four views render without crashing, a 3-way time overlap (Site visit/Team standup/Quote review) lays out side-by-side in Day view, Month view's Aug 6 cell shows 3 events plus a "+1 more" overflow with no row collision, drag-create opens the event editor pre-filled with the dragged range, and drag-move actually changed a sample event's time from 11:00 AM–11:30 AM to 1:15 PM–1:45 PM after drop, with zero console errors on the final pass.
- Stories: extends S-00.1/S-00.6 (component library + docs) — not itself a PRD-numbered story since Calendar isn't yet wired to any domain module (jobs/milestones), but built to the same bar as the rest of `@spine/ui` for whenever a scheduling surface (e.g. a jobs calendar) is needed.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-06 — Full Radix Primitives + shadcn/ui component union, and full apps/design-system doc coverage
- Cross-referenced `@spine/ui` against the complete Radix Primitives (30 components) and shadcn/ui component lists and closed every gap: `Accordion`, `AlertDialog`, `AspectRatio`, `Collapsible`, `ContextMenu`, `Form` (Radix's native-validation form, exported as `RadixFormField`/etc. to avoid colliding with the existing simpler `FormField`), `HoverCard`, `Menubar`, `NavigationMenu`, `OTPField`, `PasswordToggleField`, `Progress`, `ScrollArea`, `Separator`, `Slider`, `Toggle`/`ToggleGroup`, `Toolbar` (Radix); `Alert`, `ButtonGroup`, `Carousel` (embla-carousel-react), `Chart` (a themed Recharts wrapper — categorical series default to the primary token plus muted greys, financial-status charts pass explicit paid/due-soon/overdue colors instead, never mixed in one chart), `Command` (standalone, `Combobox` and a `CommandDialog` both compose it), `InputGroup`, `Kbd`, `NativeSelect`, `Resizable` (react-resizable-panels v4's current `Group`/`Panel`/`Separator` API, not the older `PanelGroup`/`PanelResizeHandle` names most docs assume), `Sidebar` (the collapsible admin-shell primitive from `docs/DESIGN.md`'s own Layouts spec), `Spinner`, `Typography` presets, and a richer `Field`/`FieldGroup`/`FieldSet` layout layer plus `DataTable` (search + column-visibility on top of `Table`) (shadcn). Skipped nothing — built the full union per explicit direction, including components with no current Spine screen (Carousel, Chart, Menubar, OTP/PasswordToggle fields) for catalogue completeness.
- Built the five layout primitives `docs/DESIGN.md`'s own Layouts section calls for as real components, not just documentation convention: `Box`, `Flex`, `Grid`, `Container`, `Section` — spacing/gap props map to literal, fully-static Tailwind classes (not template-string-interpolated ones), since Tailwind's content scanner can't see dynamically assembled class names.
- Found and fixed a real bug while wiring the `Command` doc page: it 500'd only when rendered from a plain Server Component page (not from a page already marked `"use client"`) — an RSC/client-boundary resolution failure, not a `cmdk` or logic bug, confirmed by bisecting down to a minimal repro. Root cause was `command.tsx` missing a `"use client"` directive that every other interactive `@spine/ui` file needed but most hadn't been given either — the gap had been silently masked because every doc page exercising them so far happened to already be a client-component page itself. Audited and added the directive to all 31 affected files (every Radix-primitive-backed or state-holding component) rather than patching `command.tsx` alone, so this class of bug can't resurface on a future Server Component page.
- Rewrote every one of the ~30 pre-existing component doc pages and wrote ~30 new ones onto a real 5-section template — Features, Anatomy, API Reference, Examples, Accessibility (with real keyboard-interaction tables) — matching the Radix Primitives documentation site's own page structure, at full depth on every page per explicit direction (no lighter-weight treatment for simple components).
- Wrote real content for the three previously-stubbed nav sections: **Overview** (introduction + a dedicated Principles page enumerating the rules — one primary action per screen, the reserved financial-status palette, token-only theming, 44px touch targets, borders-not-shadows, never-a-blank-screen, motion-confirms-don't-decorate); **Foundation** (Typography, Spacing, Icons, Motion pages, each with a live rendered preview, alongside the pre-existing Color page); **Layouts** (the five structural primitives, documented with live examples); **Templates** (a composed Admin dashboard built from real `Sidebar`/`StatCard`/`Table`/`StatusChip`, plus the empty-dashboard `EmptyState` variant — not prose descriptions).
- `nav.ts` reorganized to include every new page across all four previously-thin sections, keeping the Components grouping (Primitives / Feedback & status / Data display / Overlays) in sync with `docs/DESIGN.md`'s own grouping.
- Verified: full workspace `build`/`lint`/`typecheck`/`test` passes (15 tasks, 6 packages); all 72 `apps/design-system` pages statically prerender with zero errors; booted the dev server and re-fetched every page that previously 500'd (Command, Accordion, ContextMenu, OTPField, PasswordToggleField, Slider) plus a spot-check across every new section, all 200 with no server-log errors.
- Stories: S-00.1 (component library) — the catalogue now exceeds §F0's MUST-list, matching both reference libraries in full; S-00.6 (documented with usage examples) now has real per-component depth (Anatomy/API/Examples/Accessibility) rather than the earlier single-example pages.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-05 — Complete the @spine/ui primitive set from PRD §F0
- All 30 remaining components from §F0's MUST-list: `Input`, `Textarea`, `Select`, `Combobox` (Popover + `cmdk`), `DatePicker` (a hand-rolled month-grid `Calendar` hosted in `Popover` — no external date library), `Checkbox`, `RadioGroup`, `Switch`, `Label`, `HelperText`, `FormField` (a render-prop wrapper wiring label/control/helper-text/`aria-describedby` together so no screen hand-rolls that plumbing), `MoneyInput`, `Popover`, `Tooltip`, `DropdownMenu`, `Tabs`, `StatusChip`, `Badge`, `Toast`, `EmptyState`, `Skeleton`, `Card`/`StatCard`/`DataList`, `Table` (sortable, client-paginated, sticky header), `Avatar`, `Breadcrumb`, `Pagination`, `Dialog`, `Drawer`. All built on Radix UI primitives + `class-variance-authority`, styled directly from `docs/DESIGN.md`'s tokens — no component invents its own color/spacing/radius values.

### 2026-08-05 — Design system: docs/DESIGN.md, apps/design-system, first @spine/ui primitive
- `docs/DESIGN.md`: Spine's design token system (YAML frontmatter: colors, typography, radius, spacing, motion, icons, focus rings, per-component token maps) plus prose sections (Overview, Colors, Typography, Layout, Elevation, Shapes, Icons, Motion, Components, Do's/Don'ts, Logo) — same structure as a reference design doc supplied for this session. Personality is calm/understated/trustworthy (financial software, not a consumer app); accent is a single blue (`#2954E5`) reserved for one primary action per screen; a four-color paid/due-soon/overdue/draft palette is reserved exclusively for financial document status and kept independent of the (self-hoster-overridable) primary color, mirroring how §7.2's posting rules treat money as fact, not decoration. Dark mode is real, independently-tuned infrastructure (not a mechanical inversion) since office use — the primary dark-mode case here — has no outdoor-glare constraint arguing against it.
- `packages/config/tailwind-preset.css` rewritten to match `docs/DESIGN.md` exactly, switching from the earlier placeholder tokens (from Phase 0 scaffolding) and from `light-dark()` to an explicit `data-theme` attribute + `prefers-color-scheme` fallback — needed so a runtime theme toggle (§S00.2/S-00.3) can actually control the mode via JS, which `light-dark()` alone can't do without also managing `color-scheme`.
- `apps/design-system`: a new Next.js App Router app (no Storybook, per explicit direction) implementing the three-region docs pattern from DESIGN.md's own Documentation layout spec — persistent top bar (five section tabs: Overview/Foundation/Layouts/Components/Templates, ⌘K search via `cmdk`, theme toggle via `next-themes`), a left sidebar scoped to the current section, a center content column, and a right-hand "on this page" anchor nav that scans rendered `h2`/`h3`s. Shell chrome mounts once at the root layout (not per-page) specifically to avoid the sidebar-remount/scroll-reset bug the reference design doc calls out from its own project's history. Component docs mix MDX prose with live, real `@spine/ui` component previews (`ComponentPreview`) and a copyable syntax-highlighted code sample (`CodeBlock`) per example, matching the Radix Primitives documentation site referenced for this pattern.
- `@spine/ui`'s first real primitive: `Button` (Radix `Slot` + `class-variance-authority`), with `primary`/`secondary`/`danger`/`ghost` variants and `sm`/`default`/`touch`/`icon` sizes — `touch` meets the 44px minimum required for the mobile field-companion app per DESIGN.md's Layout section. Disabled state uses the border color rather than a dimmed fill, matching the "unambiguous in bright light" principle carried over from the reference doc's own field-use reasoning.
- Fixed a real Tailwind v4 content-detection gap while verifying: utility classes referenced only inside `packages/ui` (e.g. `bg-primary`) weren't being generated in `apps/web`/`apps/design-system`'s compiled CSS, since Tailwind v4's automatic scanning is relative to each CSS file and doesn't reach across package boundaries by default — fixed with an explicit `@source "../../../../packages/ui/src"` in both apps' `globals.css`. Caught by fetching each app's actual compiled CSS bundle and grepping for the expected utility classes, not just checking that pages returned 200.
- Verified: `apps/design-system` boots via `pnpm dev`, all three pages (`/overview`, `/foundation/color`, `/components/button`) return 200 with real rendered content (confirmed via the actual HTML response — Button's four variants and color-token swatches all present), and the compiled CSS contains the utility classes the components depend on. Full workspace `build`/`lint`/`typecheck`/`test` passes (15 tasks, 7 packages). No headless-browser tooling was available in this environment to capture an actual screenshot — visual/pixel-level review is still outstanding and worth a manual pass before this is considered final.
- Stories: S-00.1 (component library groundwork), S-00.4/S-00.5 (token-based theming — palette described but preset swaps not yet built), S-00.2/S-00.3 (dark mode — infrastructure in place, persistence via `next-themes` verified only in `apps/design-system` so far, not yet wired into `apps/web`)
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-05 — Auth: Better Auth with per-org roles, cookie sessions, and JWT for mobile
- `apps/api` upgraded to NestJS v11 + Express v5 (from v10) to use the maintained `@thallesp/nestjs-better-auth` integration package — mounts Better Auth's own request handler, registers a global `AuthGuard` (every route requires a session by default; opt out per-route with `@AllowAnonymous()`), and exposes `@Session()`/`@OrgRoles()` decorators.
- Better Auth now owns identity/org/session schema outright (`user`, `session`, `account`, `verification`, `organization`, `member`, `invitation`, `jwks`), generated via its own CLI (`@better-auth/cli generate`) rather than hand-written — replaces the hand-rolled `organisations`/`users`/`memberships`/`sessions` tables from the previous session. All other tables' org/user foreign keys (`accounts`, `journal_entries`, `fiscal_periods`, `number_sequences`, `audit_log`, `settings`) now reference Better Auth's `organization.id`/`user.id` (`text`, Postgres-generated UUIDs — not the app's own `uuid` columns).
- Argon2id password hashing (`@node-rs/argon2`) plugged into Better Auth's `emailAndPassword.password.hash/verify`, replacing its scrypt default, per §10.3.
- Organization plugin configured with three custom roles matching §2.1 exactly — `owner`, `staff`, `field` (not Better Auth's default owner/admin/member) — via `createAccessControl`. Every sign-up automatically becomes the owner of a brand-new organisation (`databaseHooks.user.create.after` calls `auth.api.createOrganization` server-side); there's no self-serve "create another org" or "join an existing org" path in v1, matching the PRD's single-org-per-signup onboarding model.
- JWT plugin issues short-lived (15 min), JWKS-verifiable tokens via `/api/auth/token` for the mobile client, coexisting with cookie sessions for web — exactly the split specified in §5.
- Seed script now signs up a real demo owner (`auth.api.signUpEmail`) rather than inserting an org row directly, so the seeded org is identical in shape to any real signup and exercises the same hook path.
- Verified end-to-end against a live dev DB: sign-up creates the user + org + owner membership; the session cookie authenticates `/api/auth/get-session` and other protected Better Auth endpoints; unauthenticated requests to protected endpoints get a 401; `/api/auth/token` issues a working JWT.
- Stories: Phase 0 exit criterion "a user can install via Docker, sign up..." (§12) — auth half of that is done; onboarding wizard is still open.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-05 — Dev infra compose, README, and `.env` loading for DB scripts
- `docker-compose.dev.yml`: a separate, infra-only Compose file (Postgres + MinIO, `spine-dev` project name, distinct named volumes) for contributors running `apps/api`/`apps/web` natively via `pnpm dev` — kept apart from `docker-compose.yml`, which is the production stack that builds and runs `api`/`web` as containers behind Caddy. New root scripts: `dev:infra:up`, `dev:infra:down`, `dev:infra:reset`, `dev:infra:logs`, `db:migrate:dev`, `db:seed:dev`.
- Reverted the loopback port mapping added to `docker-compose.yml`'s `db` service in the previous session — that belongs on the dev-only file, not the production one.
- `apps/api`'s `dev`/`start`/`db:*`/`test:integration` scripts now load `../../.env` via `dotenv-cli`, so `pnpm db:migrate:dev`/`db:seed:dev`/`pnpm dev` pick up the repo-root `.env` without each script needing `DATABASE_URL` exported manually.
- `.env.development.example` — a dev-specific env template (`localhost` hostnames for Postgres/MinIO) distinct from `.env.example` (Docker network hostnames, for the production stack).
- `README.md` — project overview, tech stack, repo layout, and a from-scratch local dev setup (clone → install → `dev:infra:up` → copy env → migrate → seed → `pnpm dev`), plus the separate production/self-hosting path and the non-negotiable architecture rules.
- Verified the full README setup sequence end-to-end against freshly created containers: `dev:infra:reset` → `db:migrate:dev` → `db:seed:dev` → `pnpm dev` (API `/health` and web both responding).
- Deleted the old dev Postgres container/volume from the previous session and rebuilt from scratch under the new dev compose file.
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

### 2026-08-05 — Postgres + Drizzle: identity/org schema and DB-enforced ledger core
- Drizzle schema for identity & org (`organisations`, `users`, `memberships` with a 4-role enum, `sessions`, `settings`), `number_sequences` (§6.5 gapless numbering), and `audit_log` (§10.4).
- Ledger core (`accounts`, `journal_entries`, `journal_lines`, `fiscal_periods`) per §7 — `journal_lines` carries optional `contact_id`/`job_id` so AR aging and job profitability can be derived from the ledger rather than a parallel calculation, per §7.1.
- The debits-equal-credits invariant (§6.3) is enforced by Postgres itself, not application code: a `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint trigger validates each entry's balance at COMMIT (a plain `CHECK` doesn't work here — it fires after each single-row line insert, rejecting an entry before its offsetting line lands). Separate triggers make `journal_entries`/`journal_lines` immutable after insert (only `reversed_by_id` may be set once, per the reversing-entry correction model) and reject any UPDATE/DELETE otherwise.
- Chart-of-accounts seed (`apps/api/src/db/seed/`, invoked via `scripts/seed.ts`) matching §14.2 exactly, including which accounts are system-protected (AR, AP, tax, retention, advances, retained earnings).
- Verified directly against Postgres via `docker compose`: balanced entries commit, unbalanced entries are rejected at commit with a clear error, and edits/deletes on committed entries/lines are rejected. Same invariants covered by an automated integration suite (`apps/api/src/db/ledger-integrity.integration.test.ts`, run via `pnpm test:integration`, kept separate from the unit `test` script so CI without a DB still passes).
- `docker-compose.yml`'s `db` service now publishes `127.0.0.1:5432` (loopback-only) so migrations/seed/`db:studio` are reachable from the host without exposing Postgres to the network.
- Stories: S-09.1 (seeded chart of accounts); ledger/numbering/audit schema underpins most of Phase 1+ but isn't itself a numbered story
- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation

- `.env.example` with the 3 required vars (`DATABASE_URL`, `AUTH_SECRET`, `APP_URL`) and optional SMTP/S3/locale blocks that degrade gracefully per §6.8.
- Verified: `pnpm install`, and `turbo run build lint typecheck test` all pass clean across every package; the built API boots and all modules initialize.

- Phase: Phase 0 · Foundation
